|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 0.5 | 3 | 3.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 3 | 0.27 |
| reviewer | 0.42 | 2.8 | 3.2 | 3 | 2.4 | 2.7 | 0.24 |
| pi | 0.5 | 3 | 3.5 | 3 | 2.5 | 3 | 0.27 |

- **ac** — risk: The likely headline is a null: unforced order-2 effects are not detectable at 24x4x4 and the published gains fall inside the noise. That null rests on an MDE/TOST argument plus a MetaRSI re-implementation on a different backbone (Qwen3-Coder-30B instead of Qwen3.5-35B). Reviewers can wave it off as "your replication isn't faithful, and the effect is underpowered by design". On top of that, the IbI group (35-45% chance) could publish a cleaner order-2 measurement first with their own harness, which would leave this paper competing on the p2 decomposition and the routing ablation alone. · raise P: Make the level-2 evidence-routing ablation the headline instead of the power-limited unforced null. Run it with enough replicates that it gives a sharp, positive, decomposed result (how much of the forced-edit gain g2 is evidence routing and how much is meta-competence), with a tight CI. Also re-run MetaRSI's own released code, or a verified-fidelity port of it, with replicates on its original task. That way the audit of +2.3 / +5.5 cannot be dismissed as a replication-fidelity problem.
- **reviewer** — risk: The most likely headline is that unforced Δ2 is not detectable at 24x4x4 and the published gains sit inside a hurdle null. That is a measurement-only paper on a single backbone, resting on an equivalence margin and a re-implemented MetaRSI whose fidelity reviewers can dispute ("you didn't replicate their system"). Because MetaRSI forces its edits (p2=1), the edit-rate story does not apply to its headline number. That leaves replicate variance plus the routing ablation, which skeptical agents-pool reviewers may read as a limited re-analysis. Separately, an order-2 follow-up from the IbI group could take the controlled-measurement slot before May. · raise P: Make the level-2 evidence-routing ablation the centerpiece and run it on a second open backbone, or on MetaRSI's released code if it is available, so the paper has a positive, mechanistic finding that holds across systems ("order-2 gain = evidence routing, X% recoverable by handing the evidence to order-1"). The null-detection result would then be secondary. This turns a fragile power-limited null into a striking, replicated claim and removes the fidelity objection about a single re-implemented system.
- **pi** — risk: The most likely result is that unforced Δ2 cannot be detected at 24x4x4, which leaves an argued null under TOST plus a changed estimand (forced g2). Meanwhile the MetaRSI re-implementation on a different backbone (Qwen3-Coder-30B instead of Qwen3.5-35B) lets reviewers dismiss any failure to replicate as a fidelity gap rather than evidence against MetaRSI. On top of that, the IbI group (35-45% chance) may publish a more rigorous order-2 measurement first, built on their own harness. · raise P: Make the level-2 evidence-routing ablation the headline positive finding, not the hard-to-detect natural-rate null. It is powered on the forced cell and shows that most of the forced g2 comes from routing meta-evidence, since giving the same evidence straight to the order-1 improver recovers it. Anchor it with a faithful MetaRSI reproduction: use their released code or backbone where possible, and first reproduce their single-run +7.2 vs +4.9 before replicating it. A positive, mechanism-level result plus fidelity to the original system turns a fragile null audit into a claim reviewers can defend.

### Order-0 Rewards Breed Routers, Not Improvers: Which Meta-Reward Raises Successor Quality, and Through Edit Rate or Edit Quality? (standalone, stationary banks; merge of MR-A bake-off + MR-E headline + MR-C hurdle + GAP-1 correlation) (`order0-rewards-breed-routers`) — EV 10.2%

**Claim.** Two-sided and pre-registered. In loops that select improver edits on order-0 signals (child score; matched-budget Δ0 as in RSI-Harness; descendant/CMP quality as in Looped-RSI/HGM), selection lowers the improver's meta-edit rate p and leaves held-out successor quality Δ1 unchanged, because it favours evidence-routing edits. Two kinds of reward instead raise Δ1, mainly through p: replay-family rewards (hurdle Q1-hat = p̂·μ̂, IFR-style, HMED-style) and order-0 reward plus a forced-edit quota. The estimands are ΔΔ1, Δp and Δμ between arms, and either sign is publishable. "Order-0 selection already finds order-1 edits" would directly support Looped-RSI and RSI-Harness. There is no pre-committed p≥0.2 crossover; the cost crossover is a derived hypothesis only. Motivation comes from IbI (ZMmJDeDTXk). Its order-1 null rests on 6 meta-states with no equivalence margin (Δ1 on M0 = +0.031, CI upper bound +0.126). There is a hidden per-edit gain (M1-edited children 0.86 vs 0.57, n=4 edits). Every IbI loop selects on child score, and its Sec 8 says to select improvers "by the improvers they produce" without ever building such a selector.

**Design.** Testbed: the IbI harness (Hyperagents-style operator file), with the task agent evolved on child dev score. The main backbone is Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B (local vLLM or DeepInfra). A second family (DeepSeek-V3.1 or Llama-3.3-70B) is used for the key contrast only. Seven arms, each 5 seeds × 40 generations at matched dollars (modifier plus evaluation tokens):
- A1 child score;
- A2 Δ0 plus an LSE/MetaEvolve-style no-improvement penalty;
- A3 descendant/CMP;
- A4 hurdle Q1-hat on a training meta-state pool;
- A5 IFR-style (current gain plus a historical-gain floor);
- A6 random promotion (negative control);
- A7 A1 plus a forced diagnose quota every k=5 generations, with a placebo-render twin.
Held-out-family Q^(0) is reported only as a labelled oracle ceiling, and an HMED-style matched-entry arm was also requested by the judges.

Improvers are scored at generations 0, 20 and 40 by full nested replay. The replay uses 48 held-out meta-states from 3 or more fault families, disjoint from the A4/A5 selection pool and with independent seeds, at R=6. The successor endpoint is a rebuilt 40-state hard bank (M1 ≈ 0.5), which removes IbI's 0.84 ceiling. A second, non-replay endpoint measures product gain after k inherited generations.

Outcomes are p, μ = E[Q|edit], Δ0 and Δ1. Also reported: corr(Δ0, Δp) over promoted edits with a disattenuation correction, and a blind diff taxonomy (routing, budget, reword or structural; two coders, κ). The statistics are a mixed model (seed > meta-state > replicate) with common random numbers. TOST at ±0.05 is not achievable, so the MDE is pre-registered from pilot variance (target about 0.06–0.08 on ΔΔ1), and p is the primary, best-powered outcome (288 modifier runs per improver at about 1¢ each).

Cost is about $1.4–2.2k with 25% contingency, under the $3k cap: nested replay about $3–8 per improver × about 105 improvers, plus $400–600 for in-loop A4/A5 replay and $300–500 for the loops and second backbone. If over budget, cut generations to 30, then A3/A5, then the second backbone, never meta-states or R. Optional stretch: a small 1.7B/4B GRPO arm contrasting Δ0 vs nested reward (MR-E), about 200 GPU-hours, run only if the gate passes. Timeline: pilot in November, OSF pre-registration by about Dec 20, main runs Jan–Feb, preprint late February (scoop insurance), submission mid-May.

**Build load.** Port the IbI harness to open backbones; it may need the IbI authors' meta-states and transcripts, so consider emailing them. Build the 48 disjoint held-out meta-states across 3+ fault families and the 40-state hard successor bank. Implement 7 selector arms: IFR and HMED reimplementations, and Looped-RSI/RSI-Harness rule specs that need released code or author confirmation. Add the in-loop hurdle Q1-hat estimator, the nested-replay pipeline with CRN, the forced-quota and placebo machinery, the non-replay product endpoint, and the diff-coding protocol. Estimate: about 8–11 person-weeks of build and pilot (harness port and banks about 3–4, arms and replay about 3–4, analysis and coding about 2–3), plus about 8 weeks of runs. Six months solo for 7 arms plus a second backbone is tight (mentor judge). · **Pilot.** Two weeks, about $80. Measure base p and μ for Qwen3-Coder-30B on the IbI banks (100 modifier runs), replicate IbI's positive control (+0.171; 7 meta-states × 6), run A1 vs A4 (2 seeds × 15 generations), and price one nested replay end to end.

GO if all of these hold:
- base p ≥ 0.05, or a dose knob moves p across about 0.05–0.5;
- the positive control gives Δ1 > 0 with a CI excluding 0;
- split-half reliability of Q^(1) ≥ 0.6;
- per-candidate Q1-hat ≤ 10¢.

KILL or PIVOT if the positive control is undetectable on two open backbones. Then pivot to a measurement-only paper (GAP-4 order-2 audit or GAP-1 routing-suppresses-meta-editing), or switch to MiMo or another cheap API backbone.

Note: the expert judge said diagnose-prompt dosing confounds p with μ, and that edit-forcing or a rejection gate should serve as the dose. The current gate still allows the diagnose dose.

This pilot runs in Week 2, alongside the Taught, Not Selected Week-1 pilot. Per round-3 REPORT, it also competes with the unified openness paper for the improver-line slot. If both pass, this paper is the cheaper and more rigorous one, and the choice goes to Xunjian.

**Closest work.** IbI ZMmJDeDTXk measures Q^(1) only, and every loop there selects on child score. Its Sec 8 "select on Q^(1)" is its own obvious next step, which makes its authors the prime fast-followers.

IFR eIJH6HV9LZ already does replay-on-frozen-states improver selection and names "meta-forgetting". HMED AnN0NpAVWL does matched-entry incumbent-vs-revision replay. Each is a single selector, with no head-to-head comparison, no p/μ split and no nested-replay endpoint. Delta: both are included as arms.

RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv (matched-budget gain, effectively an order-0 swap test), Looped-RSI sBH9cdajAw (descendant quality, which IbI Prop 7 says ≠ Q^(1)), MetaEvoForge 0b4wysHrzh, Meta-TTL nw2tokNgb2 and Godel Ecology N4GJVmrzsF each use one reward and evaluate at product level. RepRSI zxN7uhwRY0 runs a 2-arm meta-reward comparison in a weight-curriculum setting. LSE 2603.18620/RTN1lPAkIm, MetaEvolve 2607.21971 and LM2LM do RL on order-0 improver reward, which is the object of the stretch arm. RecurLens vXnQX90fv6 runs an inheritance/successor test, so the paper must state how it goes beyond it. VERSE 9WwpxPfcji covers surface × feedback.

Exact delta: the first controlled comparison of meta-rewards scored on held-out, disjoint-family nested-replay Q^(1). It decomposes each reward's effect into edit rate p vs edit quality μ, adds a causal forced-edit placebo, and gives an edit-level corr(Δ0, Δp) test of routing suppressing meta-editing. Positioning vs the round-3 unified openness paper: that paper uses meta-reward only as a 2-level credit term κ in a drifting environment, while this one is stationary banks with a Δ1 endpoint. They share the quota-placebo, p/μ and nested-replay machinery.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.62).** I ran about 10 searches: ICLR 2027 OpenReview with 8 queries plus abstract pulls, 2 alphaXiv searches ranked by recency, and Semantic Scholar, which returned nothing and was probably rate-limited. No paper I found runs a head-to-head comparison of meta-rewards scored on held-out nested-replay Q^(1). None splits an effect into edit rate p and edit quality mu, and none has a forced-edit placebo. The surrounding RSI literature is very dense, though, with roughly 40 RSI-titled ICLR 2027 submissions and 5 to 10 new arXiv papers a week in Oct and Nov 2026. So a partial overlap by May 2027 is likely, and a full one is possible.

Ranked threats:
(1) The IbI authors (ZMmJDeDTXk), with probability about 0.35-0.45 of something close by May. Their Sec 8 'select improvers by the improvers they produce' is the obvious next step. They own the harness and could add a Q^(1)-selector arm in the ICLR rebuttal or revision (Nov-Jan), or submit it to ICML 2027 (preprints around Feb). Most likely form: one new selector vs child score, frontier backbones, 2-3 seeds, no p/mu split, no equivalence or MDE framing. It would probably be strong on the headline and weak on breadth and statistics.
(2) The IFR (eIJH6HV9LZ, meta-forgetting) and HMED (AnN0NpAVWL) groups, about 0.25. They could add baseline comparisons against child-score or Delta0 selection in revisions. That would turn their single-selector papers into small 2-3 arm bake-offs, but on their own endpoints, not disjoint-family nested replay.
(3) RSI-Harness (EOrtreQLbv), Looped-RSI (sBH9cdajAw) and the HGM line, about 0.2. They might add ablations on alternative rewards, which would be product-level only.
(4) New adjacent work:
- LRSI (71wzcahKcb) is a lookahead-contribution edit selector, effectively another meta-reward family. It has 4 benchmarks, 3 backbones and matched budgets. Reviewers will ask why it is not an arm.
- 'Why Is RSI Hard to Verify?' (OnzWTehIaN) gives successor-mechanism criteria and statistical tests. It could pre-empt the framing of the methodology and statistics.
- Others are only general background and could add a meta-level metric to a benchmark: Learning to Improve (V7vjimAYGi), Agent Plasticity (Jjh60xpFVY), the replay-to-online audit protocol (x4AF6x9znL), Flex-RSI / SelfImprove-Bench / 'Beneath the Surface' (arXiv 2611.08946, 2611.02797, 2611.07624) and Skill-infinity (2610.35434).

Overall, I expect 1-2 papers by May 2027 that compare 2-3 selection criteria on some meta-level endpoint, usually with frontier models and weaker statistics. A careful 7-arm study is unlikely to appear before ours. *Stay ahead:* 1. Add an LRSI-style lookahead-contribution arm, or at least a cheap reduced-seed version. Also cite OnzWTehIaN as the formal criterion and map our Q^(1) and nested replay onto its 'successor mechanism' condition. Together these close the most obvious reviewer gap.
2. Make the p/mu decomposition, the forced-edit placebo, the corr(Delta0, Delta p) routing test and the diff taxonomy the headline contributions. They are what fast-followers running a single selector will not have, and they hold up even if IbI publishes 'select on Q^(1) works'.
3. Keep the late-Feb preprint, and have a minimal 'A1 vs A4 vs A7 on backbone 1' result public by about Feb 1. That is earlier than ICML-cycle preprints and IbI revision updates.
4. Hedge on backbone. If competitors use frontier models, run a small frontier-model check (e.g. 2 seeds of A1/A4/A7) so we can't be dismissed as a weak-backbone result. Low p on a 30B-A3B model could make the paper uninformative.
5. Watch the IbI, IFR and HMED OpenReview revision threads and the Feb ICML arXiv wave. If IbI adds a Q^(1) selector, reposition: run their selector as an arm and frame ours as the controlled, decomposed, pre-registered version.
6. Pre-register the MDE and publish the bank and harness. Being the reusable benchmark for meta-reward comparison makes us the reference version even with concurrent work. *New finds:* Not in the original related-work list:
- 71wzcahKcb: LRSI, lookahead-guided edit selection. Its selection criterion is close enough to be an arm.
- OnzWTehIaN: 'Why Is RSI Hard to Verify?', a successor-mechanism criterion with statistical tests.
- V7vjimAYGi: 'Learning to Improve', learnable RSI that edits the repair program.
- x4AF6x9znL: an audit protocol for replay-to-online transport.
- Jjh60xpFVY: Agent Plasticity.
- 75PPLZgaUM: RRSI.
- wV4bK1dHpC / arXiv 2611.10787: Trustworthy RSI with statistical guarantees.
- arXiv 2611.07624: 'Beneath the Surface' (agent evolution reliability and transfer).
- arXiv 2610.35434: Skill-infinity.
- arXiv 2611.08946: Flex-RSI benchmark.
- arXiv 2611.02797: SelfImprove-Bench.
- arXiv 2610.13991: 'Predict Before You Modify'.
- arXiv 2610.29121: 'RSI on a Budget: scoring edits before they run'.
- arXiv 2610.16491: Speculative RSI.

- **ac** — risk: A floor or power null. Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B may edit rarely (p near 0, as in IbI), and resolution is only about 0.06-0.08 on ΔΔ1. If so, the seven arms could tie, giving an inconclusive result that reviewers read as "a benchmark of other people's selectors that found nothing". That risk is made worse by a 7-arm, 2-backbone build that is too large for one undergrad in six months, which would push the work toward thin seeds or cut arms. On novelty, being clearly better than IFR, HMED and RSI-Harness through the head-to-head on held-out nested-replay Q^(1) plus the p/μ split is enough. But a fast IbI follow-up with frontier backbones would shrink that margin in reviewers' eyes. · raise P: Cut to 4 core arms: A1 child score, A2 Δ0, A4 hurdle Q1-hat, and A7 forced-edit quota with a placebo twin, plus A6 random as a cheap control. Use edit-forcing or a rejection gate as the p-dose instead of diagnose prompting. Put the saved budget into more seeds and more held-out meta-states, so that ΔΔ1 and Δp come out decisive (MDE ≤0.05) and the forced-edit placebo gives a clean causal p-vs-μ result. Report that result with a second backbone. A decisive mechanism result with fewer arms scores better than a broad but underpowered bake-off.
- **reviewer** — risk: The floor and power problem: if the open 30B-A3B improver almost never makes meta-edits (IbI found p near 0), all seven arms tie on Δ1 inside an MDE of about 0.06-0.08. That leaves an inconclusive null on a single main backbone, and reviewers will call it measurement-only and underpowered. Execution risk adds to this. One student has to build 7 arms, two of them reimplemented without code, plus nested replay, disjoint banks and a second backbone, and the likely result is thin seeds or dropped arms. · raise P: Cut to 4 core arms: A1 child score, A2 Δ0, A4 hurdle/replay and A7 forced-quota plus placebo, with A6 random as a cheap control. Use edit-forcing or a rejection gate as the dose knob, not diagnose prompting. Put the savings into more seeds and a full second backbone. The aim is for the headline Δp/ΔΔ1 contrast, plus the corr(Δ0, Δp) routing-suppression figure, to replicate across two model families with CIs that exclude zero. One clean mechanism figure that replicates across families raises acceptance more than breadth does.
- **pi** — risk: Execution and floor risk together. A solo undergrad has to port the IbI harness, build 88 disjoint held-out and successor meta-states, and reimplement 7 arms, which is 8-11 weeks of build before any runs. On top of that, the open 30B-A3B improver may rarely meta-edit (p near 0). If it doesn't, every arm ties and the result is a null nobody can interpret, or a rushed, underpowered paper with a thin set of arms. The IbI authors fast-following with a Q^(1) selector makes that worse. · raise P: Commit early to a reduced 4-arm core: A1 child score, A2 Delta0, A4 hurdle replay and A7 forced-quota plus placebo. Use edit-forcing or a rejection gate as the dose instead of the diagnose prompt, and put the build time into p-gate and pilot power. Then the headline, that order-0 selection suppresses p and replay or a quota raises Delta1 through p, comes out clean and well powered by February. Treat IFR, HMED, A3 and the second backbone as add-ons if budget and time allow.

### Meta-Overfitting: Self-Referential Improvers Get Better at Improving Only Where They Grew Up (effect-cause-decay v2, standalone; FINAL_REPORT #2, panel 72.1, spread 6.7) (`meta-overfitting`) — EV 9.9%

**Claim.** The self-edits that make a Gödel-style improver better at improving on its home domain make it worse than its own gen-0 procedure at improving a fresh agent on distant domains. The deficit survives a memory wipe and a domain-fact scrub of the improver's code, and it grows with A→B distance, crossing zero between near and far domains. Operationally, at k* = 12 accepted improver self-edits on ALFWorld with n = 10 lineages: Gate H = G_A(Pk^s,∅) − G_A(P0,∅) > 3 pp (SESOI). Primary: D_far = G_far(Pk^s,∅) − G_far(P0,∅) < 0, with a lineage-level 95% CI excluding 0. Dose: negative slope of D_B on pre-registered distance, near cell possibly positive (this reconciles the result with VERSE G.2). Secondary design rule: on far B, "reset procedure, keep memory" (P0+Mk) ≥ "forget memory, keep procedure" (Pk+∅). Falsified if D_far ≥ 0 while the gate holds. Pre-registered alternatives: (a) D_far ≥ 0 means controlled evidence of domain-general procedural meta-learning; (b) κ ≥ 0.7 (Pk passes the gate, Pk^s fails) means "evolved improver procedures are memory in disguise". Kill if even the unscrubbed Pk fails the home gate after one pivot. Per the panel, it must now be pitched as a bounded rebuttal of the ICLR27 positive-transfer wave, not as a first.

**Design.** Gödel-lite is a stripped-down self-referential loop built from Xunjian's Gödel Agent code. It has proposer, verifier/selector, budget-allocator and history-reader modules, which it can edit itself. Generations are counted in accepted improver edits, and Pk is pre-registered as the incumbent improver, not the archive best (HMED winner's curse). Models: improver Qwen3.5-35B-A3B served locally, with DeepSeek V4 Flash as the API fallback. Executor is an 8-9B open model on vLLM. Scrubber and leak judge come from a different family. Domains are existing seeded ones: ALFWorld (home), ScienceWorld (near), SelfImprove-Bench text games (mid) and Reasoning Gym (far). Distance is pre-registered as the zero-shot transfer gap of the gen-k* executor harness, cross-checked against embedding distance. Five arms are run at a matched improver-call budget on a fresh seed executor per domain: P0+∅, Pk^s+∅ (primary), Pk+∅ (gives κ), P0+Mk and Pk+Mk. Controls: P0^s scrub control, length-padded P0, leak audit, and a manipulation check (JS divergence of edit categories, CI excluding 0). Exploratory: introspective-drift share vs D_far. Analysis uses lineage-level paired t-tests with Holm correction, a mixed-model dose slope, and MDE ≈ 4 pp at σ_lineage = 4 (going to 14 lineages if σ > 5). A 4-lineage VERSE-optimizer port separates notes from tools. Cost is ≈ $1.4-1.7k ($2.2k with buffer): $300-400 for lineages, $700-950 for ~480 probes, $60 for judges, $250 for the VERSE port. It needs a part-time 4-8×A100/H100 node, not yet confirmed. Timeline: OSF pre-registration by Dec 20, preprint by Feb 15. Judge-requested additions not yet in v2: a reproduced positive near-domain cell (ideally RSI-Harness's own setup with and without wipe and scrub), a second home domain or an A↔B swap, a second improver model as a full arm, an agentic far domain with the same interface (e.g. an RSIBench split), and a format adapter for P0+Mk. Round 3 adds a forced improver-edit quota plus a placebo null-edit twin (IBI arm A7) to guarantee k*, with the inert fraction reported. Fallback scope if variance is high: 3 domains × 3 arms (P0, Pk^s, Pk+∅).

**Build load.** From scratch: (1) a Gödel-lite self-referential loop with editable improver modules, the accepted-edit gate and edit logging, adapted from the Gödel Agent code (~2-3 wk). (2) Wrappers that put ALFWorld, ScienceWorld, SelfImprove-Bench games and Reasoning Gym behind one executor harness interface, with fresh-seed-executor probing and a matched call budget (~1-1.5 wk). (3) The scrubber, a cross-family leak judge and an edit-category coder validated on 100 human labels (~1 wk). (4) A forced-edit quota plus placebo twin, if the pivot is triggered (~0.5 wk). (5) A VERSE optimizer port with notes split from tools (~1 wk). (6) The distance metric, OSF pre-registration and analysis/power simulation (~0.5-1 wk). Total ≈ 6-8 person-weeks before the full runs. The panel adds a second home domain or improver family and an agentic same-interface far domain, which pushes this to ~8-10 person-weeks, heavy for a solo undergrad (mentor's main concern). · **Pilot.** Pilot runs one week (Nov 16-23), ≤ $50. Setup: 2 Gödel-lite lineages on an ALFWorld subset (60 dev / 50 held-out tasks) run to 6 accepted improver edits, then probes P0+∅, Pk+∅ and Pk^s+∅ on ALFWorld and Reasoning Gym, R = 3, 1 seed. Also read 2610.14662, 2610.31436 and 2611.07624 in full. GO requires all of: ≥ 1 accepted improver-touching edit per 4 iterations, home gain of Pk ≥ 3 pp in both lineages, leak judge ≤ 40% (6-way) and probe cost ≤ $2.5. GO leads to freezing k* and the pre-registration. PIVOT (once) if the edit rate is too low or the home gain is in [0,3) pp: switch to DeepSeek V4 Flash, force improver-module edits (quota + placebo per round 3; fallback k* ≈ 6), or move A to a Reasoning Gym family. KILL if the unscrubbed Pk is still below the SESOI after the pivot, falling back to the other Gödel-lite ideas. The sign of D_far is recorded but is not a gate. The panel adds three conditions: verify the edit rate against ZMmJDeDTXk's near-zero self-edit finding, pre-specify what a forced-edit pivot means for validity, and confirm GPUs with Xunjian before Nov 23. In FINAL_REPORT this pilot runs in Week 2, after the Taught-Not-Selected pilot. Round 3 proposes replacing it with a merged IbI-calibration / meta-reward pilot.

**Closest work.** Opposite-sign ICLR27 submissions, all claiming positive improver transfer: RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv (improver gain 1.3→6.2 pts, transfers up to 4.5× to unseen models, benchmarks and domains; same question, opposite sign), MetaEvoForge 0b4wysHrzh, FreeEvolve 2610.09197/8UyLPIcKqX (+6.9 held-out, though a Haiku evolver overfits 35→52 on train vs 33.3→32.2 held-out), Looped-RSI sBH9cdajAw and Meta-TTL nw2tokNgb2 (+27% OOD). None wipes memory, scrubs domain facts or grades distance. That gap is the whole delta. Measurement lineage, now prior work: Improvement by Inheritance ZMmJDeDTXk owns nested replay over improver × state and finds improvers almost never edit themselves (the edit-rate risk). Learning to Repair the Learner aEPJT4hW84 owns the code × history crossing (inherited procedure = 45% of gain). HMED 2610.07979 has the in-domain Pk+∅ cell. Shape precedents: RSI-Opt 2610.32026 (held-out transfer peaks at gen 1, but product-level with a fixed meta-procedure). RSIBench Q9AHmuAk4Y (GPT-5.5 loses 8.3 pp held-out at harness level, which supports the sign flip). RRSI 75PPLZgaUM / ModularRSI pP6df7PZI7 (harness-level memorization; mitigation baselines). 2609.39148 (skills copy task details). Motivating anomaly: VERSE 2610.02616 App. G.2 (unscrubbed SWE→TB transfer helps, with notes lumped with tools). Mechanism and rigor precedents: Lineage Is Memory 2610.41157 (facts migrate into artifacts, the basis for κ), SelfSearch 2609.37968 Table 4 (in-domain gaps inside noise), MetaRSI 2609.06396 §5.4 (shows the gate is attainable). Exact delta: a scrubbed, memory-wiped improver procedure tested at lineage-level power on a pre-registered distance dose that is predicted to cross zero, plus κ (domain knowledge inside the procedure) and the P0+Mk vs Pk+∅ design rule. Every ingredient has a one-cell precedent. The claim and its powered, scrubbed, distance-dosed test do not.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.58).** The area is very crowded and getting more so every week. Since October about 10 or more papers questioning RSI evaluation or transfer have appeared, and several of them look like "bounded rebuttals" too. In order of threat:

(1) The RSI-Harness authors (EOrtreQLbv) will probably add held-out or wipe ablations during the ICLR rebuttal (Nov-Jan) because reviewers will ask about memorization. Expect a one-cell, unscrubbed version, probably still showing a positive sign. That is weak as competition but strengthens their claim.

(2) The Improvement by Inheritance (ZMmJDeDTXk) and Learning to Repair the Learner (aEPJT4hW84) groups already have nested-replay infrastructure over improver × state. A cross-domain extension for ICML 2027 (around Feb) or NeurIPS 2027 is plausible, at about 35-45%. It would be rigorous on causal replay but probably single-distance, with no domain-fact scrub and no κ. This is the most serious threat to "better version".

(3) The audit and negative-result wave is mostly harness- or product-level. None of it isolates the improver procedure under a memory wipe and scrub, and none grades distance:
- Beneath the Surface 2611.07624 (SJTU/BIGAI): transfer selectively preserves gains; coverage falls on the general target but rises on coding. This is a mixed-sign, product-level transfer finding.
- Auditing the Product, Not the Recursion 2610.27610: a meta-audit showing only 22% of studies rerun the full loop.
- Why Is RSI Hard to Verify? OnzWTehIaN: a successor-competence criterion, but theoretical and trace-based.
- Does Failure Feedback Direct Self-Modification? 2610.14662: a pre-registered DGM log analysis.
- Self-Evolving Agents Can Repair Failures but Struggle to Generalize from Them 2610.22984: a repair-generalization gap.
- RSIBench, RRSI and ModularRSI.

These groups could turn into an improver-level transfer study by May, at about 25%, but would likely reuse fixed-improver designs.

(4) Positive-transfer method papers will keep coming, and their revised versions may add more held-out domains:
- MetaRule C6OPabL11Q: cross-domain harness evolution
- Meta-TTL nw2tokNgb2
- MetaEvoForge 0b4wysHrzh
- Evo^2 2610.13025: evolving the evolver, from DeepMind
- RSIFlow 2610.21791: an evolving meta-agent

These raise the stakes of the rebuttal rather than competing with it.

(5) HMED and FreeEvolve follow-ups could add a far-domain Pk+∅ cell.

Probability that some paper by mid-May 2027 tests improver-procedure transfer with memory removed across at least 2 distances: about 50%. Probability it also scrubs domain facts and is powered at lineage level: about 15-20%. *Stay ahead:* Per the user's instruction, weight differentiation over firstness. Being the more complete version is what matters.

(a) Lock the moat items cheaply first:
- OSF pre-registration by Dec 20, timestamped, citing the RSI-Harness, MetaEvoForge, FreeEvolve and Meta-TTL claims as the targets being rebutted.
- Report κ and the scrub control, since no competitor has either.
- Make the distance dose with its pre-registered zero crossing the headline figure, not a single far cell. One-cell transfer results from audit papers will look subsumed next to it.

(b) De-risk the edit-rate problem now, because it is the main execution risk and the IBI groups will use it against you. Ship the forced-edit quota with the placebo null-edit twin (IBI arm A7) and report the inert fraction, so that k*=12 is guaranteed.

(c) Run the judge-requested reproduced positive near-domain cell in RSI-Harness's own setup, with and without wipe and scrub. That converts "opposite sign" into "same setup, sign flips under controls", and that version is hard to out-rigor.

(d) Add a second improver model as a full arm and an agentic far domain (an RSIBench split). These are what a fast-follower from the IBI or HMED groups would lack.

(e) Get the preprint out by Feb 15, before ICML submissions surface, and position it explicitly against Beneath the Surface 2611.07624 and Auditing the Product, Not the Recursion 2610.27610. Their framing is "product-level mixed transfer"; ours is "improver-level, controlled, dosed".

(f) Confirm the GPU node within 2 weeks. If it is not secured, drop to the 3 domains × 3 arms fallback at 10-14 lineages rather than thinning out power, because power and pre-registration are the differentiators.

(g) Watch the ICLR27 rebuttal revisions of EOrtreQLbv, ZMmJDeDTXk and aEPJT4hW84 in mid-Dec and Jan for added held-out or wipe ablations, and update related work. *New finds:* New close papers found in these searches, all needing citation:
- 2611.07624, Beneath the Surface: What We Overlook When We Talk About Agent Evolution. Mixed-sign transfer of evolved harnesses; the closest new product-level transfer finding.
- 2610.27610, Auditing the Product, Not the Recursion: The Evaluation Evidence Behind Recursive Self-Improvement Claims. Meta-audit; 22% of studies rerun the full loop; motivates the process-level test.
- OnzWTehIaN, Why Is Recursive Self-Improvement Hard to Verify? (ICLR27). A successor-competence criterion with statistical tests.
- 2610.14662, Does Failure Feedback Direct Agent Self-Modification? A pre-registered DGM log analysis; target repair does not imply held-out gain.
- 2610.22984, Self-Evolving Agents Can Repair Failures but Struggle to Generalize from Them. A repair-generalization gap.
- C6OPabL11Q, MetaRule: Transactional Procedure Learning for Cross-Domain Harness Evolution (ICLR27). A positive-transfer claim at procedure level.
- 2610.13025, Evo^2: Evolving the Evolver through Weight-Harness Co-Evolution (DeepMind/UIUC). Evolver evolution; a likely positive-transfer target.
- 2610.21791, RSIFlow: Adaptive Self-Improvement Workflows with an Evolving Meta-Agent.
- 2610.34386, ParetoRSI-Gym: Can AI Agents Improve Language Models Across Multiple Domains? A multi-domain RSI benchmark and a possible far-domain resource.
- 2611.12988, Third Eye: Beyond Immediate Gains in Recursive Self-Improvement. Whether a useful update stays useful after a further update.
- 3t0j2Y05EH, Self-Evolution Should Be Stable, Not Frozen: Lyapunov-Gated Recursive Self-Improvement (ICLR27).
- 2611.10787 / wV4bK1dHpC, Trustworthy Recursive Self-Improvement with Statistical Guarantees.
- 2611.04277, Harness-S: Learning from Sibling Harnesses for Generalizable Agent Training.
- 2610.22466, Harness-Delta Attribution: Dissecting Gains From Harness Evolution.

None of these wipes memory, scrubs domain facts from the improver procedure, or grades distance at lineage-level power.

- **ac** — risk: Two risks share the top spot. The first is the edit rate and k*. ZMmJDeDTXk found that improvers almost never edit themselves, so the open-model Gödel-lite may never reach 12 accepted improver edits. That pushes the design toward forced edits, and reviewers will call a forced, possibly inert edit an artifact. The second is the result itself. If D_far comes out negative on ALFWorld to Reasoning Gym with one improver family and one home domain, reviewers will read it as a format shift, a boundary condition rather than meta-overfitting. If D_far is not negative, the null branch is no longer novel, because RSI-Harness already reports positive transfer. Behind both sits the build load: about 8-10 person-weeks for a solo undergrad, on a GPU node that is not yet confirmed. · raise P: Reproduce RSI-Harness's own positive near-domain cell, then show the sign flip inside that same setup with the memory wipe and the scrub. Add an A↔B home-domain swap, or an agentic far domain that uses the same interface, so distance is separated from task format. Together these turn the paper from a one-family boundary condition into a direct, bounded rebuttal of the ICLR27 positive-transfer papers that reviewers can check. Securing this one change matters more than adding a fifth arm or a second improver model.
- **reviewer** — risk: Execution and confounding together. The solo undergrad has 8-10 person-weeks of building to do, GPUs are not confirmed, and the near-zero self-edit rate reported by ZMmJDeDTXk may force a pivot to forced edits with k* around 6, which weakens validity. If it does get done, a reviewer can still read a one-family, one-home-domain result whose far cell (ALFWorld to Reasoning Gym) is mostly a change of task format as a boundary condition, not meta-overfitting. That turns a one-sided rebuttal of RSI-Harness into 'measurement-only, single model'. · raise P: Reproduce the positive near-domain cell in RSI-Harness's own setup, then show the sign flip with wipe and scrub on an agentic far domain that uses the same interface (for example an RSIBench split), so distance is separated from format. Add a second improver family as a full arm. Pay for this by cutting to 3 domains x 3 arms (P0, Pk^s, Pk+empty) instead of 5 arms x 4 domains. This turns 'boundary condition on a format shift' into a controlled sign-reversal dose curve that reviewers can't dismiss.
- **pi** — risk: Execution and validity at the same time. Under Gödel-lite the open-model improver may rarely edit itself (the ZMmJDeDTXk near-zero self-edit finding), which pushes the project into the forced-edit pivot, and forced, inert edits weaken the claim that an evolved procedure is overfitting. On top of that, the build is 8-10 person-weeks for a solo undergrad, the GPUs are unconfirmed, and the run is 10-14 lineages × 5 arms × 4 domains. The likely result is the 3×3 fallback with one home domain and one improver family. Reviewers would then read D_far<0 on ALFWorld→Reasoning Gym as a format shift or a scrubber artifact rather than meta-overfitting, especially against RSI-Harness's opposite-sign result. · raise P: Before scaling to more arms, buy external validity on the axis reviewers will attack. Reproduce one positive near-domain cell in RSI-Harness's own setup, with and without the memory wipe and scrub, and add an agentic far domain that uses the same interface (or an A↔B swap). This separates distance from task format and turns the paper into a credible bounded rebuttal. To pay for it, cut the arms to P0, Pk^s, Pk+∅ and P0+Mk, and run k*≈6 if needed.

### Blind-Spot Hiding: When a Self-Improving Agent Writes Its Own Curriculum, It Steers Away From What It Cannot Do (curriculum-goodhart v2; FINAL_REPORT #5, panel 67.8, spread 4.2) (`blind-spot-hiding`) — EV 9.8%

**Claim.** This is a mechanism claim with a pre-registered sign. Setup: a frozen-weight meta-agent is allowed to edit its own task writer. Prediction: the writer moves probability mass away from the rule types the agent truly fails on, and it does so more than a frozen adaptive writer does. Two tests:
- T1 (steering sign). Let rho be the within-run Spearman correlation between a rule's true failure rate f_{g,r} and the writer's next-generation change in that rule's share Δq. T1 says mean rho(A3) < 0 and mean rho(A3) < mean rho(A2).
- T2 (contingency harm). Because the curriculum depends on the agent's own weaknesses, the agent ends worse on exact ground truth than a yoked twin trained on the identical task stream at matched compute. Formally, C = T20(A3) - T20(Yoked) < 0, paired over 10 yoke pairs, with TOST bounds of ±5 pp.

Secondary claim (H3, band censorship): the standard [0.2, 0.8] solve-rate band is by itself a blind-spot censor, so rho_post-filter < rho_pre-filter.

Every outcome is readable:
- Alt-1, "honest teachers": rho > 0 and C > 0, confirmed by the compute-matched Yk+ arm. This would be the first causal evidence that self-curricula help.
- Alt-2, "content-insensitive learners": the placebo Pl comes out equivalent to A2 while the oracle A* clears A2 by at least 5 pp.
- Uninterpretable: no oracle headroom. The pilot is designed to kill this case early.

Per the panel, the defensible claim is narrower than written. Concede the weight-side mechanism. Claim only (a) the frozen-weight self-edited writer substrate, (b) the signed per-rule rho against exact truth, and (c) the yoked-twin contingency test.

**Design.** World: one procedural tau-style world W1 with 15 rule types. The reference rule mix psi* comes from the tau2-bench policy categories. Success is exact: the end DB state must equal the gold state. Truth is measured outside the loop on 40 fresh stratified tasks per rule per generation, using common random numbers across arms.

Agent and improver: the solver is a frozen Qwen3.5-9B on vLLM. Its editable harness covers the prompt, tool wrappers, a memory file and control code. There is one improver, I2: GEPA/Meta-Harness-style reflective mutation driven by Qwen3.5-27B, with a Haiku/mini-class API fallback.

Per-generation loop:
1. The writer emits tasks.
2. Validity filter (S1).
3. [0.2, 0.8] pass-rate band (S2).
4. 200 tasks go to the pool and 100 to the gate.
5. The meta-agent proposes K=4 harness candidates, and a paired-test gate picks one.
6. In A3 only, the meta-agent may also propose one writer edit.

Tier-1 arms, 20 generations each:

| Arm | What it is | Seeds |
|---|---|---|
| A0 | fixed real tasks | 4 |
| Pl | placebo tasks from the irrelevant world W2 | 4 |
| A2 | frozen adaptive writer (ActiveSaddler-style) | 6 |
| A3 | self-edited writer | 10 |
| Yk | replays each A3 lineage's exact pool and gate stream to a fresh learner seed | 10 |
| A* | oracle writer, sampling rules in proportion to true failure | 4 |

That is about 38 runs for about $0.9k.
- Tier 2: 5% anchors in the gate vs in the pool (4+4 runs), plus a W2 replication of A3 vs Yk (3+3).
- Conditional: Yk+, run only if C > 0.
- Optional Tier 3: a tau2-bench cell.
- Total: at most 70 runs, about $1.6k, at roughly $20 per run.

Statistics:
- A manipulation check is pre-registered: a median of at least 4 accepted writer edits, and a TV distance above the A2 permutation null in at least 6 of 10 lineages.
- H1 and H2 get Holm correction.
- rho is reported disattenuated by split-half reliability, and as a partial correlation controlling for same-step improvement Δf.
- Power: 10 pairs detect |C| ≥ 3.5 pp, assuming a paired SD of about 4 pp. 10 vs 6 lineages detect Δrho of about 0.18.

Panel-requested additions:
- Make ActiveSaddler and DiagEvo writer arms Tier 1.
- Split T1 by learnability as measured by A*.
- Compute rho on pre-filter output.
- Tighten TOST to ±3 pp or use 14 pairs.
- Add a second solver family or size, and move W2 into Tier 1.

**Build load.** Everything is built from scratch:
- the W1 procedural world: 15-rule DSL, deterministic scripted user, gold-state executor, a D* sampler with tau2-derived psi*, and the stratified truth evaluator;
- a WriterAPI that emits rule-labelled tasks, so q_{g,r} is observed without a judge;
- the S1/S2 filters with logging of pre- and post-filter rule shares;
- yoke-replay tooling that logs and replays exact pool and gate streams with seeds;
- the I2 reflective improver loop with a separate writer module and gate;
- the oracle writer;
- the W2 placebo/replication world;
- the analysis code: rho with cluster bootstrap, disattenuation, TOST.

The v2 plan budgets about 3 weeks of build (Nov 12-30) to reach full W1 plus yoke tooling, which is about 3-4 person-weeks of build. The panel additions (ActiveSaddler/DiagEvo writer arms, a second solver, W2 in Tier 1) add about 2 more. Including runs and analysis, the total is roughly 8-10 person-weeks of a solo undergrad's time through Mar 2027, then writing. · **Pilot.** One week, at most $50. The pilot uses a minimal W1 (8 rules, 20 truth tasks per rule) and runs A2, A3, A* and Yk for 2 seeds each over 10 generations. The sign and size of rho and C are not go criteria.

| Pilot outcome | Decision |
|---|---|
| A* - T0 ≥ 4 pp at g=10, at least 2 accepted writer edits per A3 lineage, at least 80% clean edits, per-run SE(rho) ≤ 0.1 | GO |
| A* learns but A2 and A3 are flat | GO, with the "misses headroom" story strengthened |
| Fewer than 2 accepted writer edits per 10 generations | PIVOT: force a writer-edit proposal every 2 generations. If the writer distribution q still does not move beyond the permutation null, demote to a section of another paper ("meta-agents do not use writer access") |
| Clean edits below 50% | Switch to an API meta-model (about +$300) |
| A* - T0 < 3 pp | One retry with a harder W1. If still flat, KILL as a standalone paper and fold H3 band censorship (which needs only writer logs) into the #1 paper |
| Paired SD of C above 6 pp | Use 14 pairs and drop W2 |

**Closest work.** Most of the threat comes from ICLR 2027 OpenReview submissions.

High threat:
- **ecw2VvTeE5, "What Actually Evolves in Self-Evolving RL"**:
  - Shows that gains concentrate on items the model already solves.
  - In a quasi-yoked proposer swap, a later proposer teaches no more than an earlier one.
  - Delta: it uses weight-trained math, has no per-rule signed rho and no true learner-contingent yoke.
- **IkYmTAyPXZ, "Capability Loss in Embodied RSI"**:
  - Shows the same mechanism: selection down-weights weak and tail modes.
  - Delta: embodied weight-RSI with a fixed task distribution, no meta-edited writer and no yoke.
- **2611.06965, "When Can Self-Play Fix a False Belief?"**:
  - The planted generator writes 0 of 2,061 tasks that expose its error, and the 1-7/8 pass band admits almost no corrective tasks.
  - This is close to both T1 and H3.
  - Delta: a 4B weight learner, a fixed writer, and no causal pre/post-filter test.
- **SkillMentor 2607.27360**:
  - A frozen executor, with an RL mentor that targets blind spots.
  - It holds the premise this paper attacks, but checks only endpoint accuracy and has no per-category truth audit or yoke.

Medium threat:
- **TURTLE 2610.27307**:
  - Under its c(1-c) reward, the always-fail share of tasks falls from 12.3% to about 0%.
  - That is band censorship seen from outside, but it has no learner loop.
- **ActiveSaddler xmpyzuagpu**:
  - Uses exactly this substrate: a frozen-model harness curriculum run as a bandit over failure patterns.
  - Its control is a fixed order, not a yoke, so it should become a named writer arm.
- **DiagEvo MkNQtnWiuP**: a diagnosis-guided challenger. It is another "hunt weaknesses" method to audit.
- **UIvgqCcjE2**: proposer refinement filters do not beat no filter. This partly pre-empts Alt-2 and the band angle.
- **Double Ratchet 2607.12790**: a vacuous grader trains as well as a real one, which makes a T2 null likely.
- **False Frontiers Id4G8bzzKO / 2609.39102**: replaying identical proposals, which is yoke lineage.

Low threat:
- **R-Quest TMQXORjxix**: filters distort along validity, not blind spots.
- **R2Orch 2610.22740**: the closest structural overlap, an outer loop that edits the task-proposal objective, but with no truth audit.
- **RoboACE 2611.13034**: a curriculum harness with no control on who writes.

Also must cite: bZibTXNqcG, 44lXAKTNqb (which targets only recoverable deficits, an implicit band), GrokRSI, SPADE, CompassPlay, Explorer-Zero, Aspire 2608.31111, A-RSI 2610.38568, EAU 2610.16523, Feedback Dynamics 2608.02636 and SWE-Duel 2610.12569.

Exact delta: no retrieved paper tests whether a self-edited task writer under a frozen-weight harness loop steers away from the agent's true per-rule failures (a signed rho against exact truth), and none uses a yoked twin on the identical stream to isolate learner-contingency from content.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.6).** I ran 11 searches: 8 against the ICLR 2027 OpenReview search endpoint and 3 on alphaXiv, with recency ranking. Two caveats on coverage. First, OpenReview's notes?ids endpoint returned a 403 challenge, so I read abstracts through the search endpoint. Second, I skipped Semantic Scholar because of rate limits.

The field is crowded with papers that audit self-play and self-evolution. Through mid-May 2027, the threats split into three classes.

(1) Diagnostic papers on weight-trained self-play that will be revised, or follow-ups from the same groups. These are ecw2VvTeE5 (gains concentrate on items the model already solves), 2611.06965 (the generator writes 0 of 2,061 corrective tasks, plus pass-band censorship), IkYmTAyPXZ and UIvgqCcjE2. Rebuttal-phase revisions to ICLR (Nov-Jan) could add per-category breakdowns, and ICML 2027 arXiv versions around Feb could add a proposer-swap or replay control. The most likely close competitor is a v2 of 2611.06965 or ecw2VvTeE5 that adds a per-skill correlation between failure rate and generation share. I put that at about 35-45%. Quality would likely be narrow: a single small weight-trained model, a fixed writer, and no exact-truth stratified audit.

(2) Groups building harness and writer infrastructure who could run our test cheaply. These include ActiveSaddler (xmpyzuagpu / 2610.00906) and its follow-up ReproSaddler (kV2zYmLeEp). They also include task-harness co-evolution for data synthesis (6a1CEKvNeC), which is in effect a self-edited writer under frozen weights, though it is optimized for difficulty and has no learner loop. Others are Task-CoEvolve (d5UGox3RGt), R_Q-Evolve (B8oWwVLeGz, which evolves its own generator programs but trains weights) and RSI-Harness (EOrtreQLbv). Rounding out the list are 'Masked Erosion' 2610.38392, 'When the Critic Falls Behind' 2610.36826 and SkillMentor. These are mostly method papers. A blind-spot audit by them (for example "our writer covers weak categories", which is the opposite sign) is about 25% likely and would probably be an ablation, not a pre-registered sign test.

(3) The yoke lineage. 2611.02362, 'Prior Action Control Causally Alters LM Plasticity', is new and important. It already uses a yoked LM twin with identical rewards that are not contingent on the twin's own outputs, in a weight-training setting. So "first yoke in LLMs" is gone. "First yoke on the curriculum stream in a harness loop" remains open.

Overall, I see about 50-60% odds that at least one paper by May 2027 makes a qualitatively similar "self-curricula avoid weaknesses" claim. I see only about 15% odds that it has both a signed per-rule rho against exact truth and a learner-contingent yoke. Such a paper would most likely come from a self-play-audit group, not a harness group. *Stay ahead:* Following the user's steer, I put less weight on pure novelty and focused on being clearly the more rigorous and complete version.

(1) Lock in the three-part identity early and say it consistently: a frozen-weight self-edited writer, a signed per-rule rho against exact out-of-loop truth, and a yoked twin on the identical stream. Post a preprint plus the pre-registration (OSF or arXiv) by about Feb-Mar 2027, before ICML-cycle versions of 2611.06965 or ecw2VvTeE5 appear.

(2) Breadth that competitors will lack. Promote the ActiveSaddler, DiagEvo and a 6a1CEKvNeC-style difficulty-maximizing writer to Tier-1 writer arms. That turns the competitors into conditions in our study: we audit their writers. Move W2 into Tier 1. Add a second solver family or size. Split T1 by learnability as measured by A*.

(3) Rigor that competitors will lack. Measure rho both pre- and post-band filter, as a causal H3 test that goes beyond the descriptive result in 2611.06965. Report rho disattenuated and as a partial correlation. Tighten TOST to ±3 pp, or use 14 pairs. Keep the manipulation check. A null-tolerant design that frames Alt-1 and Alt-2 as findings means the paper stays strong whatever the sign.

(4) Position against the yoke lineage. Cite 2611.02362 explicitly and say the difference: their yoke is on rewards with weight-trained plasticity, ours is on the curriculum stream with a frozen-weight harness learner.

(5) A cheap Tier-3 tau2-bench cell gives realism that no competitor in the harness space has with exact DB-state truth.

(6) Re-check OpenReview revisions of ecw2VvTeE5, 2611.06965, xmpyzuagpu and 6a1CEKvNeC after the ICLR rebuttal (late Dec), and check arXiv in Feb for ICML versions. Adjust the framing if any of them adds a per-category audit. *New finds:* 2611.02362 'Prior Action Control Causally Alters LM Plasticity' (yoked LM twin with matched rewards; must cite, high relevance to T2's yoke claim). 6a1CEKvNeC 'Recursive Harness Self-Improvement for Frontier Reasoning Data Synthesis' (a self-edited writer harness under frozen weights that maximizes difficulty; candidate writer arm, medium-high threat). B8oWwVLeGz 'R_Q-Evolve: LLMs Evolve Their Own Generator Programs' (self-evolved generator programs selected by learnability; weight-trained; medium). d5UGox3RGt 'Task-CoEvolve' (adaptive validation-task selection in harness optimization; gate-side analog; low-medium). kV2zYmLeEp 'ReproSaddler' (follow-up from the ActiveSaddler group; signals a fast-follower; medium). 2611.13144 'Teaching the Harness: Student-Aware Skill Transfer' (low-medium). 2610.38392 'Masked Erosion' (acceptance gates in harness self-improvement; cite for the gate). 2610.36826 'Co-Evolving Rubrics and Harnesses' (low). 2611.08787 'Training Stability under Adaptive Curricula' (UED, low). 2609.33565 'Dr. Free' (difficulty rewards are unnecessary for the proposer; related to Alt-2 and Double Ratchet; low-medium). 2605.22217 'Survive or Collapse: data gating in self-play' (band and gating; cite for H3). 2610.09856 'Self-Evolve With a Reference' (anchored curriculum agent; cite for the anchors arm). st9i6DXgw7 'Fragility of Self-Improving Agents: task order' (cite).

| Judge | P(exec) | Sig | Sound | Clarity | Outcome | Pool | P(acc\|done) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 0.5 | 3 | 3.5 | 3.5 | 2.5 | 3 | 0.27 |
| reviewer | 0.45 | 3 | 3.5 | 3.5 | 2.5 | 3 | 0.27 |
| pi | 0.48 | 3 | 3.5 | 3.5 | 2.5 | 3 | 0.28 |

- **ac** — risk: The likely result is a muddy null. Double Ratchet, ecw2VvTeE5 and UIvgqCcjE2 all predict it. The self-edit arm may also be nearly empty, because improvers rarely edit their own writer. The paper would then report a small or insignificant rho, C sitting inside a loose TOST band, one hand-built 15-rule toy world and one 9B solver. Reviewers will say the weight-side version of this effect is already known and that the setup is too narrow to matter. Building all of it from scratch also risks the yoke and truth-audit tooling not being ready in time, or not being clean enough. · raise P: Recast the paper as a pre-registered audit of whether current weakness-targeting writers (ActiveSaddler, DiagEvo, plus the self-edited A3) still steer away from true failures. Make the learnability split from A* and the pre-/post-filter rho the headline analyses. Add a second solver family and the W2 world in Tier 1, and tighten the equivalence test to ±3 pp with 14 pairs. Then both a positive result and a null give a decisive, general finding instead of a single toy-world mechanism demo.
- **reviewer** — risk: The most likely result is a null or weak finding, and it would be easy to dismiss. Prior work suggests three things: meta-agents rarely use writer access (so the A3 arm may be empty even after forced edits), C is about 0 (in line with Double Ratchet and ecw2VvTeE5), and A*-measured headroom is small in a hand-built 15-rule world. A null like that, from one 9B solver in one toy world, reads to reviewers as "re-measuring a known weight-side effect in a synthetic sandbox". On top of this, the from-scratch build is about 10 person-weeks plus the panel additions, which strains a solo undergrad and threatens how much clean scope gets finished by May. · raise P: Cut the build down and spend the effort on breadth. First, move a second solver family/size and W2 into Tier 1. Second, make ActiveSaddler/DiagEvo writer arms named Tier-1 conditions, and report the learnability-split rho (via A*) and the pre-filter rho as the headline figure. Then even a null becomes a general claim across writers and models: "weakness-targeting writers still fail to cover learnable blind spots under exact truth, and a yoke shows contingency neither helps nor hurts". That gets past the "single toy world, single model" objection, which is the one that most often sinks this kind of measurement paper.
- **pi** — risk: The likely result is a null or an ambiguous one. The self-edit arm may stay nearly empty (meta-agents rarely edit their own writer), T2 may land inside a loose TOST bound, as Double Ratchet and UIvgqCcjE2 predict, and a negative rho can be explained away as rational triage or a side effect of the band filter. That leaves a single toy world with one 9B solver that reviewers will call a re-measurement of a mechanism ecw2VvTeE5 and 2611.06965 already published. On top of that, a solo undergrad has 8-10+ person-weeks of from-scratch build (the world, yoke replay, the improver, extra writer arms, a second solver) before any confirmatory run. · raise P: Cut the scope so the core is clean and reaches broad external validity. Make the ActiveSaddler and DiagEvo weakness-targeting writers Tier-1 arms. Report rho stratified by A*-measured learnability, on both pre- and post-filter output. Run 14 yoke pairs with ±3 pp TOST, and replicate the A3-vs-Yk contrast on a second solver family and on W2. Frame the headline as "even blind-spot-targeting writers steer away from learnable failures (signed rho against exact truth), and a learner-contingent yoke isolates the harm." Pass the pilot manipulation check before December so the forced-edit pivot is not needed.

### Parametric Gödel Agent: Does Modular Weight Self-Modification Compound, or Does Self-Training Decay Survive Inside the Module? (parametric-godel; FINAL_REPORT #4, panel 68.6, spread 1.3) (`parametric-godel`) — EV 9.3%

**Claim.** Run 5-8 rounds of self-generated, verified-correction LoRA updates on 3-8B models. Capability-specific adapters remove cross-capability interference but not the decay itself. Refreshing an existing adapter on its own corrections should reproduce Third Eye's negative continuation utility (-1.78 pp at H2) inside that module. Compounding comes only from inserting new adapters trained on verified data, and it stalls once routing over the growing library degrades. If refresh does not decay, the alternative headline is "modularity rescues self-training: Third Eye's decay is interference, not collapse." The judges' framing is that either sign is publishable. Lead with the measurement (decomposing multi-round self-update decay) and use modularity as the intervention, not the claimed win.

**Design.** Latest version: the round-1 critique-sharpened design as carried into FINAL_REPORT. No proposal or v2 was ever written. Models: Qwen3-4B, plus Llama-3.2-3B as a second family (Third Eye found Llama the most decay-prone). Updates are Third Eye scale: rank-16 LoRA, about 50 steps per round, 5-8 rounds. The task stream drifts across up to 4 verifiable capabilities: GSM8K/MATH, MBPP/HumanEval, BIRD text-to-SQL and BFCL tool calls. Arms: insert a new adapter each round / refresh the same capability adapter / shared LoRA / no-update HOLD. These are crossed with self-generated vs gold/teacher data, which separates cross-capability interference, within-module self-training degradation and data-source collapse. Readouts: Third Eye paired G1/G_{t+1} endpoints with a continuation-utility curve; P/S/I/F metrics from 'Are Self-Evolving Agents Continual Learners?'; and a fresh-adapter probe on a held-out capability (it must be renamed, since Jjh60xpFVY owns "agent plasticity"). Controls: capacity-matched shared LoRA (rank grows with K), random-partition adapters, an oracle router, and scripted vs agent-decided train/insert/refresh. Power: at least 8 seeds per arm, a pre-fixed selection/scoring split, simultaneous intervals. The harness-only arm is dropped. Cost: about $2-4k GPU for the full grid (round 1 said $2-5k), after a pilot under $100. The AC's minimal pre-registered core is refresh vs insert vs HOLD on self data, 3 capabilities, 5 rounds, 8+ seeds, 2 families. Gold-data, oracle-router and agent-decided arms become secondary.

**Build load.** Must be built from scratch: (1) a multi-round self-correction loop (generate, verify, LoRA train) for each capability; (2) an adapter library with insert/refresh/shared operations and a router (an oracle-router variant plus capacity-matched shared-LoRA plumbing); (3) a Third Eye-style paired-endpoint evaluation harness across 4 benchmarks, including agentic BIRD/BFCL scoring every round; (4) P/S/I/F stream metrics; (5) an optional agent-decided action policy (WeAct-style tool-call adapter switching). Reuse could cut this sharply: WeAct (the mentor group's own code) has the adapter action space, and Third Eye has the protocol. Estimate: about 3-4 person-weeks to reach the pilot, if WeAct infrastructure is shared; about 6-8 if built alone. Then about 1,000 train+eval cycles (mentor's estimate) for the full grid, which needs lab GPUs. GPU access is unconfirmed. · **Pilot.** Before any code, ask Xunjian whether the WeAct group plans a multi-round sequel (then join it or split the space) and confirm GPU access. Pilot: 2 weeks, under $100. Qwen3-4B, 2 capabilities, refresh vs HOLD, horizon 3 (H1->H3), 8 seeds. Go only if refresh decay reproduces Third Eye's continuation loss outside the CI. The pilot seed SD also supplies the power analysis for the main grid. Add BIRD/BFCL only if the pilot shows a separable effect. Log the difficulty distribution of the correction data from the start, to test the ecw2VvTeE5 alternative.

**Closest work.** Exact delta vs each closest work:
- WeAct 2610.26847 (mentor's own paper, Shi, Yin et al.; high threat). It has independent capability LoRAs plus adapter switching as an action, one offline-SFT sequence, one insert and one refresh. Fig. 3 shows shared LoRA loses 10-49% and capability adapters lose 0% by construction. Delta: self-generated data, a multi-round loop, repeated refresh, and a measured decay decomposition. Its Limitations section names library growth, routing and repeated refinement as open.
- Third Eye 2611.12988 (high threat). It covers 5 backbones, a single shared LoRA and horizon 2 only, with decay of -1.78 pp [0.68, 2.88] that is mostly target/OOD loss. It offers no remedy. Delta: modular refresh vs insert, horizon 5-8, self vs gold data. A Third Eye "remedy" follow-up is a scoop vector.
- Macaron-V1 2608.09819 (medium). Mixture-of-LoRA plus an RSI loop, but with no budget-matched single-LoRA comparison. Mind Lab is the second-likeliest scooper.
- Are Self-Evolving Agents Continual Learners? 2610.25858 (medium). The harness/memory-only analogue, which calls for isolating updates. This idea is its parametric counterpart.
- SLAaaT 2608.17034 (low). Adapter switching as a tool over a static library.
- CoEvo-RSI 2610.25078, also an ICLR27 submission (medium). About 10 rounds of full-weight SFT, no modules.
- Control Map 2610.36109 (foundation).
- Gödel Agent 2410.04444 (foundation, the source of the name).
- ICLR27 neighbours: FPaMYO47j4 (Harness Evolution Hits a Ceiling, LoRA internalising harness gains); ecw2VvTeE5 (weight self-training gains concentrate on already-solved items, a competing explanation for refresh decay); IkYmTAyPXZ (capability erosion under RSI); T04ZzT5m6j (negative-drift theory); LRSI 71wzcahKcb (continuation interference at the harness level); Jjh60xpFVY (owns "agent plasticity"); EOrtreQLbv RSI-Harness; 53Ak0jvesg and the other 12+ weight-harness co-evolution methods (the "saturated lane").
- Others: 2609.36535† (self-evolution degeneration), 2611.09701 (where should learning live), CaLLM 2610.36994 (LoRA routing for continual learning).
- No direct scoop found. However, the Nov 12 sweep never queried LoRA, continual-self-training or mixture-of-LoRA terms, so "no hit" is weak evidence.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.62).** No direct scoop in ICLR27 OpenReview or alphaXiv as of 2026-11-13. These searches did cover the LoRA, self-training, mixture-of-LoRA, adapter-library and refresh terms that the Nov 12 sweep missed, so "no hit" is now moderate evidence, not weak.

Closest new neighbour: if9rcHknOM, "Separating Training-Data Source from Update Rate in Verified Self-Training" (ICLR27). It runs Llama-3.1-8B for 40 rounds on GSM8K, with a crossed generator/receiver design and 3 seeds. Every run loses held-out accuracy, and the paper attributes this to a data-source component that tuning the update rate does not remove. That is the same "decompose multi-round self-update decay" framing, but with one capability, shared weights, no modules and no refresh/insert arms. It is both a competitor on the measurement lead and a citable precedent: their finding predicts that refresh decays. The authors already have a 40-round pipeline and could add an adapter arm for an ICML27 resubmission or a NeurIPS27 extension. I rate this the biggest new threat (medium-high).

Other new neighbours:
- TRACE KfjmQmylyp: per-capability LoRA trained by RL, plus MoE over the adapters. Single pass, no multi-round decay.
- HdQypP8A9q, "Two-Resource View of Adapter-Library Arbitration": routing over adapter libraries degrades with fewer router labels. This is relevant to the claim that routing stalls.
- QI800NwjXo: skill library consolidated into LoRA.
- 50kJuTajGS ReSAIL (2609.39306): collapse in iterative agent self-distillation, plus a mitigation.
- eFFBAZykXZ TMEM: online fast-weight LoRA.
- 2611.11684 TILA: task-isolated adapters plus selection.
- CaLLM 2610.36994 and many continual-LoRA merging papers. These are the CL lane, not the self-training lane.

Most likely to publish close work by May 2027:
1. A Third Eye follow-up with a "remedy" (LoRA isolation or replay) at horizon 3-5. Probably shared-vs-modular at 1-2 models and few seeds; good, but narrower.
2. The if9rcHknOM group adding a modular or adapter arm. Rigorous, but single-task.
3. Mind Lab / Macaron-V2: a mixture-of-LoRA RSI system paper. Strong engineering, weak causal decomposition, no budget-matched controls.
4. WeAct follow-up on repeated refinement and library growth. Its Limitations section names these as open, but it is the mentor's group, so likely a collaborator or coordinated work, not a scooper.
5. ICML27 submissions (visible around Feb) from the 12+ weight-harness co-evolution groups. These are mostly methods papers that add adapters as a component, without a decay decomposition.

Most of these will do one of two things: offer modularity as a win without the refresh-vs-insert-vs-HOLD × self-vs-gold cross, or measure decay without the modular intervention. A paper doing both, with 8+ seeds, 2 families and simultaneous intervals, is plausible but unlikely (about 25-35%) before mid-May 2027. *Stay ahead:* 1. Position against if9rcHknOM explicitly. Their result (data-source forgetting survives update-rate tuning, in one task with shared weights) is our "within-module self-training degradation vs data-source collapse" cell. Replicate their direction in the refresh arm, then show what modularity adds: interference is removed but decay remains, or the reverse. Cite them as the shared-weight baseline, and consider adopting their crossed frozen-data receiver trick as a cheap extra control.
2. Run the AC's minimal pre-registered core first and post it on arXiv early, ideally by Feb-Mar 2027. The core is refresh vs insert vs HOLD on self data, 3 capabilities, 5 rounds, 8+ seeds, 2 families. An early timestamp blunts ICML27 fast-followers and Third Eye remedy papers, and it does not need to be first if it is the most complete version.
3. Make routing degradation quantitative: library size K against oracle-router gap. Cite HdQypP8A9q. This is the piece that WeAct, TRACE and Macaron all lack.
4. Keep the gold-data cross and the capacity-matched shared LoRA (rank grows with K). These are the controls that system papers like Macaron and a Third Eye follow-up are least likely to run, and they make our version the clearly more rigorous one.
5. Extend the horizon to 8 rounds on at least a subset, to beat Third Eye's horizon of 2 and any H3-5 follow-up.
6. Re-check OpenReview ICLR27 revisions in late Nov and Jan, and ICML27 arXiv postings in Feb. Watch for "LoRA" or "adapter" being added to Third Eye, if9rcHknOM, ReSAIL and Macaron revisions.
7. Coordinate with the WeAct authors (the mentor's group) so the follow-up is ours, not parallel work. *New finds:* These are new relative to the prior list.
- if9rcHknOM (ICLR27): Separating Training-Data Source from Update Rate in Verified Self-Training. 40 rounds, Llama-3.1-8B, GSM8K, crossed data/update design. Closest new neighbour on the measurement side.
- KfjmQmylyp (ICLR27): TRACE, capability-targeted LoRA adapters plus MoE.
- HdQypP8A9q (ICLR27): A Two-Resource View of Adapter-Library Arbitration (routing limits).
- QI800NwjXo (ICLR27): Consolidating the Skill Library of a Self-Improving LLM Agent into LoRA.
- 50kJuTajGS / 2609.39306: ReSAIL, collapse in iterative agent self-distillation.
- eFFBAZykXZ (ICLR27): TMEM, parametric fast-weight LoRA memory.
- sCJWZK42vA (ICLR27): TITRATE, skill refresh in self-distillation.
- C3v0zhagPA (ICLR27): Limits of On-Policy Self-Distillation for Continual Post-Training.
- syDKY4t8SW (ICLR27): Model Collapse Needs Only One Generation.

- 2611.11684: TILA, task-isolated latent adapters.
- 2610.32770: On-Policy Self-Training (Google).
- 2610.31401: HERD.
- 2609.18779: CERA-MoA.
