### Modularly Varying Goals for Meta-Harnesses: Editable Improvers Pay Only Under Real API Drift (openness-varying-goals-drift, round 3, panel 70.1, non-obvious, scoop: medium-risk) (`varying-goals-drift`) — EV 9.0%

**Claim.** This borrows Kashtan & Alon's (2005) modularly-varying-goals result from biology. The claim: on static streams, an editable improver (L4) has about zero or negative held-out advantage over a fixed improver (L3). The advantage turns positive, and improver self-edit rates rise, when the task stream switches between real library-version regimes on a schedule with period P. The advantage peaks at an intermediate P* close to the improver's adaptation time tau, which can be measured from L3 recovery curves. Stronger optimizers should have a smaller P*. Every judge said the stated law, Adv(P) ≈ a·(1/P)(1−e^{−P/tau}) − c, has to be re-derived, because as written it falls monotonically and cannot produce the claimed peak. The fallback results still count: (a) improver-edit rate as a dose-response over P, and (b) a strong negative (L4 gains nothing even under drift) that settles the Hyperagents vs Improvement-by-Inheritance dispute as a property of the environment.

**Design.** DriftStream is a continual benchmark of executable coding tasks with hidden tests, each run in a Docker image pinned to one library version. It is built from real breaking changes:
- original plan: pandas 1→2, NumPy 1→2, pydantic v1→v2, SQLAlchemy 1.4→2.0, sklearn deprecations;
- judges' cut: 2 libraries, pandas plus a less-memorized one (polars or recent pydantic/FastAPI minors).

Tasks are seeded from GitChameleon, VersiCode, CodeUpdateArena and SkillShift, plus about 100-120 new tasks (down from 300) verified against changelogs, with explicit handling of stale tests that call removed APIs.

The loop is LadderLoop on Qwen3.5-27B, ideally served from a cheap hosted endpoint. The regime switches every P epochs, and switches recombine sub-goals. P values: Mix (the P→0 point from 2610.25858), 1, 2, 3, 5, 10 and ∞.

Arms:
- L3 fixed improver;
- L4 editable improver;
- forced-improver-edit L4;
- matched-token memory-only (ACE-style);
- L3 plus a version-check prompt line.

Each cell gets 5-6 lineages at matched tokens, with L4's meta-edit tokens counted against its budget and tool access (for example changelog lookup) matched across arms.

Metrics:
- 2610.25858's P/S/I/F;
- held-out score on a never-seen post-cutoff regime plus unseen task families;
- epochs to recover 90% of the pre-switch score;
- improver-edit rate, with edits coded as meta-level ('check installed version') vs task patches, using 2611.03219's taxonomy.

Analysis is a mixed model of arm × P. The pre-registered contrast is that the L4−L3 gap at P=3 exceeds the gap at P=∞. The inverted U is compared against monotone and saturating fits by AIC or LOO. tau is fitted from L3 first. P* is then predicted out of sample for a second model or optimizer strength.

Exposure confounds (number of switches, distinct regime-epochs) are matched or entered as covariates. Both switch directions are tested because of memorization asymmetry.

Cost: pilot about $45. Full run well within $3k only at the reduced scope.

**Build load.** Everything is built from scratch:
- DriftStream: version-pinned Docker images per regime, about 100-120 newly written and verified hidden-test tasks, and adapters for GitChameleon, VersiCode and SkillShift items with stale-test handling (about 4-5 person-weeks);
- an LadderLoop extension with L3/L4/forced-edit/memory arms and stream scheduling (about 2 weeks);
- an edit-content coding pipeline (about 1 week);
- the analysis and law derivation, a two-timescale tracking model (about 1-2 weeks).

That is roughly 9-11 person-weeks before compute. The grid at the cut scope is 4-5 arms × 5-7 P values × 5 lineages, about 100-175 multi-epoch 27B runs, plus a second model for the P* test. The panel's common view: original scope (5 libraries, 300 tasks, 72+ runs) is too much for one undergrad in 6 months, and the cut scope is tight but doable. · **Pilot.** Week 1, about $45. Build a 2-regime mini-stream (pandas 1.5 ↔ 2.2, 60 hidden-test tasks; the judges prefer polars or pydantic minors from the start). Run L3 vs L4 × 3 lineages at P=3 and P=∞ for 9 epochs.

GO requires all three:
- (i) the unadapted harness loses at least 15 points at each switch, so real drift exists and the model does not absorb it trivially;
- (ii) both arms partly recover within 3 epochs;
- (iii) the L4 improver-edit rate at P=3 is above that at P=∞, or the L4−L3 gap differs between P conditions by more than 1 seed-SD.

Expert-judge addition: show that L3, editing only the task harness, cannot close the gap. Otherwise drift does not select for improver-level change.

KILL or PIVOT:
- switch drop under 5 points (model priors cover both versions): move to less-memorized libraries or realistic synthetic API forks with a memorization check;
- near-zero L4 improver edits at every P (empty arm): fall back to the forced-edit arm.

**Closest work.** 2610.25858 'Are Self-Evolving Agents Continual Learners?' (Mila/GDM, Oct 2026) is the closest neighbour. It runs ReasoningBank, GEPA and Meta-Harness on piecewise drifting streams with P/S/I/F metrics, and owns the "stability-plasticity vs editable surface" framing. Delta: no switching-period sweep, no fixed-vs-editable improver arm, no library-version drift, no evolvability law.

Other neighbours:
- 2611.03219 (Tsinghua): continual coding-harness evolution and forgetting. Only 2 stages, task-type drift, no P manipulation, no improver editing.
- SkillShift (IJDSML, Oct 2026): 27 real dated library or policy change events with version-pinned execution. No self-improvement loop or schedule. This weakens 'first grounded API-drift continual benchmark' as a contribution.
- SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797 and Improvement by Inheritance (ZMmJDeDTXk): static streams where improvers almost never self-edit. They supply the predicted P=∞ null and the empty-arm threat.
- Harness Continual Learning dVv1MBkavP (ICLR27 sub): guarded continual harness evolution. No drift-rate manipulation or improver comparison.
- EvoSpace MQoRwlss2y and DyScope MXINrso5QC (adaptive scope, no drift manipulation); Governing Self-Improvement Across Timescales c3lHKtsSMq.
- GitChameleon, CodeUpdateArena, VersiCode, LibEvoBench: static, so seed data only.
- Kashtan-Alon 2005, 2103.06435, 1612.05955: biological and ML-theory precedent that sets the framing, not a scoop.

What survives: no paper varies switching period P as the moderator of the L4-vs-L3 gap or tests an evolvability-style inverted-U for meta-harness openness. Position the work as an extension of 2610.25858 along the switching-rate axis, with DriftStream as an enabling contribution only.

Novelty note for the user's re-weighting: the core is incremental over 2610.25858, but the delta (P as moderator, improver arm, real API drift) is cleaner and less contested than that of most other round-3 "novel" ideas.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.58).** I ran 8 searches: ICLR 2027 OpenReview with about 12 query terms, alphaXiv twice with recency priority, and Semantic Scholar, which returned nothing usable. Full ICLR abstracts for RSI-Harness and Improvement by Inheritance were blocked by an OpenReview challenge, so I judged those two from search snippets only. No paper today sweeps the switching period P as the moderator of the editable-vs-fixed improver gap. The competitors below are ordered by how likely they are to produce a close version by mid-May 2027.

(1) 2610.25858, "Are Self-Evolving Agents Continual Learners?" (Mila/GDM, 70 votes). This is the most natural fast-follower. They already have piecewise drifting streams and P/S/I/F metrics, so adding a period sweep is a cheap v2 or ICML-workshop extension. I put the chance they post one by May at about 30-35%. If they do, they will run on broad agent tasks with ReasoningBank, GEPA and Meta-Harness. They are unlikely to include a fixed-vs-editable improver arm or real version-pinned API drift.

(2) RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv and Improvement by Inheritance ZMmJDeDTXk (ICLR27). Both have L3/L4-style improver machinery and both report that improvers rarely edit themselves on static streams. Reviewers are very likely to ask "does improver editing pay under nonstationarity?", so a rebuttal or camera-ready ablation with a single drift condition is plausible, about 35-40%. That ablation would be one non-swept condition on synthetic or task-type drift, not a dose-response over P. It could still take the headline "L4 helps under drift" and weaken our negative-result fallback.

(3) Agent Plasticity, Jjh60xpFVY / 2610.08902 (Berkeley/Meta MSL), and 2611.03219, Can Coding Agents Sustain Continual Evolution without Forgetting? (Tsinghua). Both have continual coding infrastructure and could add multi-stage drift. Only a moderate overlap is likely, about 20%.

(4) The crowded harness-continual space: Harness Continual Learning dVv1MBkavP, Recurrent Harness 2LFnFGV58m, EvoRule ZvTJOV7xQk, HarnessWeaver XYYTrWtce8, AgentStream GGUmZybpbd, SkillFlow j2RMOsmge4, EvoHarnessBench tNNCBiSaC9, and SWE-Shift 2610.32196 (evolving workspaces and dependencies). These raise reviewers' "yet another continual harness benchmark" fatigue, but none manipulates drift rate or compares improvers. Expect 2-4 more like them in the ICML-2027 wave around February. Most will be method papers, not controlled-moderator studies.

Overall: there is perhaps a 50-60% chance that some paper reports "editable improver vs fixed improver under drift" by May. It is much less likely, about 15-20%, that anyone does a P sweep with a fitted tau/P* and real library-version regimes. Our version wins on rigour if the inverted-U or dose-response actually shows up. If only the null result appears, a single-condition ablation from (2) makes it look less distinctive. *Stay ahead:* 1. Lock the parts that are hard to copy: the dose-response over P (at least 5 values plus Mix and infinity) and tau fitted from L3 recovery curves, then P* predicted out of sample on a second model or optimizer. A one-condition rebuttal ablation from RSI-Harness or Improvement by Inheritance cannot match that.
2. Fix the law early. Use something like Adv(P) = a*(1 - e^{-P/tau}) * g(switch frequency) - c, so it can actually produce a peak, and pre-register it on OSF or arXiv by around February, before the ICML wave posts.
3. Put out a short arXiv preprint with the pilot by about March 2027. It should show the improver-edit-rate dose-response and the static-stream null, which plants the "P as moderator" claim before the 2610.25858 authors' likely v2.
4. Make DriftStream reusable: Docker-pinned pandas plus polars or pydantic, with stale-test handling. Release it as a drop-in for the Meta-Harness, GEPA and RSI-Harness codebases, so that competitors cite it rather than build their own.
5. Run both switch directions and match exposure, the two confounds a reviewer would use to say a follower did it better.
6. Frame the paper as resolving Hyperagents vs Improvement by Inheritance as a property of the environment. Both outcomes then count as a contribution, which protects the fallback result if a competitor publishes "L4 helps under drift" first.
7. Re-run these OpenReview searches after ICLR rebuttals (around late Nov or Dec) and after the ICML arXiv wave (Feb-Mar), focused on RSI-Harness and Improvement by Inheritance revisions. *New finds:* These were not in the original neighbour list: RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv (ICLR27, optimizing the harness optimizer, closest on the improver arm); Agent Plasticity Jjh60xpFVY / 2610.08902 (Berkeley/Meta); AgentStream GGUmZybpbd; EvoHarnessBench tNNCBiSaC9; SkillFlow j2RMOsmge4; Recurrent Harness for Agentic Continual Learning 2LFnFGV58m; EvoRule Harness ZvTJOV7xQk; HarnessWeaver XYYTrWtce8; SelfLineage nVl2qz74V7; Lyapunov-Gated RSI 3t0j2Y05EH; SWE-Shift 2610.32196 (evolving workspaces and dependencies, no self-improvement loop); How Do Coding-Agent Harnesses Self-Evolve? 2610.34938; HarnessPro 2610.26770; Beneath the Surface 2611.07624; RSI-Env 2611.01736; BONSAI evolvability-guided search 2608.07056 (evolvability terminology only). None of these sweeps the switching period or tests an inverted-U in the improver advantage.

### Sharing Starves the Meta-Level: Population-Genetic Modifier Theory Predicts That Cross-Lineage Edit Sharing Suppresses Improver Self-Edits (round3 selfupdate-modifier-hitchhiking, panel mean 66.0, flagged non-obvious, scoop risk medium) (`modifier-hitchhiking`) — EV 9.0%

**Claim.** In a self-improving population with several lineages, sharing accepted harness edits across lineages at a higher rate r lowers how often improver edits are retained, and lowers the final improver's successor quality, monotonically in r. Crediting shared edits back to the improver that produced them (clade credit) restores both. The mechanism comes from modifier theory: improver edits act as modifiers, are selected only by hitchhiking on the harness edits they produce, and sharing works like recombination that breaks that linkage. The law is delta p ~ p(1-p)[s_ind*h*(1-r) - c]. It predicts a critical sharing rate r* = 1 - c/(s_ind*h), above which improver edits are purged. This would explain MetaShare's 0/24 self-modification records. Alternative outcome: if sharing has no effect on retention, improver edits survive through their direct effects. That is still a publishable, clean negative that rules out a linkage worry for MetaShare- and Looped-RSI-style systems.

**Design.** Testbed: a GodelLite population of 8 lineages, each with its own editable improver and harness. Each generation, every accepted harness edit is copied into other lineages with probability r in {0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0}. Acceptance is gated per lineage on validation held out from search. A 5th arm runs r=1 with clade credit: the improver-edit selection score includes the gains its shared edits produce in other lineages (CMP-style). As written: 5 arms x 5 populations (seeds) x 15 generations, Qwen3-14B in both roles, a Qwen3-32B replication at r in {0, 1}, compute matched per population. Metrics: retained improver-edit frequency trajectory; a Price-equation split of selection on improver edits into direct and indirect (product-linked) parts; nested-replay successor quality on held-out tasks; population-best held-out score. Preregistered tests: a Jonckheere-Terpstra trend in r and the clade-credit vs r=1 contrast. Cost about $800. All three judges asked for the same revision: (a) an explicit between-lineage selection step (Moran or tournament replacement on population-relative held-out fitness), with a per-lineage-gating-only control arm; (b) a fixed improver-proposal quota (25-30%) plus seeded improver variants with known s_ind, and per-proposal retention probability as the dependent variable; (c) s_ind, h and c estimated from the r=0 arm and a cost-manipulation arm, with r* preregistered before the high-r arms run; (d) a provenance-stripped sharing arm with matched edit counts; (e) about 8 populations per arm at r in {0, 0.5, 1} (the ac judge instead wants at least 5 r levels to tell a threshold apart from a linear decline); (f) a second model family and a second task family. These changes push cost above $800.

**Build load.** To build: a multi-lineage GodelLite population harness with the improver and harness editable separately; a sharing/copy operator with rate r and provenance tracking; a clade-credit attribution pass; a between-lineage replacement step (Moran or tournament); a forced-quota and seeded-improver mechanism; a nested-replay successor-quality assay; Price-equation decomposition code; and a simulation-based power analysis run before spending. Single-lineage GodelLite pieces may already exist in the lab pipeline, but the population layer, sharing, and attribution are new. Estimate: about 5-7 person-weeks for one student, plus 1-2 weeks for the second-family replication and analysis (my estimate; the sources give no figure). · **Pilot.** Pilot: r in {0, 1.0} plus clade credit, 3 populations x 4 lineages x 8 generations, Qwen3-14B on the code family, about $40. GO if retained improver-edit frequency at r=0 is at least 2x the r=1 value, or if the Price decomposition shows a non-zero indirect component at r=0. KILL or PIVOT if improver edits are essentially absent in every arm (base rate too low to measure). In that case, rerun on top of the forced-quota arm (q=0.25) so proposals exist and only retention is studied. Per the judges, the pilot should also supply variance estimates for s_ind and c to size the full run, so the r* confidence interval does not cover [0,1].

**Closest work.** MetaShare 2610.21887 (NeurIPS26 workshop): cross-lineage sharing with an editable meta-agent, and 0 self_modification records. It only ablates the pool on/off, has no sharing-rate dose, and never links sharing to absent self-modification. That makes it a data point this idea explains, not a scoop. Looped-RSI (OpenReview sBH9cdajAw, ICLR27 sub): credits improvers by the quality of their descendants, which is essentially the clade-credit rescue arm already published. HGM 2510.21614: clade metaproductivity in a single tree, no sharing axis; about 70 forward citations, none manipulate sharing. Solo-to-Social 2609.38516: at the object level, copying starves independent exploration (producer-scrounger); this is the competing mechanism to rule out. Wright-GM cQkSamYGQi and Mendel-GM co3NCcIZ7f (ICLR27 subs): the pop-gen lens and "sharing as recombination" are already taken. View from Nowhere HpnuHDtvwJ: a high-sharing method that could serve as an audit target. Classical: mutator hitchhiking, the Feldman reduction principle, Group Elite Selection 2204.04817, 2603.02496. Together these make the theory non-novel. Improvement by Inheritance ZMmJDeDTXk / SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797: about 0% of retained edits touch the improver even without sharing, which is the floor-effect threat. What is new: the first manipulated-dose test of a quantitative linkage prediction (r*) for meta-edit retention in LLM self-improvement, with a Price-equation split. You noted the paper does not need to be entirely novel, only more novel than the alternatives; this is a new empirical test, not a new theory or method, but no concurrent paper runs this manipulation.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.62).** I searched ICLR 2027 OpenReview (10 queries) and alphaXiv with recency priority. None of the hits manipulates a cross-lineage sharing rate and measures whether meta/improver edits are retained. The closest work, ranked:
(1) MetaShare 2610.21887 (PwC). Has sharing plus an editable meta-agent, but only a pool on/off ablation. A follow-up adding a sharing-rate dose is the single most likely direct scoop. It is a small group, and a workshop paper often gets extended for ICML 2027 (around Feb) or NeurIPS. I put P(they add a sharing dose by May) at about 15%, and if they do, it will probably lack the theory or the Price split.
(2) Gödel Ecology N4GJVmrzsF (ICLR27, new find). "Collective RSI" that scores evaluators by Co-Clade Metaproductivity across agent lineages. It sits next to our clade-credit arm and could pick up a lineage-interaction analysis during the ICLR revision, but it is a methods paper with nothing on sharing as a dose.
(3) Looped-RSI sBH9cdajAw. Already holds descendant-credit, which is our rescue arm. A revision could add a sharing ablation, low probability.
(4) Wright-GM cQkSamYGQi and Mendel-GM co3NCcIZ7f / 2608.07645. Already use the pop-gen and recombination framing. Fast-followers from these groups could write a "recombination breaks meta-edit linkage" analysis, about 10-15%.
(5) Fast-followers with infrastructure:
- Improvement by Inheritance ZMmJDeDTXk and SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797: they already measure improver-edit retention near 0%, so adding a multi-lineage arm would be cheap for them.
- RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv.
- View from Nowhere 2610.36660 / HpnuHDtvwJ: a population-centered method.
- DarwinX 2608.07545 and EvolveNet 2608.04968: multi-lineage harness evolution, where a sharing ablation is a natural add-on.
- SelfLineage nVl2qz74V7: tracks the descendant consequences of self-mods, single lineage.
Overall, I put P(some paper runs a manipulated sharing dose on meta-edit retention by mid-May 2027) at about 25-30%. If one appears, it will most likely be one or two r levels inside a methods paper, without a quantitative r* prediction, provenance-stripped controls, or a direct vs indirect split. The bigger threat to being the better version is our own execution: the floor effect, where improver edits are about 0% retained even at r=0, would make the dose response untestable. Novelty was downweighted as you asked. Being first is not required, so the score mostly reflects whether we can run the revised design well. *Stay ahead:* 1. Build in the panel's revisions, because they are what makes ours the clearly better version:
- a fixed improver-proposal quota and seeded improver variants with known s_ind, which removes the floor effect;
- an explicit between-lineage selection step (Moran or tournament) plus a per-lineage-gating-only control;
- at least 5 r levels to tell a threshold from a linear decline;
- r* preregistered from parameters estimated in the r=0 arm and a cost-manipulation arm;
- a provenance-stripped sharing arm with matched edit counts, which separates the linkage mechanism from the producer-scrounger mechanism in Solo-to-Social;
- a second model family and a second task family.
2. Post the preregistration (OSF or arXiv) by Jan-Feb 2027, so an ICML-cycle competitor can't claim the r* prediction first.
3. Audit existing systems (MetaShare, View from Nowhere, Gödel Ecology, DarwinX) using their released logs or code. That turns competitors into supporting data points.
4. Frame clade credit as a test of the Looped-RSI and Gödel Ecology credit schemes under sharing, not as a new method.
5. Watch the ICLR27 revisions of Looped-RSI, Gödel Ecology, Wright-GM and Mendel-GM, plus any MetaShare v2, and re-check in Feb 2027.
6. Report the null cleanly if it comes out that way. A well-powered negative still beats a one-ablation positive in a methods paper. *New finds:* These are new finds, not on the original closest-work list. Ones marked new-adjacent are closest to our idea; the rest are lower relevance.
- N4GJVmrzsF (Gödel Ecology, ICLR27): co-clade metaproductivity across lineages. New-adjacent.
- nVl2qz74V7 (SelfLineage, ICLR27): descendant-aware history of self-modification. New-adjacent.
- 2610.38392 (Masked Erosion): acceptance gates lose redundant safeguards. Related gate-selection dynamics.
- 2608.24735 (Meta^n: emergent meta-depth).
- 2608.07545 (DarwinX: multi-lineage harness natural selection).
- 2608.04968 (EvolveNet: collaborative harness evolution).
- 2610.30042 / 6YRJH50sAy (HGM-Dual).
- 9UsBcc69Ua (Measured Behavior, Recorded Zero: measurement failures in self-improving agents). Relevant to MetaShare's 0-record interpretation.
- 75PPLZgaUM (RRSI).
- 9gq20okZa3 (When Sharing Hurts: preregistered refutation of hierarchical credit sharing). Tangential.

- **ac** — risk: The floor effect combined with the tautology critique. Improver edits are retained about 0% of the time even with no sharing (SelfImprove-Bench, Improvement by Inheritance). That forces a quota or seeded-variant design, which reviewers will call artificial. And if the system has no real between-lineage selection, the (1-r) suppression follows from the setup by construction, so the dose response reads as accounting, not a finding. On top of that, with 8 lineages and only a few seeds, the s_ind, h and c estimates are noisy, so the r* confidence interval probably cannot be tight enough to make the quantitative prediction land. The build is also heavy for one undergrad: a population layer, Moran selection, provenance stripping, nested replay, and a second model and task family. · raise P: Before the high-r arms run, preregister r* using s_ind, h and c estimated from r=0 plus a cost-manipulation arm. Then show that the observed retention collapse matches that out-of-sample prediction within a tight CI, at 5 or more r levels, under explicit Moran or tournament between-lineage selection. Pair this with a derived pure-accounting null that the LLM system measurably departs from, and replicate on a second model family. That turns an argument from borrowed theory into a validated quantitative prediction, which is what moved the expert judge to weak accept.
- **reviewer** — risk: Floor effect plus the tautology charge. Natural improver-edit retention is about 0% even at r=0, so the dose response may not be measurable. The forced-quota fix changes the system being studied, and the effect could then look like an accounting identity (at r=1 lineage selection cannot tell improvers apart by construction) rather than an empirical finding. With 8 lineages and 5-8 seeds, the Price covariances and the r* confidence interval will probably be too noisy to count as a tight out-of-sample prediction. Busy reviewers will read the result as "the Feldman reduction principle re-derived in a toy LLM setting, with HGM/Looped-RSI clade credit renamed", using only Qwen on the code family. · raise P: Preregister r* from parameters estimated independently: s_ind from seeded improver variants with known effects, c from a cost-manipulation arm, and h from the r=0 arm. Then show that the observed threshold across 5 or more r levels matches that out-of-sample prediction, under explicit between-lineage tournament selection. Also show it departs from a derived pure-accounting baseline, with a provenance-stripped, count-matched control to rule out scrounging. A tight, non-trivial quantitative prediction confirmed on two model families is what turns this from a measurement-only or tautology paper into an accept.
- **pi** — risk: The floor effect combined with tautology. Natural improver-edit retention is about 0% even at r=0, so the dose response may not be testable. The forced-quota fix changes the system being studied, and without real between-lineage selection, reviewers can call any r effect an accounting identity (the Feldman reduction principle re-derived in a toy setting). With 8 lineages and noisy LLM fitness, the Price covariances and the r* confidence interval will probably be uninformative. The revised design (between-lineage selection, seeded improvers, cost arm, provenance-stripped arm, 5 r levels x 8 populations, a second model family and a second task family) is a large load for one undergrad by May. · raise P: Preregister an out-of-sample r* prediction. First estimate s_ind, h and c in the r=0 arm and the cost-manipulation arm, using seeded improver variants with known, non-trivial direct effects under explicit Moran or tournament between-lineage selection. Then show that the high-r arms hit the predicted threshold, with a tight confidence interval, on two model families. Run a simulation-based power analysis and keep the arms only if the pilot shows a measurable improver-edit base rate. This turns "accounting identity" into "quantitative theory confirmed in LLM systems".

### When the Grader Moves: Real Verifier-Commit Drift Is What Makes Self-Improvers Rewrite Their Acceptance Gate (reality-verifier-drift-gate, round 3, panel mean 66.7, rank 7; folded into CauseShift as its D_eval/L5 cells) (`verifier-drift-gate`) — EV 8.9%

**Claim.** Replay real verifier and benchmark-harness commits (mined via BenchPulse) while the task pool stays frozen. The prediction is that this raises the share of retained edits that touch a Godel-lite agent's own acceptance gate or improver (S4/S5) from about 0% to at least 15%, and that a gate-editable arm keeps at least 50% fewer stale accepted edits than a frozen-gate arm. Stale edits are scored on held-out tasks under the post-commit verifier. A secondary law, pre-registered but not the headline: retained stale edits decay as exp(-t/tau) after each commit, with tau at least 2x shorter when the gate is editable, and tau shrinks with commit impact |Delta| only when S5 is editable. Alternative outcome: if gate edits stay near 0% under real drift, that is a clean negative showing that no ordinary environmental pressure induces meta-level change, which argues for building forced revalidation and meta-budgets into harnesses. In the latest (round-3 unified) version, the idea is the D_eval / L5 cells of the CauseShift benchmark inside the 'Open Where the Cause Is' reach x credit law: grader drift puts the cause at L5, so the openness optimum should move to the editable gate only when the gate can credit an edit made there.

**Design.** Standalone version (judged): a shared Godel-lite loop with surfaces S1-S5. Three benchmarks with real verifier churn that changes scores (tau-bench/tau2-bench verifier commits, one BFCL checker or aggregation change, one SWE-style test-harness fix). Each benchmark steps through 4 pinned verifier states V0-V3, and the agent sees only the current verifier's feedback. A 30% held-out split is scored under the post-commit verifier and under a human-validated 'intended' grader. Arms at matched tokens: (a) object-level only, frozen gate; (b) improver-editable, gate frozen; (c) gate-editable; (d) frozen gate plus an oracle 'revalidate on version bump' procedure; (e) shuffled commit order. Models: Qwen3-32B on vLLM plus one API check, 5 lineages per arm. Metrics: S4/S5 share of retained edits, stale accepted edits, recovery AUC per commit, and the verifier-overfit gap (intended grader vs current verifier). Stats: negative-binomial GLM for edit counts, mixed models for scores, bootstrap over lineages. Unified version (latest): DialLoop rung L5 (editable gate policy: revalidation schedule, thresholds, re-eval and votes, which signals are read), with the sealed grader and meta-gate kept outside every surface. D_eval uses 2 real BenchPulse-mined BFCL checker/aggregation commits on a second substrate of about 100 tasks. Block A runs L0-L5 x D_eval with 5 seeds. Block B runs quota {off, 0.25} x credit {object gate, replay meta-gate} at L5/D_eval with 6 seeds. Proposer is Qwen3.5-27B, executor a frozen Qwen3-8B. Every gate edit is coded tighten, loosen or revalidate, and intended-grader scoring is primary. Cost: about $30 for the pilot. The whole CauseShift paper is about $1.8-2.6k; judges said the standalone 5x5x3x4 grid would exceed $3k unless cut.

**Build load.** To build: (1) pinned git checkouts of each verifier version, wired into a replay harness; (2) the BenchPulse commit list, which must be obtained or re-mined, with commits chosen to span a wide |Delta| range including sign flips; (3) a human-validated 'intended' consensus grader; (4) an S5 gate surface whose semantics are explicit (cached scores for retained edits, what a gate edit can change); (5) stale-edit tracking, meaning per-edit benefit re-scored under each verifier; (6) an edit-type coder with a blind audit of S4/S5 attribution; (7) the tau-bench user-simulator budget. Rough estimate: about 5-7 person-weeks standalone with 1-2 benchmarks, or about 2-3 weeks as the D_eval slice of CauseShift on top of DialLoop. All three judges flagged the full standalone scope (3 benchmarks x 4 states x 5 arms x 5 lineages x 2 models) as too large for one undergrad in 6 months. · **Pilot.** One benchmark (tau-bench retail, 60-task subset), 2 real verifier states around the -5.66 pp commit, arms (a) and (c), 3 lineages, local Qwen3-32B, about $30 of API. GO if both hold: at least 10% of accepted V0 edits flip benefit under V1, and arm (c) makes at least one gate edit in at least 2 of 3 lineages. KILL if fewer than 5% of edits flip (real drift is too gentle); then switch to a larger-impact BFCL aggregation change. Judges warn that the 10% flip criterion will probably fail, because a -5.66 pp commit on 60 tasks changes only about 3-4 verdicts. Fixes: use at least 150 tasks or stack commits, pick commits with at least 15% verdict churn, and before the pilot show that a frozen gate really carries stale edits forward (otherwise the stale-edit metric is zero by construction). Unified CauseShift gate: R_target(L5) >= 0.6 and R_other <= 0.3 on the oracle reach probe, with the frozen-harness drop calibrated to 10 +/- 2 pp.

**Closest work.** Closest is tn4eslVXEK, 'When the Evaluator Changes' (ICLR27 sub): a cross-version model x evaluator matrix with false-progress decomposition and recoverability after an evaluator fix. It already owns the stale-progress and verifier-overfit measurement. Delta: harness-level self-improver rather than weight RL; mined, exogenous, real commits rather than controlled revisions; an editable gate; and the gate/improver edit rate as the outcome. Other neighbours: BenchPulse hpoWWEDJW9 is the data source only (4,119 commits; the tau-bench -5.66 pp commit; the BFCL change that reverses 893 orderings) and has no learning agent. If it publishes its commit list, the mining contribution shrinks. Red Queen Godel Machine 2606.26294 and Godel Ecology N4GJVmrzsF have co-evolving evaluators, but the drift is endogenous and designed in, with no stale-edit or self-gate-edit measurement. Feedback Alignment C6EElQ2PRG has a static visible-vs-target gap with no drift. Masked Erosion 2610.38392 studies fixed experimenter gate rules under a non-moving grader. AgentDriftBench 5xFWggnePr has recovery metrics for environment drift and no improver. Are-SE-CL 2610.25858 observes the Meta-Harness gate freezing but never perturbs the gate's feedback source. SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797 reports 0% improver edits on stationary graders. Also relevant: 2610.27465 (recoverability and verifier strength), 2610.11464 (a co-evolved verifier collapses to always-pass), GX4ucmiKZa (evaluator state). Scoop verdict: medium-risk. No paper replays real verifier commits as a stream for a self-improver, or tests whether grader drift causes gate or improver self-edits, so the behavioral core is open and the framing around it is crowded. Per the user's revised rubric, novelty is downweighted: the idea needs only to beat the neighbours on the behavioral claim, not be fully novel.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.6).** I ran 9 ICLR27 OpenReview queries and 1 alphaXiv recency query. Semantic Scholar was skipped. None of the hits replays real verifier or harness commits as a drift stream into a harness-level self-improver, and none measures whether drift makes the agent edit its own gate or improver. The behavioral core is still open as of 2026-11-13. These are the threats ranked for mid-May 2027:
(1) tn4eslVXEK "When the Evaluator Changes" (Loom replay dataflow). This is the strongest fast-follower. The rebuttal or camera-ready, or a resubmission to ICML27 (~Feb), could add a harness or agent arm. It would still most likely use controlled evaluator revisions under weight-RL with no editable gate, so it should stay strong on measurement (false-progress decomposition) and weak on the meta-edit behavioral claim. Chance of a meaningful overlapping extension by May: ~30%.
(2) BenchPulse hpoWWEDJW9 authors. They own the commit corpus and could add a "do self-improvers adapt to verifier churn" section. This would probably be a shallow add-on with one agent loop and no gate surface. Chance: ~20%.
(3) Masked Erosion 2610.38392 (Macau/Tsinghua). Their gate-rule setup with a moving grader is a natural sequel. It would compete directly on the gate-under-drift question, but likely with synthetic drift. Unlikely to be public before May; chance ~15-20%.
(4) RSI-infrastructure groups: Flex-RSI 2611.08946 (large multi-institution), RSI-Env 2611.01736, Beneath the Surface 2611.07624, Improvement by Inheritance ZMmJDeDTXk, Red Queen GM o3txFJw4JR, Godel Ecology N4GJVmrzsF. Any of them could add a "nonstationary grader" condition to a benchmark. If they do, expect breadth (many models) and no real-commit realism or stale-edit or tau-decay analysis.
(5) Recoverability/verifier-strength 2610.27465 (Uniphore) and Who-Verifies-the-Verifier 2610.11464 (AWS). These are adjacent; an extension to verifier versioning is plausible but more likely to be about deployment ops.
Overall, P(at least one close concurrent paper by May 2027 that tests self-gate edits under evaluator drift) is ~45%. Even if one appears, it will probably use synthetic or endogenous drift and a single benchmark. *Stay ahead:* 1. Own the realism axis. Use real mined commits on at least 2 substrates (tau-bench and BFCL), each with a quantified score impact |Delta|. Release the pinned V0-V3 verifier states as a reusable drift-replay kit, so BenchPulse publishing its commit list does not undercut the work.
2. Make intended-grader scoring primary and validate it with humans. That is the dimension where tn4eslVXEK-style measurement papers cannot match a harness-level paper.
3. Keep the negative result publishable: pre-register it so that ~0% gate edits under real drift counts as a finding and pairs with the forced-revalidation and meta-budget remedy (Block B quota x credit).
4. Include arm (d), the oracle revalidate-on-bump procedure, and the shuffled-order control. These make the causal claim stronger than any add-on section a competitor bolts onto a benchmark paper.
5. Explicitly reanalyse or contrast with tn4eslVXEK, Masked Erosion and SelfImprove-Bench, for example by reproducing their stationary-grader 0% improver-edit baseline inside our loop.
6. Post an arXiv preprint around Feb-Mar 2027, before ICML27 submissions surface, to claim the behavioral result.
7. Embedding this in CauseShift as the D_eval/L5 cells makes us the broader, more general version. But keep the drift-to-gate-edit result as a headline figure, not a buried cell. Without that, a focused competitor could look like the cleaner version.
8. Monitor tn4eslVXEK revisions and the BenchPulse and Masked Erosion author pages monthly. *New finds:* No new direct scoops. Adjacent papers not in the brief:
- 2611.07624, Beneath the Surface: reliability of harness evolution.
- 2611.08946, Flex-RSI: RSI benchmark from a big consortium.
- 2611.01736, RSI-Env.
- 2611.08318, Learning the Task or the Verifier: criteria exposure in embodied self-improvement.
- 2610.14662, Does Failure Feedback Direct Agent Self-Modification?
- 2611.13336, Veritas: verifier-generation harness.
- 2610.29005, VeriEvolve: evaluator-first evolution.
- 2610.17609 / HuOAuecWpX, Verifier-Harness Co-Evolution.
- b1C4ZHfryh, GymHarness: recursively verified verifiers.
- 3t0j2Y05EH, Lyapunov-Gated RSI: gate design.
- 0FWBc4gY08, Statistical Godel Machines: risk-controlled acceptance.
- ZMmJDeDTXk, Improvement by Inheritance.
- c3lHKtsSMq, Governing Self-Improvement Across Timescales: not close, about the cost of refinement and memory.
Red Queen GM is ICLR27 id o3txFJw4JR.

- **ac** — risk: The headline will probably be thin or null. Real verifier commits are mostly narrow bug fixes that flip only a few verdicts (roughly 3-4 on 60 tau-bench tasks), so stale edits are rare and a Qwen-class proposer may never edit S4 or S5. The likely result is 0% vs 0%, which only restates SelfImprove-Bench. If arm (c) does win, reviewers will attribute it to the agent loosening its gate or to the trivial oracle-revalidate fix (arm d), not to meaningful meta-adaptation. Separately, about 9 transitions with a handful of flip events per lineage cannot support the tau-decay law, and the full scope (3 benchmarks x 4 states x 5 arms x lineages) is too large for one undergrad. · raise P: Before any scaling, guarantee a strong drift signal and center the paper on a 2x2: changelog/diff-visible vs feedback-only, crossed with frozen vs editable gate. Run it on 1-2 benchmarks, using stacked or high-churn real commits (at least 15% verdict churn, at least 150 tasks), at least 8 lineages, two open models, and a synthetic-drift control matched on |Delta| plus a stationary-grader c0 control. Score on the intended grader, with gate edits coded as tighten, loosen or revalidate. This turns a likely null into a mechanistic result ('agents act on drift only when they can see it, and gate edits help only when they revalidate rather than loosen'), and it beats tn4eslVXEK and the RSI benchmarks on the behavioral claim even if they add drift arms.
- **reviewer** — risk: The likeliest result is the 0%-vs-0% null. Qwen almost never edits S4/S5. Real commits are narrow bug fixes that flip only a few verdicts, so the stale-edit metric is tiny or is zero by construction whenever the gate re-scores under the current verifier. Oracle arm (d), a 2-line revalidation step, then explains everything. A reviewer will read this as a restatement of SelfImprove-Bench plus a port of tn4eslVXEK, with about 9 transitions and too little power for the tau law. The behavioral core is still new, but I downweighted novelty here as you asked. What sinks the paper is the expected null and the build load: a pinned verifier replay harness plus an intended grader is a lot for one undergrad to build and validate. · raise P: Before scaling, make a positive mechanism result likely. Add a diff/changelog-visible arm against a feedback-only arm, and pick or stack commits with at least 15% verdict churn on at least 150 tasks. Then report a clean 2x2 of drift visibility against gate editability, with arms c0 and synthetic-drift controls and the intended grader as the primary score. That design shows when and why a self-improver revalidates instead of loosening its gate. It turns a probable null into a causal finding with a clear headline figure, and it works whether you keep it as the CauseShift L5 slice or run it standalone on 1-2 benchmarks with at least 8 lineages and 2 open models.
- **pi** — risk: The likely result is 0% vs 0%. Real verifier commits are mostly narrow bug fixes, so a frozen gate carries forward only a few stale edits (on 60 to 150 tasks they change roughly 3 to 4 verdicts each), and Qwen-class proposers rarely touch S4/S5. That gives an underpowered null that mostly restates SelfImprove-Bench. Even when the gate is edited, oracle arm (d), a 2-line revalidation step, may beat it outright. Reviewers would then read the paper as "tn4eslVXEK ported to a harness loop", with a trivial fix and no mechanism. · raise P: Pre-commit to a 2x2 factorial that can produce a positive, mechanistic result whichever way the main effect goes. Factor 1 is feedback-only vs diff/changelog-visible. Factor 2 is frozen vs editable gate, with c0 (editable gate, stationary grader) and a synthetic-drift control matched on |Delta|. Run it on 1-2 benchmarks, with at least 8 lineages and stacked high-churn commits (at least 15% verdict churn). Show before the main run that a frozen gate really carries stale edits forward. Code every gate edit as tighten, loosen or revalidate, and make intended-grader scoring primary. Drop the tau-decay law to an appendix and cut shuffled order and the third benchmark.

### Late Gains Are Masked, Not Missing: Decomposing Self-Improvement Plateaus with Edit-Level Epistasis (sharp-interference-plateau; FINAL_REPORT #8, panel 62.1) (`interference-plateau`) — EV 8.8%

**Claim.** In Gödel-style self-improvement lineages, the held-out effect of accepted edits shrinks as lineage depth grows. After de-noising (re-evaluation on a fresh pool) and after fitting a global-epistasis (headroom) link, a substantial share of that shrinkage is idiosyncratic pairwise or sign epistasis among the accepted edits, not selection noise or saturation. So rebasing a de-noised, interaction-aware subset of the lineage's own edits onto an ancestor beats the tip at matched compute. Additive surrogates (Mendel Gödel Machine theory, MESH-Harness LinUCB, linear diff readouts) should then be misspecified exactly where lineages plateau. Pre-registered falsifier: rebase <= tip (TOST +/-1 pp) AND pairwise terms add <10% held-out R² over global epistasis. Latest framing (panel and mentor): pitch it as a four-way DECOMPOSITION, not a method. The four accounts are noise/winner's curse, global headroom, idiosyncratic interference, and exhaustion/blindness, and every cell is publishable. The most likely honest headline is "plateaus are mostly noise + headroom" (null-framed), not "masked".

**Design.** Run about 6 x 30-generation Gödel-lite lineages on 2 domains (one code/agentic, one reasoning or tau-style), using cheap executors (Qwen3.5- or Flash-class). Log every proposal, accepted or rejected, as a structured patch. An edit-graft matrix applies each edit onto gen-0, gen-k/2 and its parent, scored on a fresh paired pool of 300+ items x 3 seeds. Graft applicability (the dependency structure) is reported as a first-class result, with LLM porting only as a secondary arm that gets a fidelity check. For the top-15 edits per lineage, about 200 random-subset knockout libraries (DMS-style) are fitted with additive, global-epistasis (per-task logit link) and pairwise models; the estimand is the pairwise share of held-out R². The four-way decomposition is pre-registered: noise means fresh-pool effect ~0 both in-lineage and on gen-0; headroom means a single nonlinear link explains the data; interference means sign epistasis plus rebase > tip; exhaustion means rejected late proposals grafted onto gen-0 do no better than early ones. Meta-level (improver) edits are scored by next-k acceptance rate. Arms: rebase vs tip vs bag-of-edits vs an LRSI-selected tip vs an RRSI-pruned tip, at matched tokens. Required extra cells: reproduce the yl1XFtnTn4 null and show where epistasis turns non-zero (depth or power), and rule out the evaluation-cost ceiling (NxGP6PGiaU) and the content ceiling (FPaMYO47j4). The mentor recommends cutting to 1 domain plus a small replication and dropping the Lenski replay and surrogate-misspecification extras. Cost is about $1-1.5k in API spend; the pilot is under $100.

**Build load.** Everything is built from scratch, and the heavy parts are engineering for a solo undergrad:
- A Gödel-lite lineage runner that emits structured, replayable patches (not free rewrites) for both accepted and rejected proposals.
- A graft/port engine that applies edits onto ancestors and tracks applicability.
- Fresh-pool evaluation harness with seeds.
- Random-subset knockout library generator.
- Additive, global-epistasis and pairwise regression fitting with held-out R² and a power analysis.
- Reimplementations of the LRSI and RRSI-pruner baselines.

Estimate about 8-12 person-weeks: about 3-4 for the runner and patch logging, 2 for graft and evaluation, 1-2 for the epistasis modelling, 2-3 for the baselines, plus analysis. Most of the API spend is evaluation; the mentor flagged evaluation spend as too high for 6 months unless the scope is cut to one domain. · **Pilot.** Pilot: 2 weeks and under $100, with 2 lineages x 15 generations and grafts only. Continue only if all three hold:
1. At least 60% of accepted edits graft cleanly onto gen-0 (AC gate). If fewer than 50% apply, drop the standalone paper (expert).
2. At least 15 accepted edits per lineage, so the top-15 knockout libraries are possible.
3. The depth-dependent shrinkage of edit effects survives fresh-pool de-noising.

If grafts fail or the shrinkage disappears after de-noising, pivot to a null-framed "plateaus are noise + headroom" paper, or fold it in as a mechanism section of Meta-Overfitting. A power analysis is also required before the main run.

**Closest work.** These two ICLR 2027 submissions decide the case:
- **LRSI, Lookahead-Guided RSI (OpenReview 71wzcahKcb; not the other "LRSI", V7vjimAYGi).** It toggles each candidate edit on and off across sampled future versions and keeps only interference-robust edits (+2.4-4.4 pp on 4 benchmarks). So it owns the knockout-across-continuations tool, the "later edits make earlier ones harmful" intuition and the selection remedy. It does NOT claim plateaus are caused by interference, and it has no epistasis matrix, no global-vs-idiosyncratic split and no rebase-onto-ancestor test.
- **Auditing Harness Evolution (yl1XFtnTn4).** Its RSI Information Ledger finds no detectable positive interaction among accepted edits, and no lineage advantage over one audit-selected edit at matched budget. This is a published prior null on exactly this effect, at small scale (4B model, MBPP).

Other neighbours:
- DarwinX (2608.07545 / XVvUgp3Bu8) states the interference motivation and has a merge-from-ancestor operator. It never measures epistasis, and all of its WebArena-Infinity merges were reverted.
- RRSI (2609.24972 / 75PPLZgaUM) has a pruner that removes stale, low-credit components; its credit logs put an epistasis analysis "one appendix away".
- Harness-S (2611.04277) asserts edit interference but only measures sibling repair overlap.
- Rival plateau accounts:
  - Winner's Curse (2610.09239): noise.
  - Opinionated Optimizers (LhhLpXBFgo): narrow proposer priors, weak evidence from noisy evals.
  - Harness Evolution Hits a Ceiling (FPaMYO47j4 / 2610.11655): content ceiling and proposer blindness. It also reports positive component epistasis.
  - NxGP6PGiaU: evaluation-cost ceiling.
  - 0FaPTsSr9C: plateau as a stopping problem.
- Attribution overlap: Harness-Delta Attribution (PX4Zpvsaca / 2610.22466) and Beneath the Surface (2611.07624).
- Methodological template: Masked Erosion (2610.38392).
- Corollary targets: Mendel Gödel Machine (additivity assumption) and MESH-Harness (linear surrogate).

Exact delta over the closest work: the first in-lineage edit-graft matrix plus a pairwise-R²-above-global-epistasis decomposition. It separates the four plateau accounts, uses a rejected-proposal graft to test exhaustion, and compares rebase vs tip vs an LRSI-selected tip at matched compute. Under the relaxed novelty criterion, the question is whether this decomposition is better than LRSI and yl1XFtnTn4. Advantage: it explains why plateaus happen and reconciles yl1XFtnTn4's null (underpowered or too shallow?), where LRSI is only a selection heuristic. Risk: if the outcome is "noise + headroom", its advantage over yl1XFtnTn4 is mostly scale, depth and rigor.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.62).** Searched 2026-11-13: about 9 ICLR 2027 OpenReview queries, 2 alphaXiv recency queries and 1 Semantic Scholar query (the second S2 query was rate-limited). Nobody has a DMS-style random-subset knockout library or an epistasis decomposition that splits global headroom from pairwise/sign interaction in RSI lineages. Nobody has tested rebasing onto an ancestor against the tip either. Likely rivals, ranked:
(1) LRSI (71wzcahKcb) authors. They already have the tool that toggles edits across future continuations. During ICLR rebuttal (Nov-Jan) or in an ICML 2027 follow-up around Feb, they could plausibly add an "interference explains plateaus" analysis. If so it would be a selection-method paper with an epistasis appendix, not a decomposition. Chance of a serious analysis: about 25%. Quality: moderate.
(2) The Auditing Harness Evolution (yl1XFtnTn4) group, with its RSI Information Ledger. The most dangerous fast-follower: they could scale their null to bigger models and deeper lineages and publish it as a definitive null. Chance: about 20-25%. If they do, and our result is also "noise + headroom", our lead rests on the decomposition with four accounts, the rejected-proposal graft and the matched-compute rebase arm.
(3) The Compositional RSI (qAYeg9I0K3) and NodeGrad (T9g5z7FPG9) groups. Both already frame "the value of one control changes with its collaborators" and "joint probes reveal conditional benefits between nodes". Both are methods with no plateau decomposition. Either could add a pairwise-interaction table.
(4) Harness Evolution Hits a Ceiling (FPaMYO47j4), which already reports positive component epistasis. RRSI (75PPLZgaUM), whose credit logs make the analysis easy. DarwinX. Infrastructure groups such as Improvement by Inheritance (ZMmJDeDTXk), RSI-Harness (EOrtreQLbv), Flex-RSI and HarnessPro, which are well placed to run large ablations.
(5) Third Eye (2611.12988), on whether self-updates stay useful after later updates, but at weight level on 3-8B models.
I expect 1-3 papers by May 2027 that measure edit interaction in passing, as an appendix or a method justification. I expect at most one, probably from the yl1XFtnTn4 or LRSI groups, that makes it a central empirical claim. That paper would likely be smaller in scale and lack the global-epistasis link and the pre-registered falsifiers. *Stay ahead:* 1) Frame the paper as the reconciling decomposition. Reproduce yl1XFtnTn4's null in its own setting (4B model, MBPP), then show the depth or power at which pairwise epistasis turns non-zero, or show that it never does. That turns the closest prior into a sub-result of our paper.
2) Include an LRSI-selected-tip arm and an RRSI-pruned-tip arm at matched tokens. Then any LRSI or RRSI follow-up is already a baseline we have beaten or matched.
3) Release the edit-graft matrix and the knockout libraries as a dataset of structured patches, with every accepted and rejected proposal logged. A reusable artifact is hard for fast-followers to beat on completeness.
4) Publish the global-epistasis (per-task logit) link early. "Epistasis" claims from Compositional RSI, NodeGrad or FPaMYO47j4 that skip the link can then be argued to confuse headroom with interference.
5) Run the pilot by about Jan 2027. If the cells come out "noise + headroom", lead with that null openly and power the deep-lineage cells, so a scaled-up null from the yl1XFtnTn4 group is still strictly smaller than ours.
6) Do the mentor's cut (1 domain plus a small replication), but keep the arm with rejected proposals grafted onto gen-0 (the exhaustion test). It is the cell no competitor has.
7) Re-search after the ICLR rebuttal revisions (around late Jan) and after ICML 2027 submissions appear on arXiv (around Feb), especially for LRSI v2 and the ledger group. *New finds:* qAYeg9I0K3 (Compositional Recursive Self-Improvement: the value of a control depends on its collaborators; guarded assembly; a method only), T9g5z7FPG9 (NodeGrad: joint probes reveal conditional benefits between nodes; SWE-bench Lite 66 to 86%), 2611.12988 (Third Eye: does a self-update stay useful after the next update; weight level, 3-8B), 2610.29121 (Recursive Self-Improvement on a Budget: a linear readout scores code edits before they run; a target for the additive-surrogate claim), 6YRJH50sAy / 2610.30042 (HGM-Dual: lineage-aware harness optimization), 9UsBcc69Ua (Measured Behavior, Recorded Zero: measurement failures in self-improving coding agents; relevant to the noise account), T04ZzT5m6j (When Self-Reinforcement Ends Self-Improvement: deterministic judge errors end improvement; a rival plateau account), 2611.13524 (The RSI Tax), 2611.08946 (Flex-RSI benchmark), 2610.34938 (How coding-agent harnesses self-evolve: an empirical study).

- **ac** — risk: The likeliest result is "plateaus are mostly noise + headroom". That is close to yl1XFtnTn4's published null, so reviewers would read the paper as a bigger, more rigorous replication of that null with LRSI's knockout tool. On top of that, low graft applicability (and the bias from keeping only self-contained edits) can undermine the pairwise-R² estimand. Both risks are amplified by heavy solo engineering (lineage runner, graft engine, LRSI/RRSI reimplementations) on one domain. · raise P: Make reconciling the yl1XFtnTn4 null the main result. Pre-register a power-and-depth curve showing at what lineage depth and evaluation budget pairwise/sign epistasis becomes detectable above the global-epistasis link, and pair it with the matched-compute rebase vs tip vs LRSI-selected-tip arm. Then every outcome is a clear "when and why the prior null holds or fails" finding rather than a plain null. Cut to 1 domain plus a small replication so the de-noised knockout libraries are well-powered.
- **reviewer** — risk: The most likely result is "plateaus are mostly noise plus headroom". This paper looks only at measurements, using small Gödel-lite lineages and cheap executors, and agents-pool reviewers tend to read that kind of null as a bigger replication of yl1XFtnTn4. Two things make it worse. Graft applicability filters the edits down to self-contained ones, which biases the estimand ("hard to trust"). And the heavy solo engineering (runner, graft engine, knockout libraries, LRSI and RRSI reimplementations) may only be done in time for one underpowered domain. · raise P: Before the main run, show that the pairwise or sign-epistasis share grows with lineage depth or statistical power, so that the yl1XFtnTn4 null is reconciled rather than simply reproduced. Then put that result in the headline figure next to the rebase-vs-tip-vs-LRSI comparison at matched compute, run on one strong domain with a second executor model for replication. This turns the paper from a null into a positive mechanistic finding, and a positive mechanism is what moves this reviewer pool. If the pilot shows that won't happen, cut the build load (drop the RRSI reimplementation) and commit to a tightly powered null paper with pre-registered falsifiers.
- **pi** — risk: The work has two linked problems. First, the engineering is heavy for one undergrad: a runner that logs replayable patches, a graft engine, knockout libraries, and reimplementations of LRSI and RRSI. Second, grafts often fail to apply, so the edits that can be studied are a biased sample, and the result will most likely be the null "noise + headroom". Reviewers would then read the paper as a larger-scale rerun of the yl1XFtnTn4 null that sits in LRSI's shadow, and its edge would rest only on scale, depth and rigour. · raise P: Cut to one domain plus a small replication, and fix the main contribution in advance as a power-calibrated reconciliation of the yl1XFtnTn4 null. That means showing at what lineage depth or statistical power pairwise or sign epistasis becomes detectable above the global-epistasis link, with the rebase vs tip vs LRSI-tip comparison at matched compute. This makes the paper valuable whatever the result, and it should be backed by a passed graft-applicability pilot and a public replayable lineage dataset of accepted and rejected patches.

### The Two-Sided Drift Barrier of Self-Modifying Agents: editor defects and inert bloat ratchet in below gate resolution (nonobv-error-threshold, FINAL_REPORT #6, panel 67.2; folds in behav-drift-barrier-bloat, R3 #14, 62.2, and the gate-dial plasticity half, #15) (`drift-barrier`) — EV 8.7%

**Claim.** A gated, single-lineage (elitist) self-modifier keeps or purges an edit according to one acceptance law, P_acc(s) ≈ Φ(s·√n/σ − z_gate), plus a tie-rule term when s = 0. Edits whose true held-out effect |s| falls below the barrier z_gate·σ/√n are invisible to selection. (1) Editor/improver code is selected only second-order, through what later edits do, so it sits below the barrier more often and retains defects faster than task code (the "mutator" asymmetry). (2) Retained held-out gain collapses at a critical harness length L*(n, ε, edit mode). L* rises with n, falls with defect load, exists under full rewrites but moves under patch editing, and is lower for a self-referential improver (Gödel Agent) than for the same editor frozen. (3) Bloat/erosion, the two-sided half: raising n cuts slightly harmful inserted junk but speeds up erosion of cheap safeguards, while exactly-inert components track the tie rule, not n. Falsifiers: equal editor vs task region retention; L* flat in n; patch and rewrite behaving the same; inert load tracking only the proposer add rate (which would mean mutation bias, not drift). Positioning: this is a quantitative, pre-registered law with an editor-vs-task mechanism, not "gates are noisy".

**Design.** Base system: Gödel Agent (the mentor is first author), self-referential vs the identical editor frozen, crossed with rewrite vs patch editing. Harnesses are seeded at L ∈ {1k, 4k, 16k} tokens with executed redundant modules; 48k is dropped per the mentor. An organically grown harness serves as a control against "padding" critiques. Gate n ∈ {8, 32, 128}, with n=128 run only at 2 lengths. Gate arms: greedy/elitist, TRUST (FDR) and SGM or an e-process gate, plus Masked Erosion's paired gate, to show L* moves but does not vanish under gate fixes. 2 open backbones (e.g., Qwen3-8B/Coder plus a second family), ≥5 seeds on cells near the knee (3 elsewhere), 15 generations, 1 main domain plus 1 transfer domain. Each generation gets a defect audit: a frozen 20-request editor-probe suite, region-tagged unit tests, and blind hunk labels (beneficial/neutral/defective), validated against injected defects with a per-region confusion matrix and against human labels. The bloat side logs component activation and a stratified leave-one-out s per component, giving the first per-edit-surface DFE (prompt/memory/tool/code; gamma fit). L* is derived on paper for the actual elitist accept rule, and slopes are pre-registered before the runs. The law is fit on injected-noise calibration runs only, then predicts each backbone's natural ceiling and final harness length out of sample. Cost: pilot under $60; full study about $900–1.5k API, plus local GPU.

**Build load.** From scratch: (a) the formal single-lineage L* / stationary-load derivation (P_acc + tie rule → L*(n, σ, gate, u_add/u_del, DFE)), which is the item judges most flagged as still not done, about 2–3 wk; (b) a region-tagged seeded-harness generator plus defect/editor-mutation injection on Gödel Agent, about 2–3 wk; (c) the audit stack (editor-probe suite, region unit tests, hunk labeller with human and injected-defect validation), about 2–3 wk; (d) gate-arm reimplementations (TRUST, SGM/e-process, paired gate) at matched rollouts, about 1–2 wk; (e) a component-activation and stratified LOO DFE pipeline, about 1–2 wk; (f) runs and analysis, about 3–4 wk. Total about 11–17 person-weeks for one student. Gödel Agent code exists and the mentor knows it, which cuts the infra risk. · **Pilot.** Two-week, under-$60 pilot: 1 backbone, self-ref Gödel Agent, rewrite vs patch, L ∈ {1k, 16k}, n=32, 8 generations. GO if Gödel Agent accepts at least 1 editor-region edit per 4 generations, and region retention already separates editor from task code beyond seed noise. If self-edits are rare (as Improvement by Inheritance, ZMmJDeDTXk, predicts), switch to forced/injected editor mutations and report "improvers protect themselves by not self-editing" as a finding. That links to the R3 IbI-gap question of why self-edits are rare, where "effects below gate resolution" is an untested cause. Bloat-side gate: inert-component count falls monotonically with n ∈ {8, 32, 128}, with the n=8 vs n=128 gap larger than 2 seed SDs, and the DFE has at least 30% near-neutral mass (|s| < 0.5 SD). If inert count is flat in n, report mutation bias and drop the bloat arm. Paper gate: L* derived and slopes pre-registered before the full grid.

**Closest work.** T04ZzT5m6j "When Self-Reinforcement Ends Self-Improvement" (ICLR27 sub) proves that every fixed acceptance rule eventually drifts negative under a deterministic-error judge. Delta: no length law L*(n, ε), no editor-vs-task split, no edit-mode contrast. EvoLyapunov 3t0j2Y05EH (ICLR27) shows improver edits amplify noise, with a sharp boundary at RAF > 1; it is the closest to the mutator angle. Delta: a control/stability framing with no length law or region audit. EvoLyapunov's RAF must be a baseline/positioning target. 2608.05810 "When Self-Evolution Backfires" has a size knee k* and defects inherited through lineage in skill pools. Delta: skills rather than self-modifying code, ungated, no law, no gate-size scaling, no editor split. Kinetic Theory 23VDi5bfRj / 2610.03243 gives a resolution law and an evaluator-outside theorem but is scoped to plugin populations and excludes monolithic self-modifiers. NxGP6PGiaU sets a ceiling by evaluation cost only (no length or editor term). Masked Erosion 2610.38392 already imports Kimura/Ohta drift language, Φ(z − z_α) acceptance and an n sweep, but only for safeguard deletion. Delta: insertion/bloat, DFE and the editor region. Phantom Guardrails 2607.13083, SHarP 2610.04178, RB-Prompt uVKMqFVBDG, EvalAlloc 2610.37276 and CoEvolve take bloat phenomenology and length-cost/CRN fixes. TRUST wV4bK1dHpC, SGM 0FWBc4gY08 and Measure First VuzgEFCuwx take gate fixes and the "gates are noisy" framing. yl1XFtnTn4 reports inert guards persisting. Meta^n 2608.24735 asserts, without measuring, that self-editors must freeze part of themselves; the editor-probe suite tests that premise directly. Also cite j8GSV186VS, da1Czy7foS, XfutYzVRFq and cQkSamYGQi, plus the GP bloat literature (Langdon & Poli). The unclaimed delta is the quantitative L*(n, ε, edit mode) law, the editor-region ratchet, "self-referential has a lower L*", and the per-surface DFE.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.57).** This is a very crowded area. Since September 2026 there have been at least eight theory-plus-gate papers on gated harness self-evolution (T04ZzT5m6j, EvoLyapunov 3t0j2Y05EH, Kinetic Theory 23VDi5bfRj/2610.03243, Masked Erosion 2610.38392, SAGE 2609.36043, TRUST/2611.10787, A Theory of Reliable Self-Evolution 2609.08175, SGM). None found yet combines a harness-length law L*(n, eps, edit mode) with an editor-vs-task retention split. Likely close competitors by May 2027, most dangerous first:
(1) Masked Erosion authors (Macau/Tsinghua). They already have the Kimura/Ohta framing, Phi(z - z_alpha) acceptance and an n sweep. Extending from deletion to insertion/bloat, or adding a length axis, is the obvious next paper, maybe 35-45% by ICML/NeurIPS 2027. It would probably be cheap and clean (prompt harnesses, simulation plus 1 backbone), with no editor region or self-referential contrast.
(2) Kinetic Theory (Neusoft) or the 2609.08175 group (USTC/HKUST). They could add a size or ceiling term: 2609.08175 already gives a ceiling set by verification and evaluation cost, and its v3 could add a harness-size dependence. Mostly theory, light empirics.
(3) T04ZzT5m6j / EvoLyapunov revisions during the ICLR rebuttal. They may add a length or noise-amplification experiment, but they are unlikely to add a region audit.
(4) Infrastructure groups: Improvement by Inheritance ZMmJDeDTXk, RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv, HyperAgents, MEvo IrNv2E8nJO, and RSIHub / Beneath the Surface 2611.07624 (BIGAI/SJTU, who already run admission replays and component interventions). Any of them could publish a "the improver degrades itself" measurement with real frontier-model scale (GPT-5.x, SWE/Terminal-Bench). That would beat us on scale and realism but probably not on a quantitative law, pre-registration, or DFE.
(5) ICML 2027 (around February) will likely bring 1-3 "bloat/redundancy in evolved harnesses" phenomenology papers, in the vein of Phantom Guardrails, SHarP and the cost-aware HarnessPro work.
Overall I expect someone to post a partial version (a drift barrier plus a length/bloat effect in harness evolution) with probability around 55-65%. A full match (editor split + L* law + edit-mode contrast + self-referential vs frozen) is unlikely, under 15%. *Stay ahead:* 1. Lead with the parts nobody else has: the editor-vs-task retention ratchet (editor-probe suite plus a region-tagged audit validated on injected defects), and the self-referential vs frozen-editor L* gap. Masked Erosion and the theory papers cannot easily copy these. Put them in figure 1 and the abstract, not the drift law in general.
2. Post a short arXiv version early, around February-March 2027, before the ICML wave settles. It should include the analytic L* for the elitist rule, the pre-registered slopes (on OSF or a GitHub commit hash) and the pilot. This timestamps the law and the falsifiers, so later papers become confirmations.
3. Absorb the competition in your own experiments rather than only citing it. Run the Masked Erosion paired gate, a TRUST/SAGE-style FDR gate, the Two-Gate/measured-margin rule from 2609.08175, and EvoLyapunov's RAF as a baseline predictor of L*. Show that L* shifts under each of them but does not disappear. Then any gate-fix paper becomes a special case.
4. Close the scale gap with infrastructure groups. Add a frontier-API backbone on a reduced grid (2 lengths x 2 n), plus one non-Godel-Agent base (a HyperAgents/DGM-style or RSIHub operator setup), so the paper doesn't read as "an old Godel Agent with 8B models".
5. Make the out-of-sample prediction the headline result: fit on injected noise, then predict each backbone's natural ceiling and final length. Theory-only competitors cannot match that. Release the region-audit toolkit and the DFE data as a benchmark so later work builds on ours.
6. Watch for ICLR 2027 rebuttal revisions of T04ZzT5m6j, 3t0j2Y05EH, 23VDi5bfRj and Masked Erosion v2 (late Nov to Jan), and ICML submissions on arXiv (around February). Update the deltas then. *New finds:* 2609.08175 (A Theory of Reliable Self-Evolution for Agent Harnesses: Two-Gate validation, a ceiling set by verification/evaluation cost, O(u^-2) samples; no length or editor term; essentially the same delta as NxGP6PGiaU). 2611.07624 (Beneath the Surface / RSIHub, BIGAI/SJTU: admission replays, component interventions and coverage turnover across AHE/HyperAgents/A-Evolve/GEPA; an infrastructure group that could fast-follow on improver audits). 2611.10787 (Trustworthy RSI with Statistical Guarantees). 2609.36043 SAGE (statistical acceptance gate). 2610.14662 (Does Failure Feedback Direct Self-Modification?). 2609.34924 (stationarity dichotomy for RSI scaffolds). 2610.34938 (empirical study of coding-harness self-evolution). ICLR27: 75PPLZgaUM RRSI (regularized RSI of harnesses), mblZCSyJsB (Compositional Self-Improvement: Promises and Pitfalls), qAYeg9I0K3, c3lHKtsSMq (Governing Self-Improvement Across Timescales), IkYmTAyPXZ (Capability Loss in Embodied RSI), RKNhYvoi7E (generator-verifier error correlation in RSI), cnaYLnpsAV (reliable self-improvement when benchmarks are reused), kpSZgKSZ05 (Terminal Self-Degradation), IrNv2E8nJO MEvo, PX4Zpvsaca (Harness-Delta Attribution). None of them has an L*(n, eps) length law or an editor-vs-task region split.
