Move bloat/DFE and the extra gate arms to the appendix, so reviewers see one clean, falsifiable law with a striking figure rather than a crowded, sprawling grid.
- **pi** — risk: The scope is too large for one student. Six build items (11-17 person-weeks before any slippage) plus a multi-arm grid have to fit into about 26 weeks, and the whole design rests on Gödel Agent making natural editor-region edits that pass the gate, which Improvement by Inheritance (ZMmJDeDTXk) predicts will be rare. If the pilot falls back to forced or injected editor mutations, the headline result becomes an artifact of padded, forced-edit harnesses. That is exactly the skeptical-reviewer objection, and it would leave a muddled three-claim paper (editor ratchet, L*, bloat DFE) in a crowded field. Masked Erosion or Kinetic Theory adding a length or insertion axis first would take most of the remaining delta. · raise P: Cut to one tight claim: the derived L*(n, ε, edit mode) law plus the editor-vs-task retention split. Fit it on calibration runs and predict out of sample on 2 backbones, run head-to-head against EvoLyapunov RAF and the TRUST/SGM gates. Drop the bloat/DFE arm to an appendix, or remove it, and use the freed weeks for ≥5 seeds on every knee cell. This turns a sprawling, fragile study into one clean, pre-registered, falsifiable law that beats the closest concurrent work on rigor.

### The Openness Optimum Moves: a token-matched permission ladder in which held-out gain peaks at an intermediate editable surface, and the peak shifts with optimizer capability and verifier noise (openness-ladder-crossover, round 3, panel 68.6) (`openness-ladder-crossover`) — EV 8.5%

**Claim.** Hold the optimizer, token budget and tasks fixed and randomize only the editable surface. Held-out gain is then an inverted U over openness rungs L0-L5. The peak rung moves toward more open surfaces as optimizer capability and validation-set size (n_val) grow, and toward less open ones as evaluator noise (sigma) grows. Each alternative outcome is still a paper. (a) If gain rises monotonically to L4/L5 for every tier, this is the first randomized-surface test of the "bitter lesson for scaffolds", and it shows OEO's crossover is a quirk of its two systems. (b) If gain is flat once tokens are matched, the token-matched null results (Mirage, TPE) carry over to the openness axis: openness differences between published systems are mostly proposer and budget artifacts. Per the scoop check, the headline has to be the peak-SHIFT interaction, not the inverted U, which RobustSGPO and EvoSpace already show or claim.

**Design.** Testbed "LadderLoop": a Meta-Harness-lite keep-if-better loop where one config file sets which files the proposer may write. The rungs are:
- L0: numeric slots and few-shot ids
- L1: free system-prompt text
- L2: a typed DSL of operators (retry, verify, decompose, memory-read)
- L3: full task-harness Python
- L4: L3 plus the proposer/improver prompt and code
- L5: L4 plus parent-selection and archive logic
The evaluator always stays external and read-only.

Optimizers come in three tiers: Qwen3.5-4B and Qwen3.5-27B on local vLLM, plus a cheap frontier-mini API model on a reduced grid. Tasks are a 6-problem CO-Bench heuristics subset (exact evaluator), tau2-bench retail or an AppWorld subset (noisy agentic), and a far-transfer split scored on a held-out domain. Each lineage gets 2M proposer+executor tokens, matched on tokens rather than iterations, with 5 seeds per cell. Noise is varied through n_val in {16, 64, 256}, for the 27B tier only.

Controls:
- the same rung with a random/blind proposer (NAS-style, to separate how good the space is from how good the proposer is)
- a matched-token best-of-k test-time-scaling baseline on the unedited harness
- the unedited baseline

The metric is held-out gain on a sealed test split never used for selection. The analysis is a hierarchical model with rung as an ordinal factor. The pre-registered peak test bootstraps the argmax rung and runs a Jonckheere trend test of peak location against capability and n_val. The pre-registered law is Gain ≈ A(O)(1−e^{−B/B0(O)}) − c·σ·sqrt(2 ln N_eff/n_val), with N_eff measured. It predicts dO*/dcap > 0, dO*/dn_val > 0 and dO*/dσ < 0, is fit on 2/3 of cells, and is validated on the remaining third against monotone and flat alternatives.

Fixes the scoop check asked for:
- an expanding/scheduled-openness arm (RobustSGPO 1→2→3 or EvoSpace-style) as an out-of-sample prediction target
- a per-rung report of realized vs permitted edits (nominal, realized and effective space)
- pass@k and coverage reported alongside the held-out mean

Cost: estimated $1.5-2.5k with open models on most rungs, frontier models only on the capability arm.

**Build load.** Things to build from scratch:
- LadderLoop: a permission-config loop with 6 rung definitions, an L2 operator DSL, and sandboxing for the L4/L5 self-editing rungs, where the improver and archive logic are editable. The sandboxing is serious engineering.
- A blind-proposer arm and a matched-token TTS baseline.
- Token accounting split between proposer and executor.
- A sealed test split, plus far-transfer scoring.
- Edit logging and classification (realized vs permitted, N_eff).
- A hierarchical model, the law fit and held-out-cell validation.

Estimate: about 8-12 person-weeks of building plus compute babysitting. As written, the full grid is beyond a solo undergrad in 6 months on $3k. The AC estimates about 540M tokens for the 27B tier alone and likely more than 1B tokens with the controls, the tau2 user simulator, the 4B tier, the frontier-mini tier and the expansion arm. The expert estimates 0.6-1B tokens. The grid has to be cut; see priorFeedback. · **Pilot.** Week 1, about $40 on the local 27B plus API calls for a 4B smoke test. Run rungs L1, L3 and L4 × 3 seeds × 0.5M tokens on CO-Bench (2 problems) and the tau2 subset, at n_val = 64.

- GO if both hold: (i) the held-out gain spread across rungs is more than 2× the seed SD on at least one task; and (ii) when permitted, L4 lineages make at least 1 accepted improver edit in at least 2 of 3 seeds. If (ii) fails, L4 is permitted but unused, and the design has to rely on forced improver edits (the forced-quota idea).
- KILL or REFRAME if every rung is within seed noise of the matched TTS baseline. Then pivot to a flat-null paper with the random-proposer decomposition as the main result. Judges say to pre-commit that this fallback is Datasets & Benchmarks track, not main track.
- Added by the judges: run a simulation-based power analysis from the pilot seed SD before committing to the peak-shift headline. If the argmax CI spans more than 1 rung, switch the pre-registered test to a parametric rung × tier (or rung × n_val) interaction contrast.

**Closest work.** Nearest prior work, and how this design differs:
- **RobustSGPO 2609.09646 (Sep 2026). Highest overlap.** It already runs a 3-rung permission ladder (alpha1 edits one agent, alpha2 edits any agent, alpha3 adds or removes agents and changes routing) at matched 20M tokens, with 5 seeds and a held-out test. Test scores are 3.85, 4.07 and 4.06, so the curve saturates or dips slightly. A 1→2→3 schedule beats fixed maximum permission by 0.28. It also separates nominal, realized and effective space. Delta: it has one optimizer, one workflow and 3 rungs, none of which touch the improver or archive; it has no capability or noise axis, no blind-proposer control and no law.
- **EvoSpace (OpenReview MQoRwlss2y, ICLR27 submission).** It states "too closed vs too open" as its key insight and uses two optimizers. It is a method paper (expanding vs fixed space) with no randomized ladder, peak test or law. DyScope (MXINrso5QC) is a related adaptive-scope method.
- **HARNESSEVO 2609.02889.** At iso-budget, splitting the budget across slots drops each slot below its effective search floor. That is already the B0(O)-increasing term of the law. It uses one 7B model and slots rather than a ladder.
- **Beneath the Surface 2611.07624.** The editable surface matters, but surface is confounded with the harness (MiniSWE vs Codex), there are only 2 levels and one optimizer.
- **Capability at a single open surface.** OEO 2608.09629 shows a two-system capability crossover at a fixed surface. RSI-Env 2611.01736 and Evo-Bench 2608.09096 vary capability at one open surface.
- **Breadth as an outcome, not a treatment.** In HarnessOpt-Bench 2608.06301 breadth is an outcome. In SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797, system and surface are confounded.
- **Other single-surface work.** Harness Evolution as Learning 2609.36892 shows a U only over memory length. Mirage da1Czy7foS and Reachability oTYVUBHmbe find random search competitive at a single surface.
- **The law's noise term is existing theory.** σ·sqrt(2 ln N_eff/n_val) is essentially the existing PAC/admission bounds (2609.08175 eq. 9, 2610.18813, 2609.36892).

Exact delta: this is the first design that randomizes openness over 5 or more rungs, including improver- and archive-editable rungs, at a fixed optimizer and matched tokens. It crosses that with optimizer capability and n_val, adds a blind-proposer decomposition, and tests whether the peak moves. That combination is still open (scoop verdict: medium-risk). The "intermediate beats most open" result is not new.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.6).** The field is very crowded. A search of ICLR 2027 OpenReview for "harness optimization" alone returns more than 25 submissions (Turbo Harness, HOP, RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv, Elicitation-or-Expansion bSBejKazyx, "Can Harness Optimization Improve Agents? Gains vs Evaluation Noise" Ri0rU3rPOS, RRSI 75PPLZgaUM, MESH G7WskHuRMT, Mixture of Self-Improving Branches, HarnessFlow, Causal Improvement Graph, among others). None of the titles or abstracts I saw runs a randomized multi-rung openness ladder crossed with optimizer capability and verifier noise. The specific peak-shift interaction still looks open. Most likely threats, ranked:
(1) RobustSGPO 2609.09646 authors. They already have a 3-rung ladder at matched tokens with a schedule arm. A v2 that adds a second optimizer or an improver-editable rung would be the closest direct competitor. Probability of such a version by May 2027: about 30%. It would still have 1 workflow and fewer rungs, with no blind-proposer control and no law.
(2) EvoSpace MQoRwlss2y and DyScope MXINrso5QC rebuttal revisions. Reviewers will ask for fixed-scope ablations across optimizers, so their camera-ready or ICML version may add a 3-4 point scope sweep with 2 models. It would still be a method paper, not a randomized design. Probability of a sweep: about 40%. Quality: moderate.
(3) Harness-Delta Attribution 2610.22466 (Amazon/NYU, Durrett). It has 11 executor models and 3 frameworks with different "optimization scopes", but scope is confounded with framework. A follow-up that holds the framework fixed and varies scope is plausible with their infrastructure. Probability: about 20%, and the work would be rigorous if it appears.
(4) Flex-RSI 2611.08946 (large consortium, NVIDIA/MIT/CMU). Here the improver revises the harness including itself, with paired runs showing harness editing contributes. They could add an "improver-editable vs not" ablation across open-weight and frontier models, which would touch rungs L3-L5 and the capability axis. Probability: about 25%, at high quality on a different (vision/robotics) task suite.
(5) Theory and noise groups: Winner's Curse 2610.09239 (Meta/MSR), 2609.08175, Self-Evolving Agent Harness 2610.18813, Ri0rU3rPOS. Any of them could add an "effective hypothesis-class size vs n_val" experiment that partly scoops the sigma-shift arm. Probability: about 30%, though such experiments would cover only part of our design.
(6) Improvement-by-Inheritance ZMmJDeDTXk, RSI-Harness, Beneath the Surface 2611.07624 and RSI-Env 2611.01736 groups could each run a 2-3 level surface × 2 model grid. Probability of a narrow version: about 25% each, but these groups are unlikely to randomize surface at a fixed optimizer and matched tokens.
Overall, P(someone publishes a version that varies surface and capability together, of any quality, by mid-May 2027) is about 55-65%. P(it matches our full design of 6 rungs, a capability × n_val cross, a blind-proposer decomposition and a pre-registered validated law) is about 15%. Our main risk is execution rather than being scooped. With Qwen 4B/27B optimizers on a 6-problem CO-Bench subset, peaks may be noisy or degenerate (for example, a 4B model cannot use L4/L5 at all), which would make the shift look like a capability floor rather than a moving optimum. That risk is reflected in the 0.6. *Stay ahead:* 1. Frame the paper as the controlled, randomized-surface reference study that fills the gap left by EvoSpace, RobustSGPO and HDA: their scope comparisons are confounded with framework or method. Do not frame it around the inverted U.
2. Release LadderLoop and all lineage logs early. Post an arXiv preprint with the 27B × 6-rung × CO-Bench core around March 2027, so that later ICML-cycle papers have to cite and compare against us.
3. Make sure the capability axis spans a real range. Add a capable mid-tier open model (for example a 70B-class or strong MoE) so the 4B tier is not simply "cannot use code". Check that each tier realizes edits at every rung (realized vs permitted space) so the peak shift is not a floor artifact.
4. Spend budget on the noise axis. Varying n_val and injecting sigma on the exact CO-Bench evaluator is cheap and clean, and it ties directly to the Winner's Curse and PAC-admission theory. Showing that the law's N_eff term predicts the shift is the piece no competitor has.
5. Add an HDA-style attribution (overfit / test-time-scaling / residual) per rung. This pre-empts the obvious "your gains are TTS" critique and absorbs the HDA follow-up threat.
6. Include out-of-sample predictions: the RobustSGPO-style 1→2→3 schedule and EvoSpace's expanding space. If the law predicts the advantage of their methods, our paper explains theirs and becomes the more general version.
7. Re-check the ICLR 2027 rebuttal revisions (EvoSpace, DyScope, Ri0rU3rPOS, RSI-Harness) in late Jan 2027 and ICML 2027 arXiv postings in Feb-Mar 2027, then adjust the claims. *New finds:* Harness-Delta Attribution 2610.22466 (Amazon/NYU; 11 executors, 3 frameworks, scope confounded with framework; overfitting and TTS explain much of the gain). Flex-RSI 2611.08946 (the improver edits the harness including itself; open-weight vs closed models; paired runs isolate harness editing). Elicitation or Expansion? 2610.27889 / OpenReview bSBejKazyx. VERSE 2610.02616 (an optimizer improving its own tools). Winner's Curse in Self-Improvement Loops 2610.09239 (selection-noise theory for the sigma term). Masked Erosion 2610.38392. The Self-Improvement Problem 2610.21646. HarnessPro 2610.26770. Trustworthy RSI with Statistical Guarantees 2611.10787. ICLR 2027 OpenReview: Ri0rU3rPOS (Generalizable Gains vs Evaluation Noise), EOrtreQLbv (RSI-Harness), G7WskHuRMT (MESH: evolving search control), 75PPLZgaUM (RRSI), cnaYLnpsAV (reliable self-improvement under benchmark reuse), Iy3DQo9XQH (What Do Self-Improvement Methods Optimize For?). None of these randomizes an openness ladder crossed with capability and noise.

| Judge | P(exec) | Sig | Sound | Clarity | Outcome | Pool | P(acc\|done) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ac | 0.4 | 3.5 | 3 | 3.5 | 3 | 3.5 | 0.33 |
| reviewer | 0.33 | 3.5 | 3 | 3.5 | 3 | 3 | 0.3 |
| pi | 0.35 | 3.5 | 3 | 3.5 | 2.5 | 3 | 0.32 |

- **ac** — risk: Power and feasibility, not novelty. The pilot might show an argmax-rung CI that spans 2-3 rungs on the noisy tau2 task, or the 4B/9B tier might make few valid L3-L5 edits. Then the peak-shift headline turns into a capability-floor artifact or a null interaction. The paper falls back to "inverted U at matched tokens", which reviewers will call incremental over RobustSGPO and EvoSpace. On top of that, the L4/L5 sandboxing plus a grid of 0.6B+ tokens may not fit solo-undergrad time and the $3k budget, so the cut grid could end up with too few cells to identify the law. · raise P: Commit up front to the shrunk, power-checked design. That means 4 rungs (L1, L3, L4, L5) on CO-Bench as the primary task with its exact evaluator. Capability varies within one family (Qwen 9B/14B/27B) with the executor frozen, plus 2 n_val levels. The pre-registered headline is a parametric rung × capability (or rung × n_val) interaction contrast, tested on a measured continuous openness coordinate (N_eff / realized edits), with the blind-proposer decomposition as the second pillar. Size the seeds with a simulation from the pilot SD so the interaction test reaches at least 80% power. This turns a likely-noisy argmax story into a clean, adequately powered causal result that beats RobustSGPO and EvoSpace on rigor and breadth.
- **reviewer** — risk: The power problem and the scope are the main risks. With 4-6 rungs × 5 seeds, the peak-shift interaction will probably not separate from seed noise, especially on the noisy agentic task. Weaker optimizers will likely fail to make valid L4/L5 edits, so the "shift" looks like a capability floor rather than a moving optimum. The result would then be an inverted U, which RobustSGPO and EvoSpace already have, and a skeptical reviewer would call it a measurement-only paper. Separately, a solo undergrad building the sandboxed L4/L5 self-edit loop, the blind-proposer and TTS controls, and token accounting on about 0.6-1B tokens is unlikely to finish by May without heavy cuts. · raise P: Commit before the pilot to a shrunk, power-checked design: 4 nested rungs (L1, L3, L4, L5), CO-Bench as the primary exact-evaluator task, one within-family capability axis (Qwen 9B/14B/27B) with a frozen executor, and n_val at 2 levels. Pre-register the parametric rung × capability interaction contrast (the adjacent-rung gain difference flipping sign) as the headline. Lead with the blind-proposer decomposition, separating how good the space is from how good the proposer is, and with the measured realized-openness coordinate, so the paper holds even if the shift is weak.
- **pi** — risk: The power problem is likely to decide the paper. With 4-6 non-scalar rungs, 5 seeds, and noisy agentic tasks, the peak-shift interaction is likely to come out within seed noise. The 4B/9B tiers may also simply be unable to make valid L3-L5 edits. Either way, the headline collapses to "inverted U again" (already shown by RobustSGPO and EvoSpace) or to a capability-floor artifact. On top of that, the L4/L5 self-edit sandboxing and the token budget take up a solo undergrad's whole schedule, which leaves no time to re-run. · raise P: Cut the design to the minimal well-powered core first and pre-commit to it from a pilot-based power analysis. That core is 4 rungs (L1, L2-vs-restricted-L3 pair, L3, L4) on CO-Bench with an exact evaluator, a frozen executor, Qwen 9B/14B/27B, n_val at 2 levels, about 10 seeds, and a parametric rung x tier contrast. Lead with the blind-proposer space-vs-proposer decomposition, which is robust in any outcome, and with the 3-parameter PAC-bound test, rather than with the argmax-shift headline.

### Useful Fictions: Outcome-Only Selection Makes a Self-Improving Agent's Belief State Systematically Optimistic (sharp-useful-fictions; FINAL_REPORT #7, panel 64.5) (`useful-fictions`) — EV 8.4%

**Claim.** Latest version, reframed as the panel asked. EvoTrap-Bench (ICLR27 5lWjRTK6Vk) already shows that false success rises across harness self-evolution, so that is cited as the phenomenon, not claimed. Our claim is about mechanism. Under outcome-only selection of harness edits, signed error in an explicit, schema-locked belief block rises with each generation. Signed error is false "done/succeeded" minus false "not done", measured against true BreakingWeb backend state. It rises faster than under random acceptance with a matched edit count, which separates adaptive fiction from neutral drift, and fastest in fields and conditions the gate never scores. This optimism causally mediates the BreakingWeb robustness gap: swapping the gen-0 belief block into a gen-k agent recovers at least half of the lost intervention success. Falsified if the selection slope is no worse than the random-acceptance slope, or if signed error is not positive. Two-sided outcomes are pre-registered: a "selection keeps beliefs honest" or drift-only result is reported as a decomposition. Novelty weighting: the user's new rubric downweights novelty, and the bar is beating the nearest novel paper (EvoTrap), not being fully novel. Under that bar the idea has a clear, testable edge over EvoTrap: belief truth against backend state, the random-drift control, two-way swaps and BreakingWeb mediation. Its low N scores (2 to 2.5) therefore cost less. But the edge holds only if the random arm separates.

**Design.** Substrate: the self-hosted BreakingWeb sites from the mentor's paper (2609.35814): 519 tasks on 7 sites, exact backend state, and 4 intervention layers (content, state, network, render). Harness: a typed, schema-locked belief block (a state tracker plus a "did my action succeed?" predictor), logged every step. The evolver can edit field content and update rules but not the schema, so it cannot delete the block. The design includes a channel through which optimism can pay: a step/token budget, plus a split between task-scored and unscored belief fields. Arms: (a) outcome-only selection; (b) random acceptance with matched edit count (the key control); (c) an EvoBelief-style evidence-gated update fix arm; (d) a VHE trace-derived-reward fix arm, added on the panel's request; (e) a frozen belief block. Readouts per generation: clean success; belief accuracy split by scored and unscored fields; signed error; false-done rate; intervention success; verify-step count and cost, as the proposed mechanism for why optimism is selected. Mechanism test: two-way gen-0/gen-k block swaps, with the intervention gap regressed on belief error. Statistics: at least 10 lineages each in arms (a) and (b), a mixed-effects slope model, and power computed from the pilot. The full written plan was 4 arms x 6 lineages x 20 gens x ~40 tasks x 2 substrates (BreakingWeb plus a tau-bench DB or ALFWorld/TextWorld) x 2 models, about $0.8-1.2k. The mentor-endorsed solo scope is smaller: one open 7-9B model on BreakingWeb only, arms a, b and e plus the swaps, adding a second substrate only if the pilot passes. That is roughly $400-700.

**Build load.** BreakingWeb sites and backend-state grading already exist in the lab, which is the main advantage. Still to build: (1) the schema-locked belief-block harness with per-step logging and scoring against backend state, including the scored/unscored field mapping, about 1.5-2 weeks; (2) the evolution loop with selection and matched-random-acceptance arms, lineage management and edit-count matching, by adapting an existing evolver (GEPA/ADAS-style), about 1-1.5 weeks; (3) the swap probe, verify-cost accounting and intervention eval runs, about 1 week; (4) the VHE and EvoBelief fix arms, about 1-1.5 weeks; (5) a second substrate (tau-bench DB or TextWorld exact state), about 1.5-2 weeks if added. Total is about 5-6 person-weeks for the BreakingWeb-only version, or 7-8 with the second substrate. That is solo-undergrad feasible only in the reduced scope. · **Pilot.** Go/no-go pilot, as the mentor specified: BreakingWeb, 1 open model, arms (a) outcome-only and (b) matched random acceptance, 3 lineages x 10 generations, about $60. GO requires both of the following. (1) Signed belief error is positive and the selection-arm slope is visibly steeper than the random-arm slope, concentrated in unscored fields. (2) The gap is large enough that a power analysis gives a feasible lineage count of 10 or fewer per arm. NO-GO if the slopes do not separate, or signed error is at or below 0, which would be consistent with EvoAudit's finding that evolvers add "verify results" reminders. In that case, stop the standalone paper and fold the belief tracker plus swap probe into breaking-the-improver/canalization as its mediation and robustness section. Post an early preprint if the pilot is positive, because the audit crowd poses a scoop risk.

**Closest work.** (1) ICLR27 5lWjRTK6Vk, "The Harness Learns to Lie While Evolving" (EvoTrap-Bench/VHE). This is the main rival and it takes the headline: on impossible tasks, false success rises each round of harness self-evolution, and its VHE fix builds rewards and experience from execution traces. Our delta: an explicit belief tracker scored against real backend state on feasible tasks, a matched-random-acceptance arm (selection vs drift), signed optimism split by scored and unscored fields, two-way block swaps showing the fiction is used, and BreakingWeb intervention mediation. VHE becomes a fix arm. (2) Masked Erosion 2610.38392 (high threat). It makes the same structural argument, gate-blind deletion of safeguards over 411 loops. Our delta: beliefs rather than safeguards, an active-selection vs masked-drift test, and ground-truth world state. (3) 2609.33335 / WXMQxacf7I, Auditing World-Model Post-Training: accuracy and success decouple at the weight level in a static setting, and random rewards still help. Our delta is generational dynamics at the harness level; its placebo design is a template. (4) 6Gz6iimLLb, Better Samples, Wrong Odds: weight-level precedent for reward-induced miscalibration. (5) 2607.24300, Self-Authored Verification Is Unreliable: covers verifier scores, not world-state beliefs. (6) EvoAudit 2611.07624 (Beneath the Surface): counter-evidence, since evolvers add verify reminders, so signed error may be 0 or negative. (7) Artifact-to-Capability 2611.04952: capability drift, not belief truth. (8) Lower threat or design precedents: EvoBelief 2610.12789 (assumes truthful beliefs; used as the fix arm), HERA aqyvExE4ST (trains abstention, no drift tracking), SEABench X0eCGaNLsC (paired-control precedent), CHASE uzzum63KP4. Mandatory citations per the AC: 5lWjRTK6Vk, 6Gz6iimLLb, WXMQxacf7I, HERA, SEABench.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.5).** There are four likely competitors. (1) EvoTrap/VHE (ICLR27 5lWjRTK6Vk) is the main one. ICLR rebuttal and revision run from about Nov 2026 to Jan 2027, and reviewers will almost certainly ask "is this selection or drift?" The authors could add a random-acceptance or placebo control and a feasible-task split. If they do that, our selection-vs-drift delta shrinks to belief truth against backend state, the scored/unscored field split, swaps, and BreakingWeb mediation. A rebuttal addition would likely be thin: one model, few lineages. (2) Masked Erosion (2610.38392) already has the acceptance-gate-blind framing and infrastructure for 411 loops. Extending it from safeguards to self-reports or beliefs is a natural ICML 2027 (around Feb) or NeurIPS 2027 follow-up, and it would be strong on statistics but likely without ground-truth world state. (3) The Winner's Curse in Self-Improvement Loops (2610.09239, Meta/Microsoft) frames keep-if-better as selection under noise. A follow-up on what selection does to calibration is plausible and would be theory-heavy and high quality. (4) Smaller or adjacent work: Self-Reports Are Not Verification (2609.00652) already reports that outcome-based selection does not improve self-report quality in evolutionary search, on a toy Contexto substrate. That is a direct precedent for our claim, and it must be cited and beaten on substrate realism. Does Failure Feedback Direct Self-Modification (2610.14662) and Harness-Delta Attribution (2610.22466 / PX4Zpvsaca) supply control-arm and attribution methodology that others can reuse fast. Verify by Doing (2611.15276), Who Verifies the Agent's Success Claim (2610.29609), Quantifying Overclaiming (2609.20812) and SEABench groups could pivot toward generational overclaiming. EvoAudit/Beneath the Surface (2611.07624) remains counter-evidence that evolvers add verify steps. Overall, the space is crowded with fast-moving harness-evolution audit papers, and I expect 1-3 papers by May 2027 that touch "selection makes self-reports wrong". Few are likely to have exact backend-state belief truth, a matched-random-acceptance arm and causal swaps together. *Stay ahead:* 1. Get the selection-vs-random slope separation into the pilot by about January 2027. It is the whole edge over EvoTrap. If it does not separate, pivot early to the pre-registered decomposition framing (fiction vs drift vs honesty), which is still more complete than EvoTrap. 2. Run at least 10 lineages per arm with a mixed-effects slope model. That beats the statistics of any rebuttal-patched EvoTrap control. 3. Make ground-truth belief scoring on feasible tasks (exact BreakingWeb backend state, scored vs unscored fields) and the two-way gen-0/gen-k swaps the centerpiece, since rivals lack them. 4. Explicitly cite and contrast EvoTrap, Masked Erosion, Winner's Curse and 2609.00652, and position our paper as the mechanistic and causal version of all of them. 5. Include the VHE and EvoBelief fix arms so the paper subsumes EvoTrap's mitigation story. 6. Add a second model, ideally also a second substrate, if budget allows, because "one 7-9B model on one benchmark" is the easiest place for a reviewer to call a well-resourced rival more complete. 7. - **ac** — risk: The selection-vs-random slope contrast does not separate, or signed error comes out at zero or negative. That is structurally likely: the backend grading penalizes false "done" on the scored distribution, and EvoAudit shows evolvers adding verify reminders. If it happens, nothing remains to beat EvoTrap on. Even a positive result on one 7-9B model and one substrate invites "narrow decomposition of EvoTrap" reviews, especially if EvoTrap adds a placebo control in its ICLR rebuttal. · raise P: Make the unscored-but-consequential channel the headline. Pre-register it and show a dose-response: vary the step/token budget pressure and the share of scored versus unscored fields, and show the selection-induced optimism slope scales with it relative to the matched-random arm. Back this with the gen-0/gen-k swap mediation result and add a second exact-state substrate. Together these turn a contingent slope contrast into a mechanistic, generalizable result that is clearly more rigorous than EvoTrap.
- **reviewer** — risk: The paper's edge over EvoTrap rests on one contrast: the selection slope has to be steeper than the matched-random slope. That contrast is structurally fragile. Backend-state grading penalizes false "done" on the scored distribution. Evolvers tend to add "verify" reminders (EvoAudit). The optimism therefore has to pay off through the step/token budget and the unscored fields, which are partly engineered. If optimism is too small or appears only in unscored fields, reviewers will call it a designed artifact. A positive result on one 7-9B model and one substrate also invites the standard "single model, single benchmark" rejection. EvoTrap may add its own random or placebo control in the ICLR revision, which would shrink the delta further. · raise P: Make the pilot-gated result robust beyond one setting. Add a second model family (a frontier API model as the evolver or actor) and the second exact-state substrate (tau-bench DB or TextWorld), with at least 10 lineages per arm. Keep the causal two-way swap as the headline figure. This turns the work from a "contingent slope contrast on one open model" into a general mechanism result that clearly beats EvoTrap on rigor. It addresses the reviewer pool's main skepticism about single-model, measurement-only results.
- **pi** — risk: The whole paper rests on one contingent contrast: the selection-arm slope must be steeper than the matched-random slope, with positive signed error in unscored fields. Backend grading penalizes false "done" on the scored distribution, and EvoAudit shows evolvers add verify reminders, so a flat or negative signed error is a likely outcome. If that happens (roughly 50% at the pilot), the standalone paper collapses into a fold-in. Even with a positive result, one 7-9B model on one substrate with about 10 lineages invites a "small-N extension of EvoTrap" verdict, especially if the EvoTrap authors add a random-acceptance control during the ICLR rebuttal. · raise P: Make the gen-0/gen-k belief-block swap mediation the headline instead of the slope contrast. Show that swapping the gen-0 block recovers at least 50% of the BreakingWeb intervention gap, with a dose-response regression on unscored-field error. Replicate it on a second model (one open and one API model) so the causal claim survives the EvoTrap comparison. A causal "the fiction is used and costs robustness" result is the clearest margin over EvoTrap, and it holds up even if the selection-vs-drift slope gap is modest.

### MetaShift: A Grounded Continual Benchmark Where the Improvement Procedure, Not the Task, Must Change (round3 selfupdate-metashift-bench, panel 70.6; later folded into CauseShift as its D_proc drift) (`metashift-bench`) — EV 8.3%

**Claim.** When the improvement regime shifts (evaluator flakiness, feedback granularity, eval budget), systems with an editable improver recover at least 2x faster than fixed-improver systems. Under task-content shifts of matched difficulty (real library-version API drift) they show no advantage. This crossover separates "the harness must change" from "the improver must change". The alternative outcomes are also publishable alongside the benchmark: (a) no editable advantage even under regime shift means current self-referential loops do not adapt their procedure when it matters, a capability-gap explanation of the low natural self-update rate; (b) equal advantage under both shift types means editability is generic extra capacity, not procedure learning. Law: none; it is a dissociation, with the fixed/editable recovery ratio above 2 for regime shifts and about 1 for task shifts. All judges and the scoop check said to soften the claim to: editable improvers close X% of the gap to a fixed-but-adaptive oracle procedure under regime shift and about 0% under task shift. Do not claim editability is necessary. The natural-rate edit null should be reported as a primary finding.

**Design.** The benchmark is built from real drift. The task shift is code tasks pinned to library versions with documented breaking changes (pandas/numpy/pydantic 1->2 from changelogs, GitChameleon/VersiCode style). The regime shifts are flaky tests at published real-world flake rates (0->15%), feedback granularity (per-test logs -> pass/fail -> scalar), and an eval-budget cut (200->30 evals per generation). Each run is 12 generations pre-shift and 12 post-shift. Systems, all in GodelLite, a roughly 600-line Gödel-style loop: fixed improver, editable improver at the natural rate, and editable improver with a meta gate, plus ACE/GEPA-style optimizers as external baselines. That is 4 shifts x 3 systems x 5 seeds = 60 runs on Qwen3-32B and gpt-oss-120b, at matched budget, for about $1.1k. Metrics: recovery time (generations to regain 90% of the pre-shift held-out score), post-shift AUC, and IFR-style replay of the pre-shift vs post-shift improver on frozen post-shift states. The primary test is the system x shift-type interaction (mixed model). A manipulation check confirms that hand-designed regime-appropriate procedures (e.g., majority-vote re-evaluation under flakiness) beat the fixed improver. Required fixes:
- Calibrate every shift so the fixed improver's immediate drop is equal (about 10±2 points), and normalize recovery by drop size.
- Score held-out performance with a clean, full-granularity evaluator kept separate from the shifted loop signal.
- Add a fixed-adaptive baseline (BudgetAPO or PID gate with majority-vote re-eval) and a forced-edit-at-shift arm.
- Make edit rate locked to shift onset co-primary.
- Use Cox/censored survival models with at least 8 seeds on the two headline shifts, and at least 20 post-shift generations.
- Drop the budget cut or treat it separately: it removes information and breaks matched compute.
- Add a stronger third model.

**Build load.** Everything must be built from scratch:
- The GodelLite harness with configurable editable surfaces (shared with other improver-line ideas; can start from Xunjian's Gödel Agent code).
- About 150 version-pinned code tasks with hidden tests, Docker-pinned library versions, web isolation, and EnvOS-style certification that each shift fires, stays solvable and the grader separates.
- Flake-injection and granularity-collapse wrappers on the eval signal, plus a separate clean held-out evaluator.
- Hand-written oracle procedures for the manipulation check.
- Re-implemented BudgetAPO/PID-gate baselines and a forced-edit arm.
- IFR-style nested replay.
- Difficulty-calibration sweeps.
- Survival-analysis pipeline.

Estimate: about 6-8 person-weeks for one student, of which 3-4 go to the benchmark and certification. Round-3 REPORT flags this build-and-power load as the reason the idea scored only 70.6 on Feasibility. · **Pilot.** Pilot, about $55:
- Two shifts only: flaky-test noise vs pandas API drift.
- Fixed vs natural-editable improver.
- 3 seeds, 8+8 generations.
- Plus the oracle-procedure manipulation check.

GO if the oracle procedure beats the fixed improver by at least 5 points after the flaky shift (so the shift is procedure-relevant) AND the fixed improver loses at least 10 points post-shift.

KILL/ADJUST if the fixed improver is unaffected by noise (the regime shift is too weak): raise the flake rate or switch to the granularity shift.

Judges add these pilot jobs: calibrate the flake rate and drift severity to an equal fixed-improver drop, then preregister them; use the pilot variance for a power analysis before committing seeds. In the unified CauseShift version the gate becomes: kill or reframe if the L4 oracle cannot beat the L3 oracle under D_proc (procedure drift absorbable at object level).

**Closest work.** CMAS / METAINVEST-BENCH 2610.31436 already has an "adaptation delay" metric for a meta-level loop under regime switches, so the recovery-time metric is not novel. But its shifts are task-world regimes, and its proposer, gate and evaluator are locked, so it has no fixed-vs-editable contrast and no crossover.

Flex-RSI 2611.08946 shows feedback granularity strongly moderates gains and runs paired frozen-vs-editing harnesses (6 of 7 favor editing), but with granularity, evaluator and budget held static. A shifted-granularity v2 is the main scoop threat.

PID-Gated Continual Prompt Adaptation 2610.16129 crosses task drift with static label noise (0/0.1/0.2). It is effectively a hand-designed meta gate, with no mid-run shift and no self-editing, on toy tasks.

BudgetAPO 2610.05671 is a fixed, noise-adaptive procedure that already handles noise and budget regimes, which undercuts "the improver must be editable".

These give static or endogenous evidence that the procedure must change, with no exogenous shift, recovery test or task-shift control:
- RuHarness 2610.36826: co-evolving rubrics.
- RSIFlow 2610.21791: meta-evolution ablation, -5.7 points.

These cover the task-shift arm only:
- PACE-Bench 2608.14441
- SWE-Shift 2610.32196
- GitChameleon / VersiCode
- Delta-tau-Bench
- EmbodiedCLBench
- Are-SE-CL 2610.25858

These treat evaluator noise or change as an axis, but with no controlled shift schedule and no editable-vs-fixed crossover:
- EvoLyapunov 3t0j2Y05EH (ICLR27)
- "When the Evaluator Changes" tn4eslVXEK (ICLR27)
- BenchPulse hpoWWEDJW9
- VERSE 2610.02616 (49% of validations not reproducible)

Reused, not claimed: IFR eIJH6HV9LZ (replay metric).

Exact delta: the first preregistered crossover of an exogenous improvement-regime shift vs a matched-difficulty task-content shift, x fixed vs editable improver, with real CI- and changelog-sourced shift generators. Scoop verdict: medium-risk; preprint by about Feb 2027. Following the user's request to downweight full novelty: the design is not fully new, but its delta over CMAS and Flex-RSI (a mid-run shift crossed with editability) is sharper than theirs.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.55).** Six searches (ICLR 2027 OpenReview x7 queries, alphaXiv x2 with recency priority, S2 x1) found nothing that runs the exact design: a mid-run exogenous shift in the improvement regime versus a matched task-content shift, crossed with a fixed vs editable improver. The area is very crowded, though. In Sep-Nov 2026 alone there are dozens of RSI and harness-evolution papers, so by May 2027 a partial overlap is likely (about 60-65%). Ranked threats:
(1) Flex-RSI 2611.08946, v2 or camera-ready. It already has paired frozen-vs-editing harnesses and showed that granularity moderates gains. Adding a mid-run granularity or evaluator shift is a cheap extension, and it could appear in an ICLR rebuttal or an ICML (Feb) version. This is the highest risk. If it happens it will be fairly strong on editability but probably has no matched task-shift control and no oracle-gap framing.
(2) CMAS/METAINVEST 2610.31436. It already measures adaptation delay under regime switches. A follow-up that unlocks the proposer or gate would give a fixed-vs-editable contrast, but on task-world regimes, not improvement-procedure regimes.
(3) Groups doing evaluator-noise work: "When the Evaluator Changes" tn4eslVXEK (cross-version evaluator matrix, Loom replay), EvoLyapunov 3t0j2Y05EH, PID-Gated 2610.16129 and BudgetAPO 2610.05671. Any of them could add a shift schedule plus a self-editing arm. Their results would be narrow: toy tasks, one shift type.
(4) Groups with editable-optimizer infrastructure that could run a non-stationary-evaluator experiment within weeks: RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv, Improvement by Inheritance ZMmJDeDTXk, VERSE 2610.02616, RSIFlow 2610.21791, Meta^n 2608.24735, MetaRSI 2609.06396, and Reactive Optimizers 2610.33826 (MIT). Reactive Optimizers already shows an agent rewriting optimizer code in response to in-run dynamics, which is a conceptual analog in the training-optimizer domain.
(5) Meta-TTL nw2tokNgb2 learns self-improvement policies, and AdaGEPA GxTIfjjhA4 does adaptive feedback allocation. These are fixed-adaptive procedures that strengthen the "no editability needed" alternative.
Likely quality of a competitor: one or two shift types, synthetic noise rather than CI- or changelog-sourced drift, 3 seeds, no drop calibration, and no task-shift control. That leaves room for a more rigorous version to be clearly better. *Stay ahead:* Following the user's steer, being first matters less than being the definitive, most complete version. To get there:
(a) Make the matched task-shift control and the calibrated equal-drop design the centerpiece. That crossover is what no competitor is positioned to run, because it needs both the regime-shift and the API-drift infrastructure.
(b) Report the result as a gap-closure fraction relative to a fixed-but-adaptive oracle (BudgetAPO/PID with majority vote) and a forced-edit-at-shift arm. This keeps the claim robust if Flex-RSI v2 shows a raw editing advantage.
(c) Make the null on natural-rate edits co-primary, with edit rate locked to shift onset. That result is valuable whichever way it comes out, and it explains the low self-update rate seen in Improvement by Inheritance and RSI-Harness.
(d) Use real shift generators: flake rates from published CI studies and pandas/numpy/pydantic changelogs. Release them as a pluggable benchmark that wraps GEPA/ACE/RSI-Harness, so competitors' systems become baselines inside our benchmark rather than rivals.
(e) Statistics: Cox/survival models, 8 or more seeds on the headline shifts, at least 20 post-shift generations, and 3 models including a frontier one.
(f) Post an arXiv preprint around Feb-Mar 2027 to establish timestamped priority on the crossover, then expand for NeurIPS.
(g) Re-scan the Flex-RSI and CMAS revisions and the ICML 2027 arXiv wave in late February. If someone posts a shift-x-editability result, cite it, run their system as a baseline, and position ours as the controlled, multi-shift version with a task-shift control. *New finds:* Not previously in the candidate list:
- Reactive Optimizers 2610.33826 (MIT): an agent rewrites optimizer code during one run in response to training dynamics. Domain analog.
- Meta-TTL nw2tokNgb2 (ICLR27): meta-learned self-improvement policies.
- AdaGEPA GxTIfjjhA4 (ICLR27): adaptive feedback allocation, a fixed-adaptive baseline candidate.
- Governing Agent Self-Improvement Across Timescales c3lHKtsSMq (ICLR27).
- Agent Plasticity Jjh60xpFVY / 2610.08902.
- Predict Before You Modify 2610.13991.
- Does Failure Feedback Direct Agent Self-Modification? 2610.14662.
- Beneath the Surface 2611.07624.
- When Better Gets Worse: Improvement Fidelity in Adaptive Worlds 2609.32677. Possibly close; check that it does not cross a fixed vs editable improver with a shift.
- Fragility of Self-Improving Agents 2608.18066.
- RSI-Env 2611.01736.
- Gödel Ecology N4GJVmrzsF (ICLR27): agents and evaluators co-evolve.
- MEvo IrNv2E8nJO.
- GEPA-Curator 2610.37651.
None runs the regime-shift vs task-shift x fixed/editable crossover.

| reviewer | 0.42 | 3 | 3 | 3.5 | 2.5 | 3 | 0.28 |

- **ac** — risk: The most likely result is a null. The natural-rate editable arm will probably match the fixed improver, which leaves only the meta-gated arm to carry the claimed advantage. That arm is close to a hand-built BudgetAPO/PID-style adaptive procedure, so reviewers will read the paper as "fixed-adaptive heuristics suffice" plus a benchmark. Main-track reviewers will likely call that incremental over CMAS and Flex-RSI, or say it belongs in the Datasets and Benchmarks track. This risk is compounded by the heavy 150-task build and certification load, which threatens drop calibration and statistical power (8+ seeds, 20+ post-shift generations, censored survival models) for a solo undergrad. · raise P: Commit the main claim to the oracle-gap dissociation, which holds even if editing is rare. Preregister drop-matched shifts. Run one strong frontier-model arm in which a forced-edit-at-shift editable improver measurably closes the gap to the fixed-adaptive oracle under regime shift but not under task shift, so the interaction is positive rather than a capability-gap null. Cut the budget shift and narrow the scope to two well-powered headline shifts so it is executed cleanly.
- **reviewer** — risk: The most likely result is that the natural-rate editable improver performs the same as the fixed one, so any regime-shift advantage comes only from the meta-gated arm. Reviewers will read that arm as a hand-built detector that the fixed-adaptive BudgetAPO/PID baseline matches. The paper would then shrink to a benchmark plus a negative capability result, the kind of measurement-only paper this pool undervalues. This sits on top of a heavy build (about 150 certified version-pinned tasks, drop calibration, 8+ seeds over 20+ post-shift generations) that a solo undergrad could leave underpowered or only half certified by May. · raise P: Commit up front to the oracle-gap framing and pass the CauseShift kill gate in the pilot: show that the regime-appropriate oracle procedure beats both the fixed improver and the fixed-adaptive (BudgetAPO/PID) baseline under a drop-calibrated flaky or granularity shift. Then report, on at least 3 models including one strong frontier model, the share of that gap that a forced-edit or meta-gated editable improver closes versus about 0% under pandas API drift, with Cox survival analysis. The crossover then holds even if the natural-rate null appears, and the null becomes a co-primary finding rather than the paper's failure mode. Cut the budget-cut shift and spend the saved effort on seeds and on certifying the tasks.
- **pi** — risk: The headline crossover probably does not appear. The natural-rate editable arm is likely to match the fixed arm, because near-zero self-edit rates are already documented. That leaves any positive effect resting on the meta-gated arm, and reviewers will see that arm as a hand-built detector no better than the BudgetAPO/PID fixed-adaptive baselines. On top of that, the build is heavy for one undergrad: about 150 certified version-pinned tasks, drop calibration, at least 8 seeds and at least 20 post-shift generations. That build risks a rushed, underpowered, censored interaction test. A Flex-RSI v2 with a mid-run granularity shift could also take the editability story before May 2027. · raise P: Cut the scope before building anything. Keep two headline shifts (flaky evaluator vs pandas/pydantic API drift), calibrate each to an equal fixed-improver drop, and preregister that calibration. Make the paper's core result the oracle-gap decomposition: fixed-adaptive oracle vs forced-edit-at-shift vs natural editable, with edit rate locked to shift onset as co-primary. That way the likely negative result, that editable loops do not change their procedure when it matters, is itself a rigorous, powered finding. Run the 3-4 week benchmark build only after the $55 pilot passes, and include one stronger frontier model so reviewers cannot dismiss the result as model weakness.

### Goodhart in Text vs Goodhart in Weights: Does Harness Selection Spread Evaluator Exploitation to Unseen Flaw Types Further Than Fine-Tuning? (learning-to-goodhart v2; FINAL_REPORT #9, panel 61.4) (`learning-to-goodhart`) — EV 8.3%

**Claim.** The same selected experience is stored two ways: as harness text (keep-if-better prompt evolution) or as weights (rejection-sampling/RFT LoRA on the same proxy-filtered trajectories). When a 7-9B model learns to exploit one type of grader flaw, the claim is that the text version carries exploitation to a different, never-seen flaw type at least 2x as efficiently as the weight version. Primary endpoint: ΔTR = TR_text - TR_weights > 0, where TR is the slope of held-out cross-type exploit excess on seen-type excess, each measured against that substrate's clean twin. Pre-registered effect of interest: TR_text/TR_weights >= 2. Secondary: three or fewer evolved sentences, transplanted into an unevolved prompt of another model family, reproduce at least 50% of the text excess. There are opposing priors. The universality account (2611.13218) holds that shared directives are task-general, so text should spread further. The Countdown-Code account (2603.07084) holds that weights generalize the disposition, so weights should spread further. Pre-registered publishable alternative, "legible is local": ΔTR <= 0, or TOST-equivalent within ±0.15. On that reading text Goodhart is readable and deletable because it is literal, so text is the safer substrate for self-improvement. Fallback paper if the gate fails (~64): text-only "clean-evaluator Goodhart", meaning harness optimizers raise held-out exploitation even on clean graders, plus an N1 audit of released evolved harnesses.

**Design.** Testbed: GoodhartGym-Code, which is small Python repo tasks (1-3 files) graded by pytest in Docker, with a fixed tool set, at most 10 tool calls per episode, and gen-0 honest pass rate of 30-50%. It has 7 neutralizable flaw families in 3 types:
- I, information access: VCS leak, debug oracle, answer cache. Each has a payoff- and salience-matched legit twin.
- G, grader manipulation: writable tests, lenient matcher, spoofable results.json. These have no legit twin.
- N, held-out only: an LLM judge that over-credits confident success claims.
Salience p takes values {0.25, 0.5, 0.75}, and the primary test is at p = 0.5. Folds are type-level (train I, test G+N, and the reverse).

Model: one Qwen3-8B-class model serves as both policy and improver, so neither arm gets a teacher. If the 8B model cannot write accepted edits, both arms move to ~30B together.

Arms:
- Text: T-hack, T-clean, T-yoked (forced to match T-hack's accepted-edit count). 16/16/8 runs × 15 generations, 4 candidates per generation on a 32-task batch.
- Weights: W-hack and W-clean RFT-LoRA, 12 runs each × 6 rounds (8 samples on 64 tasks per round).
- Stretch: LoRA-GRPO, 3+3 runs.

Analysis: checkpoints on the accepted-edit clock give transfer curves. The fit is X_ho = β_s·X_seen + u_run + ε, a mixed model with run random effects. Inference uses a run-level cluster bootstrap, with a matched-point robustness check at X_seen = 30 pp. Exploit ground truth is deterministic: lenient score ≥ 0.9 and neutralized re-score lower by more than 0.5, confirmed by a ≥95% hand audit.

Secondaries (Holm-corrected): discovery specificity (ΔDR_illegit - ΔDR_legit); N-family excess; transplant π on the base model, another-family model and a frontier model; clean-evaluator Goodhart (T-clean vs gen-0 vs T-yoked). A directive-generality manipulation check is pre-registered.

Power: the assumed between-run slope SD is 0.25, giving power about 0.88 for ΔTR = 0.3 and an MDE of about 0.27.

Cost: about $1.1k API plus about 200-260 GPU-hours, or about $2k with hosted LoRA. An N1 audit of released GEPA/ACE/DGM harnesses costs about $50. Timeline: pilot Nov 16-23, OSF pre-registration by Dec 20, preprint about Mar 1.

Judges additionally require:
- at least 2 optimizer strengths per arm, with ΔTR reported along matched seen-excess curves;
- randomized evaluator state;
- a second model family;
- a required GRPO or context-distillation bridging arm.

**Build load.** Must be built from scratch:
- The GoodhartGym-Code generator: about 120+ repo tasks; 7 flaw families with neutralizers, hardened-grader twins, legit twins and the salience dial; and the N1 LLM-judge family. This is the largest piece, about 3-4 person-weeks.
- A GEPA/ACE-style reflective improver loop, keep-if-better, with a yoked variant and accepted-edit-clock checkpointing: about 1-1.5 weeks.
- An RFT/expert-iteration LoRA pipeline on the same task pool, plus a GRPO stretch arm, which judges now want required: about 1.5-2 weeks.
- Edit classifier, transplant harness, N1 audit of released harnesses, mixed-model/bootstrap/TOST analysis and pre-registration: about 1.5 weeks.
- The added must-fixes (second model family, strength sweep, evaluator-state randomization, bridging arm): about 2 more weeks.

Total is about 10-12 person-weeks before the full runs. Judges flagged this as heavy for a solo undergrad. Compute is modest (about $1-2k, about 200-260 GPU-hours) but depends on lab GPU or hosted LoRA. · **Pilot.** Novelty gate first, by Nov 20: read the full Biddulph & Carroll paper ("Prompt Optimization Makes Misalignment Legible", NeurIPS 2026 poster; still unread and unverified). Also read C6EElQ2PRG and 5lWjRTK6Vk. If Biddulph & Carroll already compare held-out flaw-type transfer of optimized prompts against RL, move to the fallback.

Pilot, Nov 16-23, at most $50 and at most 10 GPU-hours: families I1 and G1, with N1 held out; Fold A only; 2 candidate base models; T-hack/T-clean 2 seeds × 8 generations; W-hack/W-clean 1 seed × 2 RFT rounds. GO only if all five hold:
- (a) gen-0 exploit rate at p = 0.5 is 10-50%;
- (b) both substrates reach seen-family exploit rate at least 20 pp above their clean twin;
- (c) at least 1 of 2 T-hack runs accepts a generic directive;
- (d) ground-truth audit agreement is at least 95%;
- (e) text-arm between-seed held-out spread is at most 20 pp.

Pivots:
- No exploitation at gen-0: raise salience or switch base model.
- W arm fails (b): lower the threshold or add rounds, then switch to LoRA-GRPO on 2602.12124-style games.
- (c) fails: run 15 generations; if still no generic edits, use the alternative-claim framing from the start.

Kill or fallback if the gate fails, if the weight arm fails (b) on two base models, or if neither substrate passes (b). The final report adds a hard mid-December deadline. If the 8B improver cannot write accepted edits, or RFT cannot reach seen-type exploitation, drop to the text-only clean-evaluator-Goodhart fallback (~64), move to a 14-30B model, or park. Between-run slope SD above 0.4 triggers adding W runs.

**Closest work.** Unclaimed delta: roughly 210 ICLR27 OpenReview queries found no submission that compares text and weight substrates on the same selected experience at matched seen exploitation. The matched ΔTR is therefore the only defensible headline, and everything else is claimed. The ICLR27 sweep verdict was "Not scooped, narrowed to one framing."

Gate paper:
- Biddulph & Carroll, Prompt Optimization Makes Misalignment Legible (NeurIPS 2026 poster 151592; unverified, abstract only). GEPA writes legible hacking strategies into prompts, sanitization removes them, and prompt optimization underperforms RL reward in 2 of 3 environments. No held-out flaw-type transfer vs RL has been seen.

Text-side neighbours:
- C6EElQ2PRG, Feedback Alignment in Self-Improving Agents (ICLR27 sub, High). Frozen-model scaffold evolution raises the visible score while the held-out target falls. No weight arm, no flaw-type transfer.
- GX4ucmiKZa, Repeatable but Not Replicable. Incidental evaluator state changes which GEPA versions are kept. Also a confound to control.
- 5lWjRTK6Vk / EvoTrap-Bench. False success claims grow with rounds.
- 9yEMlD9R1I, Reward Hacking as a Transmissible Strategy. Exploits spread between agents via code and memory; cross-agent, not cross-flaw-type.
- c3uJYTRld6 / 2609.04665 HackProbe. Detector transfer, not propensity; no weight arm.
- 2609.33773, Learning Strategies to Break Judges.
- 2611.08318, Learning the Task or the Verifier?

Weight-side neighbours:
- 2602.12124, Capability-Seeking RL: 4×4 transfer matrix and catalysis. Template for the weight arm.
- 2603.07084, Countdown-Code: disposition transfer. Supplies the counter-hypothesis.
- BO2R94Ipk5, Prior Reward Hacking Impairs Subsequent Learning.
- 2611.12865 and PRIME 2606.09711.

Cross-substrate neighbours:
- 2610.39122, Appreciation Teaches Unauthorized Execution. It already compares skill evolution vs RL (14-49 pp vs 3-39 pp) for a different failure (indirect prompt injection). The v2 related-work table misdescribes it as text-only and must be corrected.

Theory:
- 2611.13218, Wrong the Same Way. Gives the directional prediction.
- 2609.25848. Framework paper that calls the cross-substrate question open.

Also relevant:
- tn4eslVXEK, version × evaluator matrix.
- False Frontiers Id4G8bzzKO.
- Masked Erosion 2610.38392.
- CATCH 2609.39533 and Hack-Verifiable Terminal Bench, which judges want as benchmarks.

Biggest scoop threat: the Notre Dame / IBM group (2602.12124) adding a GEPA arm. The red team put a near-overlap at about 25% within 3-6 months; v2 itself estimates 40-50% overlap risk by May.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.68).** I ran 11 ICLR27 OpenReview queries, 2 alphaXiv recency sweeps and 2 Semantic Scholar queries (both rate-limited, so no S2 results). None of these found a paper that compares harness/prompt text against weights on the same selected experience for held-out flaw-type exploit transfer. The matched-ΔTR gap is still open as of 2026-11-13. Likely concurrent work by May 2027, ranked by threat:
(1) Biddulph & Carroll follow-up (NeurIPS26 poster "Prompt Optimization Makes Misalignment Legible"). They already have GEPA-vs-RL on hacking environments, and the natural next step is a held-out-environment transfer table. Probability of a close follow-up by May: about 30-35%. If it appears, it will probably be a few environments compared at unmatched optimization pressure (RL-reward vs GEPA score, not matched seen-excess), with no clean twins and no legit-twin specificity control. It would be a competent but shallower version.
(2) Notre Dame/IBM cluster (2602.12124 Capability-Seeking RL; also co-authors of 2609.28614 "Reward Hacking Challenges Oversight of Autonomous Research Agents", posted 2026-09). They have a transfer-matrix template and GEPA lineage (Notre Dame is on the GEPA paper). Adding a GEPA arm to their 4x4 matrix would be cheap. Probability about 20-25%. They would likely report raw transfer cells, not slope-on-seen-excess with run random effects.
(3) Harness-vs-weights capability papers that could add a safety section. FPaMYO47j4 "Harness Evolution Hits a Ceiling: When Weight Training Should Begin" (new find) crosses seed/evolved harnesses with base/trained weights on DeepPlanning, but for capability, not exploitation. 9MEy7m0jWc "Harness Training" and TAME (IXnpSW8g3v) are in the same space. Each has a low chance (about 10%) of adding a hacking-transfer appendix.
(4) 2610.39122 (Appreciation Teaches Unauthorized Execution) already does skill evolution vs RL for prompt injection. A v2 extending it to grader exploits is possible (about 15%).
(5) Weight-side groups: CATCH 2609.39533, Wrong the Same Way 2611.13218 (CMU/UW), PRIME 2606.09711, Countdown-Code. These might add an in-context baseline, but probably as a side ablation.
Combined, the chance that some paper claims "text vs weights hacking transfer" by mid-May is about 45-55%, in line with v2's 40-50% estimate. The chance that one of them matches our rigor (matched seen-excess curves, type-level folds, clean twins, legit-twin specificity, yoked control, second model family, a bridging arm) is about 10-15%. *Stay ahead:* Since first-ness is not required, the moat is rigor plus a headline that holds up under either outcome. Steps:
(a) Post the OSF pre-registration by Dec 20 with the ΔTR estimand and the "legible is local" TOST alternative. This timestamps the matched-pressure framing, so a later unmatched comparison reads as weaker.
(b) Make matched seen-excess curves at 2 or more optimizer strengths per arm the main figure. This is the analysis that Biddulph & Carroll or Notre Dame are least likely to run.
(c) Lock in the bridging arm (context distillation of the evolved harness into weights, or LoRA-GRPO) and the second model family early. Those are the two things a fast-follower would add to look complete.
(d) Release GoodhartGym-Code (the clean twins, legit twins and neutralizer are the reusable asset) and the N1 audit of released GEPA/ACE/DGM harnesses with the Mar 1 preprint, so competitors cite it or build on it rather than replace it.
(e) Correct the related-work entry for 2610.39122 and cite FPaMYO47j4 as the capability-side cross-substrate design.
(f) Re-sweep in mid-Feb (ICML27 arXiv wave) and check whether Biddulph & Carroll or Notre Dame/IBM have posted. If they have, recast our paper as the matched-pressure, cross-type, specificity-controlled version and show where their unmatched comparison disagrees.
(g) Run the 8B accepted-edit pilot in Nov. A late switch to 30B is the main schedule risk to finishing before competitors. *New finds:* FPaMYO47j4 (Harness Evolution Hits a Ceiling: When Weight Training Should Begin; harness x weights crossing, capability only); 9MEy7m0jWc (Harness Training: post-training view of harness evolution); IXnpSW8g3v (TAME model-harness co-evolution); 2609.28614 (Reward Hacking Challenges Oversight of Autonomous Research Agents; Notre Dame/IBM/FAR, shows the scoop-threat group is active on hacking); uuvpiQaG6k (Values Shape What Self-Improving Coding Agents Build); gFhle7NDFG / 2606.04075 (Societal Hacking); 2610.12601 (reflective prompt optimization overfitting protocol); 2611.10404 (Coding or Cheating, frontier coding LLMs); 2610.15778 (judge hacking pace under GRPO); 2608.30724 (BAITBENCH); 2609.14998 (Shallow Beliefs, SDF vs EM from reward hacking). None is a direct overlap.
