- **ac** — risk: The main risk is that optimizer strength is confounded with substrate. Even with 2 strengths per arm and matched seen-excess curves, reviewers can say that ΔTR reflects how hard RFT-LoRA was pushed compared with keep-if-better prompt evolution, rather than text versus weights. Execution compounds this. Building GoodhartGym-Code, both arms, a bridge arm and a second model family takes about 10-12 person-weeks solo before the full runs. The 8B improver may write no accepted edits, and RFT may never reach the seen-type excess, so the paper could collapse to the text-only fallback. · raise P: Make the context-distillation bridge (T-hack distilled into LoRA weights) the core of the design, not just one more arm. It holds the selected content fixed and changes only the substrate, which answers the optimizer-strength confound directly. Then cut scope, for example to 4-5 flaw families and leave GRPO as a stretch, so that the two-family matched-curve result is clean and well-powered by March.
- **reviewer** — risk: The core problem is optimizer strength versus substrate. Reviewers can blame any sign of ΔTR on how hard prompt evolution was pushed compared with RFT-LoRA (rank, rounds, generations). The strength sweep and bridging arm are meant to fix this, but they add to a build of about 10-12 person-weeks for a solo undergrad. If the 8B improver writes no accepted edits, or RFT never reaches seen-type exploitation, the headline result disappears. What remains is a measurement-only fallback with a single model family, which this pool undervalues. · raise P: Make the context-distillation bridge (T-hack distilled into LoRA weights) the centrepiece of the design, and do not build the full strength sweep. The bridge holds the selected experience and the optimization pressure fixed and changes only where the experience is stored. That directly answers the strength-confound critique all three judges raised. Run it on two model families with GoodhartGym-Code cut to 4 families. This lowers the build load and makes the matched ΔTR claim hard to dismiss.
- **pi** — risk: The scope is too big for one person. That means 10-12 person-weeks of infrastructure (the GoodhartGym-Code generator with 7 neutralized flaw families, an evolution loop, RFT plus a required GRPO or distillation bridge, a second model family, and a strength sweep) before any full run. On top of that, the 8B model may never write accepted edits, or RFT may never reach seen-type exploitation. Either failure collapses the matched-ΔTR design by the mid-December gate. Even if everything is built, reviewers can still blame any sign of ΔTR on optimizer strength rather than substrate. With matched seen-excess curves, 2 strengths per arm is a thin defense. · raise P: Cut the build to the minimum and make the bridge the centrepiece. Run context distillation, meaning T-hack harness trajectories distilled into a LoRA, as the matched-experience weight arm alongside RFT. Then report ΔTR along matched seen-excess curves at 2-3 strengths per arm. Use 4 flaw families rather than 7, in two model families (Qwen plus Llama or Gemma). This turns the optimizer-strength confound into a dose-response result reviewers can believe, and it is finishable solo by March.

### Which Meta-Reward Breeds Improvers That Are Better and Domain-General? An Order-1 Bake-off with a Forced-Edit Placebo and Meta-Overfitting Decay as the Held-out-Domain Endpoint (merged MR-A/MR-E/MR-C/GAP-1 x Meta-Overfitting; working title "Order-0 Rewards Breed Routers, Not Improvers") (`metareward-x-metaoverfitting`) — EV 7.5%

**Claim.** Pre-registered and two-sided. Loops that select improver edits on order-0 signals (child score, matched-budget Δ0 in the RSI-Harness style, descendant/CMP in the Looped-RSI/HGM style) lower the improver's meta-edit rate p and leave held-out successor quality Δ1 unchanged, because selection favours evidence-routing edits. Two kinds of reward raise Δ1, mainly through p: replay-family rewards (hurdle Q1-hat = p̂·μ̂, IFR-style, HMED-style) and order-0 plus a forced-edit quota. The domain-general half is the Meta-Overfitting Decay claim. After a memory wipe and domain scrub, the Δ1 an improver gains at home (A→A) shrinks with pre-registered domain distance and turns negative far away (Δ1(A→far) < 0), even where order-0 transfer looks positive near home. Estimands are ΔΔ1, Δp and Δμ between arms, then Δ1 by distance. Either sign can be published. If order-0 selection already finds order-1 edits that transfer, that directly supports RSI-Harness and Looped-RSI. The plan commits to no p≥0.2 crossover threshold, and the cost model is presented as a derived hypothesis.

**Design.** Testbed: the IbI harness (a Hyperagents-style operator file). The task agent evolves on child dev score, and the improver is promoted by the arm's meta-reward. Main backbone is Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B (local vLLM or DeepInfra). One second family (DeepSeek-V3.1 or Llama-3.3-70B) is used for the key contrast only. Arms, each 5 seeds x 40 generations at matched dollars (modifier plus evaluation tokens):
- A1 child score;
- A2 Δ0 with a no-improvement penalty;
- A3 descendant/CMP;
- A4 hurdle Q1-hat on a training meta-state pool;
- A5 IFR-style (current gain plus a historical floor);
- A6 random promotion (negative control);
- A7 A1 plus a forced diagnose quota every k=5 generations, with a placebo-render twin. This guarantees enough accepted self-edits, and the inert fraction must be reported.
If budget binds, the cap is 4 core arms (A1, A4, A6, A7) x 2 domains (near, far).

Endpoint: full nested replay at generations 0/20/40 on 48 held-out meta-states from at least 3 fault families, disjoint from the A4/A5 selection pool and with independent seeds, at R=6. A rebuilt 40-state harder successor bank (M1 near 0.5) removes IbI's 0.84 ceiling. The Decay endpoint scores memory-wiped, scrubbed improvers on near and far (non-coding) domains and reports Δ1(A→B) vs Δ1(A→A) by distance. A labelled held-out-family Q^(0) oracle ceiling and a non-replay product-gain-after-k-generations endpoint are added.

Readouts: p, μ and Δ0 per arm; corr(Δ0, Δp) with disattenuation; blind two-coder diff taxonomy (routing / budget / reword / structural, κ); the proposed → retained → inert funnel. Power: TOST at ±0.05 is not achievable, so the MDE is pre-registered from pilot variance (target 0.06–0.08 on ΔΔ1). The model is mixed (seed, meta-state, replicate) with common random numbers, and p is the best-powered primary outcome. An optional stretch is a 1.7B/4B GRPO arm (MR-E: Δ0 reward vs nested reward). Cost is about $1.4–2.2k with 25% contingency before the domain endpoint, roughly $2–3k merged, against a $3k cap. Cut in this order: generations to 30, then A3/A5, then the second backbone, and never meta-states or R. Timeline: OSF pre-registration by about Dec 20, preprint late Feb, NeurIPS mid-May.

**Build load.** Built from scratch, about 10–14 person-weeks solo (my estimate; the sources give none):
- Port the IbI harness to the open backbone and replicate its positive control.
- A nested-replay scoring pipeline with common random numbers, plus in-loop Q1-hat for A4/A5. In-loop replay per candidate per generation is the main cost and engineering risk.
- 48 new held-out meta-states over 3+ fault families, plus a 40-state harder successor bank.
- Selection code for 6–7 meta-reward arms, including faithful IFR/HMED-style reimplementations.
- Quota and placebo-render machinery.
- Memory-wipe, domain-scrub and leak-audit tooling, plus far-domain meta-states and successor banks with a distance measure. This is new, because IbI tests order 1 on coding only.
- A diff-coding protocol.
The optional GRPO arm needs about 200 GPU-hours. Judges said MR-E's own estimate was 5–20x low. The lab has no confirmed GPU node. · **Pilot.** Pilot runs 2 weeks for about $80 and replaces the Week-2 Meta-Overfitting edit-rate pilot. Work:
- 100 modifier runs to measure base p and μ for Qwen3-Coder-30B on the IbI banks.
- Replicate the IbI positive control (+0.171): 7 meta-states x 6.
- A1 vs A4, 2 seeds x 15 generations.
- Price one nested replay end to end.

GO if all of these hold:
- base p ≥ 0.05, or the diagnose dose moves p across about 0.05–0.5;
- the positive control gives Δ1 > 0 with a CI excluding 0;
- split-half reliability of Q^(1) ≥ 0.6;
- per-candidate Q1-hat costs ≤ 10¢.
For the Decay half, forced or quota edits must produce a home gain over gen-0 above 3 pp, which catches retained-but-inert edits. The fallback is k* ≈ 6.

KILL or PIVOT if the positive control cannot be detected on two open backbones. The pivot is a measurement-only paper (GAP-4 order-2 audit, or GAP-1 routing-suppresses-meta-editing), or a move to MiMo or another cheap API backbone.

Slot decision: this paper competes with round-3's CauseShift/openness paper for the improver-line slot.
- CauseShift's reach pilot passes and the IbI control fails: take CauseShift.
- Both pass: put the choice to Xunjian. This paper is cheaper and more rigorous; CauseShift has the bigger story.
- Taught, Not Selected stays the safer lead on novelty.

**Closest work.** - **IbI ZMmJDeDTXk.** Only measures Q^(1), and every loop there selects on child score. It recommends selecting improvers "by the improvers they produce" but never builds such a selector, and its order-1 test covers coding meta-states only. Its 6-meta-state null has no equivalence margin.
- **IFR eIJH6HV9LZ and HMED AnN0NpAVWL.** Each is a single replay-on-frozen-states meta-selector. IFR already names "meta-forgetting". Neither runs a head-to-head comparison, a p/μ split, a nested-replay endpoint or a domain-distance test. Both are included as arms.
- **RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv, MetaEvoForge 0b4wysHrzh, FreeEvolve 8UyLPIcKqX, Looped-RSI sBH9cdajAw, Meta-TTL nw2tokNgb2.** One reward each, evaluated at product level. They claim positive cross-domain improver transfer (RSI-Harness up to 4.5x), with no memory wipe, no scrub and no graded distance. This is opposite-sign evidence for Decay.
- **RepRSI zxN7uhwRY0.** A 2-arm meta-reward comparison in a weight-curriculum setting.
- **LSE 2603.18620, MetaEvolve 2607.21971, LM2LM 2610.23344.** RL on order-0 improver reward; this is the object of the stretch arm.
- **aEPJT4hW84 (Learning to Repair the Learner), Godel Ecology N4GJVmrzsF, LRSI 71wzcahKcb, VERSE 9WwpxPfcji, RSI-Opt 2610.32026.** Lineage and adjacent work; RSI-Opt shows product-level transfer peaking at gen 1.
- **RSIBench Q9AHmuAk4Y and rM2eQio3e3.** Harness- and skill-level overfitting, a different object.

Exact delta: the first controlled comparison of meta-rewards scored on held-out, family-disjoint nested-replay Q^(1). It decomposes the gain into p and μ, adds a causal forced-edit placebo, and extends the endpoint to Δ1 by domain distance after memory wipe and scrub. This turns the positive-transfer wave into a bounded test: near/memory-carried vs far.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.6).** No ICLR 2027 submission or arXiv preprint I found runs a head-to-head of meta-rewards scored on held-out nested-replay Q^(1). Nested replay still turns up only in IbI ZMmJDeDTXk. The risk is the crowd around it. RSI evaluation papers in this pre-registered, audit-the-claims style are appearing almost weekly. The ones most likely to publish close work by May 2027:
(1) The IbI authors. They already have nested replay and the Hyperagents history, and they recommend selecting improvers "by the improvers they produce". The most natural follow-up for them is to build that selector and compare it with child-score selection, possibly in an ICML 2027 submission around February or in an ICLR rebuttal revision. I put this at about 35-45%. It would probably be narrower than ours: coding only, 2-3 arms, no forced-edit placebo, no domain-distance test. It would still take the headline "order-0 vs replay selection".
(2) OnzWTehIaN, "Why Is RSI Hard to Verify?". It sets out a sufficient criterion for RSI, made of target gains, causal benefit from installed self-produced updates and more competent successor mechanisms, with statistical tests attached. A follow-up that applies these tests to compare selection rules is plausible, but it would be framed around theory and verification.
(3) The RSI-Harness, Looped-RSI and MetaEvoForge groups. In ICLR revisions they may add held-out-domain transfer tables to defend their cross-domain claims (up to 4.5x for RSI-Harness). These would be product-level and would not wipe memory or scrub domains. Even so, a reviewer could say transfer "has been checked".
(4) The IFR (eIJH6HV9LZ) and HMED (AnN0NpAVWL, also 2610.07979) groups. Each could add a baseline comparison against child-score selection, but only for their own selector.
(5) Adjacent measurement papers that weaken the novelty of individual pieces:
- RRSI 75PPLZgaUM: harness RSI overfits; regularized proposal and selection.
- ModularRSI pP6df7PZI7: generalizable harness RSI.
- Winner's Curse 2610.09239: pre-registered study of selection noise and acceptance rules. It found no acceptance rule beats greedy acceptance, which is close to our order-0 claim, but at order 0.
- Third Eye 2611.12988: continuation utility goes negative after a further update.
- Beneath the Surface 2611.07624: transfer of evolved agents keeps gains only selectively; the top-scoring agent never gives the largest mean improvement.
- Auditing the Product 2610.27610: only 22% of RSI studies audit reruns of the full loop.
- SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797: a G0-and-up generalization ladder for RSI.
- Does Failure Feedback Direct Self-Modification? 2610.14662.
Each covers one ingredient at order 0 or at harness level, not the improver's Q^(1) by meta-reward. Taken together they mean reviewers will see "meta-overfitting" and "selection rules don't matter" as partly known. Our paper then has to win on being the controlled order-1 comparison, with the p/μ split, the forced-edit placebo and Δ1 by domain distance. With the user's rubric, where being the better version counts, I put the chance that ours is clearly the more complete and rigorous one at about 0.6. The main downside is IbI's own group doing a quick selector comparison with its stronger infrastructure and lineage credibility, or our budget forcing cuts down to 4 arms x 2 domains, which would look thin next to a well-run 3-arm IbI follow-up. *Stay ahead:* - Get the preprint out early, by mid-to-late February, before ICML-cycle versions appear. Include at least the core contrast (A1 vs A4 vs A6 vs A7) on the nested-replay endpoint, so priority on the order-1 meta-reward comparison is on record. Put the OSF pre-registration in December as planned, since it is a credible differentiator against post-hoc follow-ups.
- Treat the placebo-controlled forced-edit arm (A7) and the p/μ decomposition as the parts that are hard to copy. Neither IbI nor RSI-Harness can add them cheaply. Never cut A7.
- Protect the Decay endpoint (memory wipe, scrub, graded distance including far non-coding domains). It is the axis no competitor is moving on, and it answers the 4.5x cross-domain claim directly. If budget binds, cut A3/A5 and the second backbone before cutting the far domain.
- Cite and position against the new measurement wave: Winner's Curse 2610.09239, Third Eye 2611.12988, RRSI 75PPLZgaUM, Beneath the Surface 2611.07624, OnzWTehIaN, Auditing the Product 2610.27610. Frame ours as the order-1 analogue that they explicitly lack. Map our estimands onto OnzWTehIaN's sufficient criterion so verification reviewers see it as an application of that test.
- Release the rebuilt 40-state harder successor bank (M1 near 0.5, removing IbI's 0.84 ceiling) and the held-out fault-family meta-states as a public resource, so that even a concurrent IbI follow-up is likely to be evaluated on our bank.
- Watch IbI ZMmJDeDTXk, RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv and Looped-RSI sBH9cdajAw for revisions during the ICLR rebuttal (Nov-Dec). Check arXiv and ICML for "improver selection" titles in February. If IbI publishes a selector comparison, recast ours as the multi-reward, cross-domain, placebo-controlled generalization of it. *New finds:* New close work not in the idea's Closest list. ICLR 2027: OnzWTehIaN (Why Is RSI Hard to Verify? Criterion: target gains + causal benefit of installed updates + more competent successors, with statistical tests), 75PPLZgaUM (RRSI: regularized harness RSI against overfitting outside the training distribution), pP6df7PZI7 (ModularRSI: generalizable harness RSI), g0Iv9XAmuc (EvoPathBench: process-level evaluation of self-evolving agents), qAYeg9I0K3 (Compositional RSI), wV4bK1dHpC (Trustworthy RSI with statistical guarantees), 3t0j2Y05EH (Lyapunov-gated RSI), hRSpuaLKkC (Mixture of Self-Improving Branches). arXiv: 2610.09239 (Winner's Curse in self-improvement loops; pre-registered; acceptance rules do not beat greedy), 2611.12988 (Third Eye: utility after a further update), 2611.07624 (Beneath the Surface: transfer and admission replays of evolved agents), 2610.27610 (Auditing the Product, Not the Recursion: 114-study audit), 2611.02797 (SelfImprove-Bench: generalization ladder), 2610.14662 (Does Failure Feedback Direct Agent Self-Modification?), 2610.13991 (Predict Before You Modify), 2611.08946 (Flex-RSI), 2611.01736 (RSI-Env), 2610.32253 (RSIHub). None of these runs a meta-reward comparison on order-1 successor quality.

- **ac** — risk: The project is overloaded for one undergrad. It merges a 7-arm bake-off with in-loop nested replay and a Decay endpoint that needs far-domain meta-states built from scratch, all on a $3k budget with no confirmed GPUs. The likely outcome is falling back to the 4-arm x 2-domain cut. If that happens and the floor effect also hits (Qwen3-Coder-30B barely self-edits, so p is near 0), most arms tie for an uninteresting reason. The forced-quota arm then carries the paper, and its edits may be retained but inert. Reviewers would read that thin result as a benchmark of IFR/HMED-style selectors on IbI's coding banks, which is weaker than a quick 3-arm follow-up from IbI's own group. · raise P: Split the paper and lead with only the bake-off half: A1, A4, A6 and A7 with its placebo twin, plus A2 if budget allows, on IbI coding, at full power, on two backbone families. Move Decay to a single pre-registered near/far probe, or to follow-up work. Make the headline the p/μ decomposition backed by the causal forced-edit placebo. That claim is clean and mechanistic, and the IbI or IFR groups are unlikely to reproduce it quickly. The aim is one decisive, well-powered result rather than many underpowered cells.
- **reviewer** — risk: Floor and scope risk together. A 30B-A3B improver may almost never meta-edit (IbI saw p≈0, 0/36), so most arms tie for a trivial reason. Meanwhile the merged design (7 arms, in-loop replay, two new meta-state banks, far-domain banks, wipe/scrub tooling) is probably 2x over a solo 6-month budget. That likely forces the 4-arm x 2-domain cut, which a busy reviewer reads as a thin single-backbone benchmark of other people's selectors in a crowded, partly-known area ("selection rules don't matter", "meta-overfitting"). · raise P: Drop the Decay/domain-distance half, or make it a small secondary figure. Spend the freed budget on making the core bake-off undeniable: the forced-edit placebo arm, which gives a causal p→Δ1 result, the second backbone as a full arm, and a non-replay product-level endpoint that answers the circularity critique. The result is one clean claim with one strong headline figure (ΔΔ1 split into p and μ across arms), not two half-powered claims.
- **pi** — risk: Two risks stack up. First, the floor: the Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B improver may almost never edit itself (IbI saw 0/36), so every arm ties for a boring reason. Second, the build is far too big for the time: 10-14 person-weeks or more of solo engineering, including in-loop nested replay, 6-7 arms, new held-out and far-domain banks, and wipe/scrub tooling, all by mid-May. Under those conditions the paper either gets cut to 4 arms x 2 domains, which looks thin next to a fast IbI follow-up, or arrives half-finished with a null nobody can interpret. · raise P: Split the merge. Make the order-1 meta-reward bake-off the whole paper: A1, A4, A6 and A7 with its placebo, two backbones, the full 48-state held-out-family nested-replay endpoint, and the p/μ decomposition. Drop the far-domain Decay half, or keep only one near/far contrast as a secondary result. That gives a solo undergrad a realistic shot at a clean, well-powered, pre-registered result by May, and this paper wins as the better version, not the novel one.

### Open Where the Cause Is: A Reach x Realization x Credit Law for Meta-Harness Openness Explains the Self-Edit Drought and Predicts When Improvers Must Rewrite Themselves Under Real Drift (round3/REPORT.md unified proposal, latest version; benchmark = CauseShift, testbed = DialLoop) (`open-where-the-cause-is`) — EV 6.7%

**Claim.** The held-out gain from opening rung k of a self-improver's editable surface (L0 prompt slots, L1 free prompt, L2 memory/skills, L3 harness code, L4 improver code, L5 gate/evaluator policy) is the product of three factors, each measured separately, minus a selection penalty. The factors are: reach R_k (the share of post-shift failure mass whose fix lives at rung k, from an oracle-edit probe), realization rho_k (the rate of valid rung-k proposals, from proposal-only logs) and credit kappa_k (the chance the gate keeps a beneficial rung-k edit, from planted-edit tests). The law is G(O|d,g) = sum_{k<=O} R_k rho_k kappa_k Delta_drop - lambda*sigma*sqrt(2 ln N_eff(O)/n_val). The optimum openness O* therefore moves to whichever rung real drift puts the cause at: task-content drift gives L3, improvement-procedure drift gives L4, grader drift gives L5. On stationary benchmarks, reach x credit at L4 is about 0. That explains the observed self-edit drought (0% in SelfImprove-Bench, 'almost never' in IbI) without any appeal to unwillingness. Five outcomes are pre-registered and each is publishable: A) the law holds; B) reach without realization ('priors, not gates'); C) realization without credit ('gate, not willingness'); D) openness pays everywhere, which supports RSI-Harness/MetaEvoForge; E) a flat matched-compute null with a decomposition, as a D&B fallback.

**Design.** The testbed is DialLoop, a ~600-line Godel-lite keep-if-better loop with nested rungs L0-L5. The sealed held-out grader, the meta-gate/replay computation and token accounting sit outside every editable surface. Every accept/reject is logged and bypass attempts are flagged. The benchmark is CauseShift: about 150 Python hidden-test tasks pinned to post-cutoff polars/pydantic/httpx in Docker, run as 12 pre-shift + 12 post-shift generations. It has four drifts: D0 stationary; D_task, real changelog API breaks with the cause at L2/L3; D_proc, real flake rates 0 to 15% plus a feedback-granularity collapse, with the cause at L4; and D_eval, 2 real BFCL verifier commits on a ~100-task second substrate, with the cause at L5. A pre-registered cause-location gate requires R_target >= 0.6 and R_other <= 0.3, with the frozen drop calibrated to 10 +/- 2 pp. Block A is O in {L0..L5} x 4 drifts, object gate, 5 seeds (24 cells). Block B is a 2x2 of quota q=0.25 {off,on} x credit {object 1-step gate, meta-gate = replayed child gain on 3 frozen post-shift states}, plus a placebo null-AST-edit twin, run at L4 (D0, D_proc) and L5 (D_eval) with 6 seeds. Block C repeats {L3, L4, L4+meta-gate} x {D0, D_proc} with a second proposer family (gpt-oss-120b or DeepSeek-V3.x) at 4 seeds. That totals about 205 lineages. The proposer is Qwen3.5-27B and the executor is a frozen Qwen3-8B, so only proposer-side openness varies; a cheap API annotator is validated on 200 hand labels. Primary stats are the interaction (L4-L3)x(D_proc-D0) and its L5/D_eval analogue in a mixed model, Cox recovery time plus post-shift AUC, TOST for L4 = L3 under D0, and a law test in which lambda is fit on D0 only and frozen. The law must reach held-out-cell Spearman >= 0.6 and beat a rung-index model on RMSE. The behavioral component codes every proposal by rung and mechanism (kappa on 15%) to give a proposed -> retained -> effective funnel, a shuffled-evidence directedness control, and habit lock-in with a rationale codebook. Cost is about $1.8-2.6k hosted, less with local vLLM. The timeline is a pilot Nov 16-29, the build plus OSF pre-registration by Dec 20, Block A in Jan, Block B with a preprint around Feb 25, Block C in Mar, the law fit in Apr, and writing in May.

**Build load.** This is the heaviest plan on the board, and the build load for a solo undergrad is the report's own top risk. It has to be built from scratch: the DialLoop harness with 6 nested rungs, a sealed grader and replay meta-gate (about 2-3 weeks); about 250 certified CauseShift tasks across two substrates, with Docker pins, changelog-mined breaks, flake/granularity injectors, 2 mined verifier commits, EnvOS-style certification and no-docs memorization probes (about 4-6 weeks); 2-3 hand-written oracle edits per rung x drift, plus planted-edit acceptance tests at 20 injections each (about 2 weeks); and the proposal-coding codebook, annotator validation and second coder (about 1-2 weeks). Running and analysing about 205 lineages takes roughly 8-10 more weeks. The total is about 17-23 person-weeks of building and running. Mitigations: one primary substrate, D_eval with only 2 commits, and Block C can be dropped. · **Pilot.** The pilot runs Nov 16-29 for at most $80. (1) An oracle reach probe on CauseShift-mini (40 polars/pydantic tasks, D_task and D_proc only, one oracle edit per rung). It passes if the target-rung R >= 0.6, other rungs <= 0.3, and the frozen drop is >= 10 pp. If the drop is < 5 pp, raise the flake rate or switch once to the granularity shift. KILL or reframe if the L4 oracle cannot beat the L3 oracle under D_proc: procedure drift would then be absorbable at object level, so pivot to the IBI-merged meta-reward x Meta-Overfitting paper. (2) A natural L4 run, 3 seeds x 8+8 generations, D0 vs D_proc, object gate, measuring proposed and retained L4 share. (3) A planted-edit test of kappa_4 under the object gate vs the meta-gate. The overall GO requires (1) to pass AND either the proposed L4 share to rise under D_proc or kappa_4(meta) - kappa_4(object) >= 0.3. Slot decision: this competes with the IBI-merged paper for the single improver-line slot. Take this paper if separability passes and the IbI positive control fails to replicate on open backbones. If both pass, put the choice to Xunjian: IBI-merged is cheaper and more rigorous, this one has the bigger story. If separability fails, drop this paper. It does not replace Taught, Not Selected (73.1) as lead.

**Closest work.** RobustSGPO 2609.09646 has a 3-rung matched-token ladder, a nominal/realized/effective split, and finds that scheduling beats max permission. The delta: 6 rungs including improver and gate, a drift-moved optimum, and reach and credit measured independently. EvoSpace MQoRwlss2y and DyScope MXINrso5QC state 'too closed vs too open' and propose adaptive scope. The delta: we predict where scope should go from the cause's location. Are-SE-CL 2610.25858 crosses drifting streams with editable surface, with P/S/I/F metrics and a Meta-Harness gate freeze. The delta: real drift, drift type placed by rung, and editable improver and gate arms; its metrics are reused. SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797 and IbI ZMmJDeDTXk report 0% improver edits, a framing lever and nested replay; Table 6 shows 20-45% retained meta edits under explicit framing. The delta: a causal reach x credit account and the funnel to effective edits; IbI's assay is credited. VERSE 2610.02616 / 9WwpxPfcji shows a frozen-vs-self-evolving optimizer x verification crossing. The delta: verification is one way to raise kappa, and reach is added as the competing condition. HGM, IFR eIJH6HV9LZ, RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv and LRSI are used as the kappa manipulation and not claimed. 2610.14662 has an editable-cause share pi_e, which is extended here to a per-rung, oracle-measured, predictive reach. 2611.09701 (restriction gap vs discovery regret) and 2610.09239 / 2609.08175 (winner's-curse/PAC admission) are instantiated and their functional form is not claimed. CMAS 2610.31436 owns the adaptation-delay metric, which is borrowed. BenchPulse hpoWWEDJW9 and tn4eslVXEK cover real verifier churn and evaluator-change false progress; the delta is exogenous grader drift placed at L5 with an editable-gate arm. BudgetAPO and the PID gate serve as fixed-adaptive reference arms at L3. Still open: no paper runs the cause-location crossover, i.e. the drift type that sets which rung pays, crossed with an editable improver and gate. Most component pieces have precedent, so the novelty is the unification and the predictive decomposition, not any single mechanism. That is enough if it beats the round's other novel options.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.55).** The field is very crowded. ICLR 2027 has dozens of submissions on harness RSI and self-evolution, e.g. RSI-Harness EOrtreQLbv, RRSI 75PPLZgaUM, ModularRSI pP6df7PZI7, MEvo IrNv2E8nJO, Lyapunov-gated 3t0j2Y05EH, Gödel Ecology N4GJVmrzsF, MetaEvoForge 0b4wysHrzh. Most of them are method papers that report one optimum on stationary suites, so none run the cause-location crossover. The real threats are fast-followers that already have the right pieces and could add a drift x rung axis by ICML (Feb) or in ICLR rebuttals (Nov-Jan):
(1) RobustSGPO 2609.09646. It already has the matched-token permission ladder and the nominal/realized/effective split. Adding a drift-moved optimum would be the cheapest extension for anyone. This is the most direct overlap risk, with medium-high quality.
(2) Are-SE-CL 2610.25858 and CMAS 2610.31436. Both already cross drifting streams with surfaces. A v2 adding an editable improver or gate is plausible.
(3) The IbI ZMmJDeDTXk / SelfImprove-Bench 2611.02797 groups. They own the self-edit drought finding and a nested-replay assay. Explaining the drought is their natural next paper, and they may frame it as "framing/unwillingness" rather than reach x credit. High infrastructure, strong quality.
(4) Flex-RSI 2611.08946, a large NVIDIA/MIT/CMU consortium. Its improver already edits the executor and itself, with paired with/without-improver runs. A v2 adding drift or a rung ablation is possible and would have broad task coverage, but it is benchmark-style rather than causal.
(5) Gate/credit papers that could claim the kappa story: Masked Erosion 2610.38392, Recoverability & Verifier Strength 2610.27465, Trustworthy RSI w/ statistical guarantees 2611.10787 / wV4bK1dHpC, and Evaluator-state GX4ucmiKZa.
(6) Diagnosis papers adjacent to reach, i.e. where the fix lives: Trace-to-Architecture 2610.16725, Harness-Delta Attribution 2610.22466 (Amazon/NYU) and 2610.14662 (pi_e). They decompose gains by component on stationary tasks only.
My estimate is that 1-3 papers by May 2027 will show "optimal editable scope depends on shift/task" with 2-4 rungs and synthetic or single drift. Fewer than 15% will include an editable gate/evaluator arm plus independently measured reach/realization/credit plus real drift. *Stay ahead:* Per the user's steer, novelty is weighted less than whether this is clearly the better version. The edge comes from completeness and rigor: 6 rungs including L4/L5, three real drifts, an independent R/rho/kappa measurement, and a frozen-lambda out-of-cell prediction test. A paper with a mere crossover claim cannot match that.
(a) Get the Block A pilot and the cause-location gate (R_target>=0.6) done by early Jan. Post a preprint around Feb 25 as planned, and consider an earlier short version once D_proc x {L3,L4} is in, so ICML-cycle versions of RobustSGPO/Are-SE-CL have to cite you.
(b) Run RobustSGPO's ladder and IbI's nested-replay/framing assay as explicit baseline arms inside DialLoop, and show the law subsumes them: framing raises rho but not R x kappa. That converts the closest competitors into special cases.
(c) Add Flex-RSI-style paired improver on/off runs and a T2A/Harness-Delta-style diagnosis baseline as a reach estimator to beat. The oracle-edit probe should predict better than trace diagnosis.
(d) Make the law's predictive test the headline: held-out-cell Spearman, RMSE vs a rung-index model, plus a prospective prediction on a 4th unseen drift. Competitors will offer post-hoc decompositions, not frozen predictions.
(e) Release CauseShift/DialLoop with sealed graders early. Being the standard drift-x-rung benchmark makes later work look derivative even if it was concurrent.
(f) Each week, monitor ICLR 2027 revisions of RobustSGPO, Are-SE-CL, IbI, EvoSpace and DyScope, plus ICML 2027 arXiv drops in Feb.
Main risk to being the better version: execution scope (about 205 lineages, real drifts that may fail the cause-location gate). Keep outcome E (D&B) viable, and cut Block C before cutting L5/D_eval, which is the most distinctive cell. *New finds:* Flex-RSI 2611.08946 (improver edits executor and itself; paired with/without-improver runs; stationary); Masked Erosion 2610.38392 (acceptance-gate credit failures); Recoverability & Verifier Strength 2610.27465; Trace-to-Architecture 2610.16725 (diagnose which harness component to fix: adjacent to reach); Harness-Delta Attribution 2610.22466; Beneath the Surface 2611.07624; RSI-Env 2611.01736; Harness-S 2611.04277 (nonstationary harness utility); Who Verifies the Verifier 2610.11464; Trustworthy RSI w/ guarantees 2611.10787 / wV4bK1dHpC; ICLR 2027: Governing Agent Self-Improvement Across Timescales c3lHKtsSMq, Evaluator State hidden facet GX4ucmiKZa, MetaEvoForge 0b4wysHrzh, RRSI 75PPLZgaUM, ModularRSI pP6df7PZI7, Lyapunov-Gated RSI 3t0j2Y05EH, Gödel Ecology N4GJVmrzsF, Which Self-Improvements Should We Trust cnaYLnpsAV, SelfLineage nVl2qz74V7. None runs the drift-type x rung (incl. editable improver/gate) cause-location crossover.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| reviewer | 0.28 | 3.8 | 2.9 | 2.8 | 3 | 3.2 | 0.33 |

- **ac** — risk: A solo undergrad has to build 17-23 person-weeks of infrastructure (6-rung DialLoop, about 250 certified CauseShift tasks on two substrates, oracle and planted-edit probes, a codebook) and then run about 205 lineages. The likely result is a thinned execution: 5 seeds, rung CIs spanning 2-3 levels, too few L4/L5 events, and a D_eval with too few grader flips. That combination produces reviewer complaints of 'descriptive regression / curve-fit law' and 'kappa is tautological for an object gate'. A second risk compounds it: the pilot may find that procedure drift can be absorbed at L3 (or that the 27B model has memorized the APIs), which collapses the cause-location crossover. · raise P: Cut the scope to one substrate and three drifts (D0, D_task, D_proc), keep L5 only as a small appendix, and spend the saved budget on seeds (8 or more) and on an out-of-sample law test. Fit R, rho and kappa only on probe data, then pre-register predicted O* per held-out cell and show that the law beats a rung-index baseline. One clean, well-powered crossover at L3 vs L4, plus a demonstrably predictive decomposition that explains the drought, is worth more to reviewers than the full 6x4 grid. Novelty can safely be weaker than strict uniqueness as long as the paper is clearly more rigorous and broader than RobustSGPO and Are-SE-CL.
- **reviewer** — risk: The plan needs 17-23 person-weeks of building and about 205 lineages, which is too much for one undergrad. Under that load the likely result is thin cells: few L4 and L5 edit events, 5-seed CIs that span 2-3 rungs, and a censored recovery analysis. Reviewers would then read the 'law' as a descriptive regression with a near-tautological credit term, because an object gate cannot see meta value by construction. There is a second risk at the pilot. If real drift does not cleanly separate the cause by rung, for example if an L3 version check absorbs D_proc, the central crossover disappears. The fallback would then be a measurement-only paper in a crowded pool, next to RobustSGPO, Are-SE-CL and SelfImprove-Bench. · raise P: Cut the scope to one substrate and three rungs that differ in kind (L3 harness, L4 improver, L5 gate). Use one or two drifts, D0 vs D_proc, plus D_eval only if it is cheap. Put the saved compute into 10+ seeds and a second proposer family. Make the headline the preregistered cause-location crossover, backed by independently measured reach and credit and the proposed -> retained -> effective funnel. Present the multiplicative law as a secondary, held-out-validated predictor rather than the main claim. This raises pExecute substantially and gives the robust, multi-model result that skeptical reviewers in the agents pool reward.
- **pi** — risk: The build load is 17-23 person-weeks, which is too much for one undergrad. It is likely to collapse into a thin version: 5-6 seeds per cell, an empty or nominal L4/L5 arm with a 27B proposer, and few D_eval flip events. Cause location may also not separate cleanly in real drift: an L3 'check version' or retry step could absorb D_proc. The law fit then turns into descriptive regression across 24 cells, with rung-argmax CIs spanning 2-3 rungs. Reviewers would read that as overclaimed bundling with no decisive crossover. · raise P: Make the paper one clean pre-registered crossover: L3 vs L4 vs L4+meta-gate under D0 vs D_proc (plus the D_task L3 analogue), on a single substrate with 10+ seeds. Measure R and kappa independently from oracle and planted-edit probes. Demote L5/D_eval, Block C and the full six-rung law fit to the appendix or a short extension. A smaller design keeps the reach x credit story but makes the core interaction well powered, and makes it far more likely to be finished by May. Because the field is crowded, being more rigorous than concurrent work matters more than covering more ground.

### MR-E: Training Improvers for Successors. RL on a nested-replay successor reward vs child gain (Delta0) for a small open improver model (`mr-e-rl-improver`) — EV 6.5%

**Claim.** Keep the base model, environment and data fixed and change only the reward. GRPO-training a small open improver/modifier model on the order-0 objective the field already uses (a faithful LSE/MetaEvolve reimplementation: improvement delta with a -1 penalty for no improvement) raises Delta0. It also lowers the meta-edit rate p and leaves held-out successor quality Delta1 at or below zero, so the order-0 reward trains away order-1 competence and the policy collapses onto evidence-routing or prompt-rewording edits. A nested-replay successor reward does not do this, and it raises held-out-family Delta1. This is the gradient-level causal version of IFR's selection-level "meta-forgetting". If the result goes the other way (Delta0-RL also raises p and Delta1), order-0 RL already captures order-1 skill. Both outcomes are publishable only if positive controls make a null interpretable. Per the judges, the claims should be stated as absolute Delta1 and diff-class shares, not as the original ">=30% cut in p", because base p may be about 0.

**Design.** Policy: a LoRA on Qwen3-8B / Qwen2.5-Coder-7B. The latest plan cuts this to 1.7B/4B, run as a gated stretch arm of the merged "Order-0 Rewards Breed Routers" meta-reward paper. The policy acts as the modifier on IbI-style semantic and interface banks, editing a target improver given meta-evidence. A frozen local Qwen3-8B task agent and unit tests mean almost no API spend. Reward arms: (1) Delta0, a faithful LSE/MetaEvolve reward; (2) nested-replay successor gain (the edited improver is applied to 2 or more inner targets); (3) a hybrid of the nested reward plus a small Delta0 term; (4) the MR-C hurdle reward (p and mu shaped separately), optional; (5) the untrained base. Required additions: a matched-compute Delta0 arm (as many rollouts as the nested arm, so "more environment compute" cannot explain the result), and a positive control (oracle or upweighted-meta reward) showing Delta1 moves on the training families. Data: 120 training meta-states from 6 fault families. Judges ask for a held-out test of about 48 meta-states x R=6 from at least 3 unseen families, with at least 5 seeds per arm (originally 24x6, 2 families, 3 seeds). Analysis: a mixed model with seed, meta-state and replicate random effects and a pre-registered MDE in place of TOST at 0.05. Report p, mu, Delta0 and Delta1, the replicate SNR of the reward, and the fraction of GRPO groups with nonzero variance. A blind diff taxonomy (routing / budget / reword / structural, two coders plus kappa) is tied to LM2LM's overoptimization analysis. Anti-circularity checks: Delta2 / deeper nesting, unseen inner targets, and transfer where the trained policy edits improvers that run on an API model. Cost: the original estimate of ~600 GPU-h + $150 API is judged 2-3x (ac) to 5-20x (mentor) too low; a realistic figure is >=1,500-2,000 GPU-h at 8B. The 1.7B/4B two-arm-plus-hybrid version is the affordable path.

**Build load.** Port the IbI harness and banks to a local vLLM ecology. Build a GRPO environment whose reward runs nested replay (edit, then the edited improver on inner targets, then task-agent rollouts and unit tests), plus cached rollouts or a cheaper proxy inner target with occasional full replay. Reimplement the LSE/MetaEvolve Delta0 reward. Possibly add an SFT warm start on frontier-generated meta-edits, a held-out-family bank, the diff-coding protocol and an API-transfer eval. Estimated 6-9 person-weeks for one student, mostly environment and RL infrastructure, plus heavy GPU time. This is why it was demoted from lead to stretch arm (Mar-Apr slot in the merged-paper timeline). · **Pilot.** Original pilot: 1 week, about $20 API plus about 60 GPU-h, Qwen3-1.7B, Delta0 vs successor reward, 1 seed, 300 GRPO steps on 30 meta-states, evaluated on 8 held-out x 3. GO requires non-degenerate within-group reward variance and a measurable divergence in p between arms. Judges add these checks before any GPU commitment: (a) measure base p, mu and Delta1 at 1.7B/4B/8B on the IbI banks, plus nested-reward replicate SNR and the share of GRPO groups with nonzero variance. If p < ~0.1 or SNR < ~1, change the environment first (SFT warm start, format bonus, curriculum of meta-states where meta-edits help, denser inner eval). (b) Time one full nested-replay rollout and rebuild the GPU budget from it. (c) The merged paper's calibration gate: replicate IbI's +0.171 positive control on the open backbone. If it cannot be detected, all nulls are uninterpretable. The pilot steps are judged optimistic: 300 steps at 1.7B in one week is unlikely given nested rollout cost.

**Closest work.** IbI (Improvement by Inheritance): only measures Q^(1) with prompted frontier improvers and never trains one; MR-E turns its measurement into a reward. LSE (ICLR27 RTN1lPAkIm / 2603.18620): RL-trains a 4B improver on downstream gain, i.e. exactly the Delta0 arm; it never rewards successor quality or measures p. MetaEvolve (2607.21971): GRPO with Score(P2)-Score(P1), -1 if no gain, so order-0 again. LM2LM (2610.23344): DAPO on a 27B proposer with a world-model reward and an overoptimization analysis; reviewers will cite it on reward hacking. Evo2 (2610.13025), Evolving-RL (d7bZVSos27), ACE-RL (2D1JCgZ0py): weight-trained proposer/reflector roles on order-0 signal. IFR (eIJH6HV9LZ): names "meta-forgetting" and replays frozen historical states, but at selection level with frozen weights; it pre-empts the framing. HMED (AnN0NpAVWL / 2610.07979): a nested-replay-like counterfactual for meta-revisions, distilled into text, not weights. RSI-Harness / Looped-RSI / MetaEvoForge / Meta-TTL / LRSI / Skill-infinity: descendant-style successor selection on frozen weights, with no gradient training and no order-0 vs order-1 contrast. Exact delta: the first gradient-level, same-base reward swap (order-0 vs nested-replay order-1) that reports p, mu, Delta0 and Delta1. Novelty is only in that core; "RL-training a small open improver" is already shown. Scoop check (20+ searches of ICLR27 OpenReview, alphaXiv, S2): medium risk. The IbI authors adding a GRPO arm is the obvious fast-follow.

**Competition forecast (P(clearly better at submission) = 0.55).** I ran 8 searches: 6 term searches on ICLR 2027 OpenReview and 2 recency-ranked alphaXiv searches. No search turned up a gradient-level, same-base reward swap of order-0 against nested/order-1 reward. Every new RSI paper keeps the weights frozen and works by selection: LRSI 71wzcahKcb, SO-RSI SpGgovoVDV, RRSI 75PPLZgaUM, MetaEvoForge 0b4wysHrzh, Meta-TTL nw2tokNgb2, Flex-RSI 2611.08946 and Trustworthy-RSI 2611.10787. LRSI measures how much an edit contributes in sampled future versions, which is the closest selection-level analogue of a successor reward. Likely competitors by May 2027, most to least likely: (1) The IbI authors (ZMmJDeDTXk) adding a GRPO/RL arm that trains an improver on Q^(1), either in their ICLR rebuttal/camera-ready or as an ICML 2027 follow-up (about 30-35%). They have the banks and the metric, but their work would probably be one model, few seeds and no matched-compute or positive controls. (2) LSE (Mila/Snowflake) or MetaEvolve releasing a v2 that adds a multi-step/descendant reward (about 20%). They have strong RL infrastructure but would frame it as better performance, not as the order-0 vs order-1 causal contrast, and would not report p or Delta1. (3) LM2LM (PSU/Amazon, DAPO on a 27B model) extending its overoptimization analysis to second-order rewards (about 15%). This would be the highest-quality threat at large scale, but it is unlikely to isolate meta-edits. (4) The LRSI or IFR/HMED groups distilling their lookahead or counterfactual signal into weights (about 15%). (5) RSI-Env (2611.01736, Fudan/HKUST), an open-ended environment where agents improve their own models and harnesses. It could become the default testbed others use for weight-level order-1 RL by ICML (about 15-20%). I put the chance that at least one close weight-trained order-1 paper appears by mid-May at about 50%. If one does, it will most likely be narrower than ours: one scale, no meta-edit rate or diff taxonomy, no held-out families, no positive control. Our main risk is execution, not being scooped. At 1.7B/4B the base p may sit near 0, GRPO groups may have zero variance, and the result could be an uninterpretable null. A larger lab could then publish a cleaner 8B+ result that reads as the definitive version. *Stay ahead:* On your point about novelty, I agree: what has to hold is that our paper is clearly the better version at submission. Strict first-ness matters less, and I gave it less weight in the estimate. To be the more complete version: (a) Fix what the judges flagged early. Use at least 3 unseen held-out families and at least 5 seeds, and include the matched-compute Delta0 arm and an oracle/upweighted-meta positive control, so even a null result is interpretable. A competitor's quick GRPO arm will almost certainly lack these. (b) State the results as absolute Delta1 and diff-class shares, and report the fraction of GRPO groups with nonzero variance and the replicate SNR of the reward. This makes the paper the reference for whether an order-1 signal can be learned from at all. (c) Position against LRSI, IFR and HMED as the gradient-level causal version of their selection-level lookahead/meta-forgetting. Cite IbI's Q^(1) as the measurement we turn into a reward. (d) Run the cheap 1.7B arm by about February so the core contrast is in hand when ICML 2027 submissions appear. Then add one 4B or 8B replication, plus the transfer test where the trained policy edits improvers running on an API model, so results at two scales outweigh a single-scale competitor. (e) Consider releasing the nested-replay reward and environment, possibly on RSI-Env or IbI banks, so fast-followers build on our setup. (f) In February-March, re-check IbI's OpenReview revisions, the LSE/MetaEvolve arXiv versions and ICML 2027 arXiv postings. If someone else posts the core contrast, shift the emphasis to the diff taxonomy, the held-out-family Delta1, deeper nesting (Delta2) and the controls. *New finds:* ICLR27: 71wzcahKcb (LRSI, lookahead-guided RSI, frozen weights; closest selection-level analogue), SpGgovoVDV (SO-RSI, second-order problem solving), 75PPLZgaUM (RRSI), 0b4wysHrzh (MetaEvoForge, already known), nw2tokNgb2 (Meta-TTL, already known), qAYeg9I0K3 (Compositional RSI), zxN7uhwRY0 (RepRSI), 6a1CEKvNeC (Recursive harness self-improvement for reasoning data synthesis), qgf2YVxbNC (LLM-as-an-Improver). arXiv: 2611.01736 (RSI-Env, possible testbed for competitors), 2611.08946 (Flex-RSI benchmark), 2610.21040 (Recursive Reward Improvement via adversarial meta-prompt evolution), 2610.32026 (RSI-Opt), 2609.33867 (R^2 Flow), 2611.10787 (Trustworthy RSI with statistical guarantees), 2610.27610 (Auditing the Product, Not the Recursion; supports the order-0 vs order-1 framing). None does gradient-level order-1 reward training.

- **ac** — risk: The floor effect. At 1.7B/4B the base meta-edit rate p and Delta1 are probably close to 0, so GRPO groups get zero variance from the noisy nested-replay reward. Neither arm then moves Delta1, which leaves an uninterpretable null or a tautological "trained on successor gain, scored on successor gain" result. On top of that, the nested-rollout compute (realistically more than 1.5k GPU-h) stops a solo undergrad from reaching 5 seeds x 3+ held-out families by May. Reviewers who know LSE, MetaEvolve, LM2LM and IFR will then call the work an incremental reward swap with weak evidence. · raise P: Run the base-p/SNR and timed-rollout pilot first. Then make the environment non-degenerate with an SFT warm start on frontier meta-edits and a curriculum of meta-states where meta-edits demonstrably help, and confirm the positive control (IbI +0.171 replicated on the open backbone). Only after that, commit to a lean 2-arm-plus-hybrid design (Delta0 matched-compute vs nested vs hybrid). Run it with at least 5 seeds on at least 3 held-out families and report a pre-registered MDE. That way the headline is a powered, interpretable result: either Delta0 suppresses p and Delta1, or it doesn't.
- **reviewer** — risk: The floor effect. At 1.7B/4B the base meta-edit rate p and Delta1 are probably near zero, so GRPO groups get zero reward variance. Both arms would then fail to move Delta1, which gives an uninterpretable null. A busy reviewer would read that as a single-small-model measurement paper that has no positive control and does not transfer to frontier improvers. On top of this, the nested-replay environment needs 6-9 weeks of infrastructure work and 1,500+ GPU-h, which makes a clean, well-powered run (5 seeds, 3 or more held-out families) unlikely by May. · raise P: Before any RL, run a gate that does two things: replicate IbI's +0.171 positive control, and show that p and the nested-reward SNR are non-degenerate on the open backbone. If they are degenerate, add an SFT warm start or a curriculum until they are not. Then cut the scope to two arms plus a matched-compute Delta0 arm at 4B, with at least 5 seeds and at least 3 held-out families. That turns the likely floor null into an interpretable causal contrast backed by a strong headline figure (Delta0 rising while p and Delta1 fall over training), and it keeps the infrastructure feasible.
- **pi** — risk: A floor effect at 1.7B/4B. Base meta-edit rate p and Delta1 are probably near 0, so GRPO groups get zero reward variance and the nested reward's SNR is too low to learn from. Nested-replay rollouts are expensive, so a solo undergrad who also owns the merged paper's main arms will likely run out of GPU time and calendar time. The likely outcome is then an underpowered null that cannot be interpreted, with few seeds and no working positive control, and a larger lab (IbI or LSE follow-ups) could publish the clean 8B version first. · raise P: Before any RL spend, build the environment for detectability. Use an SFT warm start or a curriculum of meta-states where meta-edits demonstrably help, then prove the positive control (an oracle or upweighted-meta reward moves Delta1, and IbI's +0.171 replicates on the open backbone) together with nonzero group variance. After that, cut the study to Delta0 vs nested vs hybrid plus a matched-compute Delta0 arm, with at least 5 seeds and at least 3 held-out families. A clean two-sided causal result that does not sit at the floor is what reviewers would accept.
